# S6 staging API re-pin packet

Packet ID: **S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1**. Status: **NOT RUN; NO EXECUTION GO**. Revision 7, draft 11 (implements Root ruling R7 items 2 to 4, Root ruling R8 item 3 and the packet text list of Root ruling R9 item 13, with the pin that Root set on 28 October 2026, the U-4 result and the gate L decision of Root ruling G3; Root records it in the tool pins and the acceptance record, never in this file.

## Revision 7 changes

Basis: revision 6 (sha256 `fcf4e5b9`) and its changed-lines audit (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 0 P2, 5 P3); Root ruling R7 (items 2 to 4); Root ruling R8 (item 3); the packet text list of Root ruling R9 (item 13, with the items it names) after the review of the A2, A2M, RA and AR build; the A0 record, the U-4 record and Root ruling G3. Draft 2 (the text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R10 (items 2 and 3, on draft 1 and its changed-lines audit: GO_WITH_CONDITIONS, 0 P1, 1 P2, 10 P3), Root ruling R12 (item 17, with the R12 items 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11, 14 and 15 that it names or relies on) and Root ruling R13 (items 2 to 16, with amendment R13a); its changes are items 25 to 40 below, and items 7, 9, 10, 13, 17, 18, 19, 23 and 24 are restated where those rulings change them. Draft 3 (the second text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R14 (items 2 to 17, on the draft 2 changed-lines re-check, GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 2 P2, 7 P3, and on the draft 2 open items D1 to D8); its changes are items 41 to 48 below, and items 9, 17, 27 and 37 are restated where R14 changes them. Ruling R14 item 9 confirms that ruling R13 items 3, 9, 10, 13, 14 and 16, and ruling R12 items 7, 10 and 14 and the applied parts of item 15, belong in revision 7. Draft 5 (the fourth text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R16 (item 3) and Root ruling R17 (items 2 and 3, on the draft 4 changed-lines re-check, GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 0 P2, 4 P3, and on the draft 4 open items D13 and D14); its changes are items 55 to 60 below. Draft 6 (the fifth text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R18 (items 2 to 10, on the draft 5 changed-lines re-check, GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 1 P2, 6 P3, and on the draft 5 open items D15 and D16); its changes are items 61 to 65 below. Draft 7 (the sixth text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R21 (items 2 to 7, on the draft 6 changed-lines re-check, GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 0 P2, 5 P3), with the A2, RA and ADP rules of Root ruling R19 and the A1 rules of Root ruling R20 that it puts into the text; its changes are items 66 to 74 below. Draft 8 (the seventh text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R22 (items 2 to 9, on the draft 7 changed-lines re-check, GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 1 P2, 8 P3, with amendment R19a and the draft 7 open items D17 to D22); its changes are items 75 to 82 below. Draft 9 (the eighth text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R23 (items 2 to 7, on the draft 8 changed-lines re-check, GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 1 P2, 6 P3, and on the draft 8 open items D23 to D25) and Root ruling R24 (item 6, with the readings of item 3, the behaviour of fix round 5 in item 4 and the sums of the A2RA follow-up fix round 4); its changes are items 83 to 89 below. Draft 10 (the ninth text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R25 (items 2 to 6, on the draft 9 changed-lines audit r9, GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 3 P2, 8 P3, and on the draft 9 open items D26 to D29, with the G1, G2 and G5 behaviour of item 7, which fix round 6 builds); its changes are items 90 to 96 below. Draft 11 (the tenth text-fix pass) adds Root ruling R26 (items 2 to 7 and the contract sentence of item 8, on the A2RA fix round 6 review: audit r7, second opinion r7 and the tester; and on the draft 10 open items D30 and D31); its changes are items 97 to 103 below. Each change names its source. Revision 6 text that no item changes is kept, and the revision 6 note below is history. The five fixed sentences S0 (outcomes), S1 (next RA), S2 (exclusion), S3 (terminal RA and the accepted RA receipt) and S4 (the A2 receipt that reopens the chain) stay word for word equal in section 8, section 9, the owner summary (items 5 and 6) and GO-RA: no item below changes any of them.

1. **Author readings confirmed (R7 item 2).** The revision 6 author readings (a) to (g) are confirmed. Reading (a) is recorded as a named adaptation away from the P5a code names: `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` absorbs a failed unwind reload, a failed loaded-configuration check and a failed unwind start, and `A2_UNWIND_FAILED` keeps the RD-32 form only. Section 18.
2. **Row recognition (R7 item 3, P3-1).** For row L0, any listed write, rename or archive line of the re-pin layer, labelled `ATTEMPTED` or `DONE`, refuses. For row LP, a rename counts as shown only when labelled `DONE`, or labelled `ATTEMPTED` with the file renamed on disk; an `ATTEMPTED` line with the pending file still pending is row LP. Section 8 (rows L0-n and LP-n and the paragraph after the table), GO-RA and section 15.
3. **A2 progress writes (R7 item 3, P3-2).** Any failed A2 progress write before readiness is an unwind trigger. A failed unwind progress write other than the pre-rename digest line ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` and starts nothing more, with the end row read from the files on disk and the reload label (row LP-n included). Sections 6.4 and 6.6, GO-A2 and section 15.
4. **RA reserve (R7 item 3, P3-3).** The 474-second reserve is measured against the in-run limit of 1140 seconds (the host bound 1200 minus 60). The 100-second stop added after a failed unwind start is host time, not API gap, and the gap bound stays 380. Section 8 (Bounds) and section 11.1.
5. **Reconciling reload (R7 item 3, P3-4).** A time-budget stop or a failed progress write at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r counts as a failed reconciling reload (`RA_API_STOPPED`, end row LU-r or LR-r unless the reload is labelled `DONE`), not case 7. Section 8 (cases 4 and 7, the outcome table), GO-RA and section 15.
6. **Case 5 end codes (R7 item 3, P3-5).** Case 5 with immediate checks ends `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` or `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`; the case 5 path never ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`. Section 8 (case 5, the outcome table), GO-RA and section 15.
7. **Heartbeat interval (R7 item 4, superseded by R9 items 11b and 10m).** Ruling R9 item 11b replaces only the environment override of ruling R7 item 4 (the 250 ms default at the pin stays, with the minimum 50 and the build assertion that the two reads of the tick-time rule fit the RA gate budget; ruling R10 item 3(f)): the interval is a manifest input from 50 to 5000 ms; the core filters the heartbeat rows to the trading worker role for the own-row and freshness rules only, and checks mode and Live on all rows (ruling R12 item 7); the two reads that test for advancing are at least 2 × interval + 250 ms apart; the timing proof asserts the A2 and RA gate budgets at the 5000 ms maximum. Sections 8 and 11.1, GO-RA and section 15.
8. **Composite layer fit (R8 item 3, N6; restated by R9 item 3).** `layerNameFits` is true only when the P5a flag layer and the admission-off layer are both found, no live layer other than the admission-off layer sorts after the P5a flag layer, and the name-derivation function returns a name. The A2 gate uses the same composite rule, because copying the derivation function by value is not enough. Sections 5.2 and 6.2, GO-A1, GO-A2 and section 15.
9. **Phase-aware error routing (R9 item 2).** A run has four phases (GATE, EFFECT, READY, MARKET). In READY the tool sends no control command at all; in MARKET only the market branch's own start, readiness poll and stop (in RA also the reset-failed) are admitted, as a consumable allowance granted when the change is seen (ruling R9a; ruling R10 item 2). This is enforced by the error routing and by a guard in the control-command layer. The A2 and RA end codes after readiness or a market change are stated. A termination sends no command in any phase and ends `PRE_TERMINATED` before the first effect and `EFF_TERMINATED` after it (ruling R12 item 5; ruling R14 item 4). Sections 6.6 to 6.8 and 8 (cases 8 and 10, the tables), GO-A2, GO-RA and section 15.
10. **Crash-looping staging API (R9 item 5).** The staging API is exempt from the settle-wait refusal; in automatic restart, in the activating state with a start sub-state, or with its own pending start, stop or restart job, it is classified restarting and stopped, and its own job is not a pending job. Section 8 (Gate, API state), GO-RA and section 15.
11. **A2M and AR refusals (R9 item 6).** Host health is recorded and never refused. A2M refuses only on another S6 job in flight or the staging API not active; AR refuses only as section 6.12 lists. A worker-thread throw is recorded as unknown. The A2M and AR regions hold no control-command shape. Sections 6.11 and 6.12, GO-A2, GO-AR and section 15.
12. **Inventory function (R9 item 8).** One shared function writes the A2 inventory and reads it in RA: digests sorted non-decreasing, duplicates allowed, at most 64. Above 64, A2 refuses before any effect with `PRE_INVENTORY_TOO_LARGE`. Sections 6.2, 6.6 and 8, GO-A2 and section 15.
13. **Receipt frame and payload ceiling (R9 items 9 and 11a).** The worst-case A2 and RA receipt bodies (64 inventory entries, every progress line and the maximum prior records) fit the receipt frame limit, asserted by a test. The payload ceiling is 262144 bytes for each of the four modes; the build asserts the size with a worst-case manifest, and before each GO request Root runs the payload-size check with the real manifest (ruling R10 item 3(i)). Sections 6.10, 8, 11 and 11.1 and section 15.
14. **`marketChanged` (R9 item 10f).** The RA receipt carries `marketChanged` in every end. A market change plus a failed immediate check stays `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, with `marketChanged` true. Section 8 (cases 8 and 10, the outcome table) and section 15.
15. **`resetFailedGranted` (R9 item 10g).** Reset-failed is bound to a manifest boolean that is true only when the GO-RA carries the optional clause; a failed API with the flag false refuses before any effect with `PRE_RESET_FAILED_NOT_GRANTED`. Section 8 (API state), GO-RA and section 15.
16. **Lost receipts (R9 items 10h and 11c).** A record with source PROGRESS (A2 or RA) carries sha null and the job ID; RA reads the progress record that the job ID names, checks the job ID and the form, and records its sha256 in the RA gate line and receipt, and Root reviews it afterwards; a RECEIPT record needs a sha and non-null lines. The private A2 receipt body is the RA manifest record form. Sections 6.10 and 8, GO-RA and section 15.
17. **Budget stop versus termination (R9 item 10i; R12 item 5).** In A2, a step-budget or in-run-limit stop before readiness is a failure and unwinds. A termination sends no command in any phase: A2 and RA end `PRE_TERMINATED` before the first effect (no effect) and `EFF_TERMINATED` after it, with the end row from the disk and the API state as read, and never claim the API stopped (ruling R12 item 5, which withdraws the split of ruling R10 item 3(b); ruling R14 item 4). In RA the tool writes a `NOTE TERMINATED` line, there is no put-back and the result says terminated; a step-budget or in-run-limit stop keeps case 7. Sections 6.6 and 8 and section 15.
18. **Torn progress line (R9 item 10l).** After a failed or partial write the writer starts the next line on a fresh line and writes a `NOTE TORN_PREVIOUS` line; the reader tolerates exactly that pattern (the torn step counts as `ATTEMPTED` and the disk decides) and refuses any other unparseable line. One torn last line with no line ending, at the end of the file, is read as an `ATTEMPTED` step of unknown kind and the disk decides; if the row's facts are missing because of it, row recognition refuses (ruling R12 item 15). Sections 6.4 and 8 and section 15.
19. **Archive forms (R9 item 11d).** The archive suffix forms of the build are accepted (this packet names no file form; ruling R10 item 3(g)); RA decides the row from the files on disk and the records, never from a suffix alone. Section 8.
20. **AR with the A2 tool package; adapters (R9 items 13 and 11e).** The AR tool is built, reviewed and accepted with the A2, A2M and RA tool, not with A1. The nine adapters are built in their own lane (ADP) before any GO and reviewed inside the A2RA fix rounds, where the lane keeps its own suite and mutants (ruling R17 item 2); no mode, A2M and AR included, is accepted for a real run before the build fix round and ADP are both accepted. Sections 3, 6.1, 6.12, 15 and 17.
21. **Pin and A0 (Root, 28 October 2026; A0 record).** The pin is `33e5a8c3c31b0fca61fa44792aad7d1cea769c35`; the `[pin]` placeholder stays in the GO templates and Root fills it with that commit. Section 2.2 gets the facts of the A0 record (7 changed runtime files, API closure 147 by both scanners, Paper entry list 11 and union 32, cache tokens `qr2o9`, case 4 with data-time stops within 30 days, Root's 384 MB estimate and its bound). Section 4 notes that A0 is done for this pin. Sections 2.1, 2.2, 2.4, 4 and 12 and the owner summary (items 1 and 2).
22. **U-4 result (U-4 record).** Section 10 records the result (a) PASS for RM and RC, the result (b) not pinned in the tools, the both-revisions rule for a later GO-RM or GO-RC and the retirement-inventory token rule; section 12 records the RM and RC availability residuals. Section 6.1 item 4 notes that the blocker is recorded.
23. **Gate L (ruling G3).** The sentence about a later packet and option A states that option A comes after a passing gate L and a staging re-measure, that gate L run 1 did not select it, because bar 2 (first touch) failed also at the shipped budget, and that it stays open (ruling R10 item 3(d)). Section 17.
24. **Install (R7 item 1; R10 items 3(e) and 4).** Revision 6 with rulings R7 to R26 is the build contract until revision 7 is installed; revision 7 is the contract after installation. Root installs revision 7 under `docs/` only after A2RA fix round 3 and its follow-up are accepted (the follow-up is fix rounds 5 and 6 (rulings R24 and R25), accepted by ruling R26), A1 fix round 2 is accepted (ruling R20), the latest text re-check has passed and Root has taken the final changed-lines look, takes `PACKET_SHA` from the LF Git blob and sets it in the A1, A2RA and ADP tools. Sections 15, 17 and 18.

Draft 2 (the text-fix pass; rulings R10, R12 and R13). Each item names its source; the findings of the draft 1 changed-lines audit are prefixed "audit".

25. **Market branch and the guard (R10 item 2; R9a, R12 item 3; audit P2-1).** Author reading (a) is confirmed in this form: the market branch's one start, its readiness poll and, after a failed start or readiness, one proved stop (in RA also the reset-failed under R9 item 10g), granted as a consumable allowance when the change is seen; nothing else in MARKET; nothing at all in READY. GO-A2 and GO-RA take the qualifier "other than the start, readiness poll and stop of the market branch above". Section 15 adds the guard-exemption test and the flipped t-phase test. Sections 6.4, 6.6, 8 (the Effects and case 8), GO-A2, GO-RA, 15 and 18.
26. **Budget-stop wording (R10 item 3(a); audit P3-1).** "Step-budget or in-run-limit stop" is used in every place the audit lists, and the tests inject both stops. Sections 6.6 to 6.8, 8 and 15.
27. **Termination (R12 item 5, which withdraws the split of R10 item 3(b); audit P3-2).** A termination never sends a command, in any phase. A2 and RA end `PRE_TERMINATED` before the first effect (no effect) and `EFF_TERMINATED` after it, with the end row from the disk and the API state as read, and never claim the API stopped; the RA family treats `EFF_TERMINATED` as a termination with no end code, and it never counts as the API ready (ruling R14 items 4 and 17). `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` stays for its non-termination causes, which removes the overlap of audit P3-2. Sections 6.6 and 8, GO-A2, GO-RA and section 15.
28. **Derive-b (R10 item 3(c); audit P3-3).** Derive-b of the A0 record (Opus 5.5 / high, independent, from Git) is the N1 re-derivation that A1 acceptance needs. Sections 4, 15 and 17.
29. **Option A (R10 item 3(d); audit P3-4).** The option A sentence reads as ruled. Item 23 and section 17.
30. **One contract sentence and the install order (R10 item 3(e) and item 4; audit P3-6).** The title status line, item 24, the Root rulings list (item 20) and section 15 use the one sentence "Revision 6 with rulings R7 to R26 is the build contract until revision 7 is installed; revision 7 is the contract after installation." Revision 7 goes under `docs/` only after A2RA fix round 3 and its follow-up are accepted (the follow-up is fix rounds 5 and 6 (rulings R24 and R25), accepted by ruling R26), A1 fix round 2 is accepted (ruling R20), the latest text re-check has passed and Root has taken the final changed-lines look. Sections 15, 17 and 18.
31. **Heartbeat interval scope and Live (R10 item 3(f); audit P3-7; R12 item 7).** Ruling R9 item 11b replaces only the environment override of ruling R7 item 4, and the 250 ms default stays. The role filter serves only the own-row and freshness rules; mode and Live are checked on all rows, and a Live signal above zero refuses (the admin health `liveEnabled` check moved to the owner's step; R12 item 17). Item 7, sections 6.7, 6.9 and 8, owner summary item 7, checklist item D and GO-A2.
32. **Citations and archive forms (R10 item 3(g); audit P3-8).** Reading (c) cites section 6.4 for the failed-check code; reading (d) names no file form, because ruling R9 item 11d accepts the archive suffix forms. Sections 8 and 18.
33. **Activating state wording (R10 item 3(h); audit P3-9).** Section 8 (API state), item 10 above and GO-RA use "in the activating state with a start sub-state".
34. **Payload (R10 item 3(i); audit P3-10).** The build asserts the size with a worst-case manifest; before each GO request Root runs the payload-size check with the real manifest. Item 13, sections 11 and 15 and section 18.
35. **Extra R9 items (R10 item 3(j); audit P3-5).** R9 items 3 (as R8 N6), 6, 9, 10f, 10l and 10m, with the writer's consequential edits, belong in revision 7. A2 keeps `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`; RA keeps `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` with `marketChanged` true. R9 items 10a to 10e, 10j, 10k, 10n, 10o, 4, 7 and 12 stay build-only. Section 18.
36. **Case 7 in START and the in-run limit (R12 item 6; R13 item 9).** After the RA start command and before readiness is proved (phase START), a step-budget or in-run-limit stop first gets one proved stop; the case 7 put-back runs only after a proved stop, and no start follows; a stop not proved ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`. When the in-run limit, not the readiness step, runs out inside the readiness poll, the core throws the budget code and takes that route, and the receipt records `startSent` and the API class. Sections 6.6 and 8 (case 7) and section 15.
37. **Stop and start errors (R13 items 3 to 6 and 8).** An error in a stop's proof wait other than a termination counts as not proved and sends no further command; a command timeout at the first stop ends as a stop not proved, and any other failure of the stop command gets one snapshot read; case 2 with a failed API runs the put-back and its reload, with no stop and no start, and ends `RA_UNWOUND`; the A2 market branch with a stop not proved ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`, also when no unwind ran; a market-branch start that leaves the API failed sends no stop. Ruling R14 items 2 and 3 name the codes of the failed first stop command (items 41 and 42). Sections 6.4, 6.6 and 8, GO-A2, GO-RA and section 15.
38. **Restart count (R13 item 2, which amends R12 item 14).** n0 is read right before the start command; n1 is the count in the snapshot that ends the start poll; the start is not ready unless n1 is 0 or equal to n0; the count read after readiness must equal n1. The rule holds whether the service manager resets the count or keeps it, and its residual is in section 12. Sections 6.4, 8, 12 and 15.
39. **Trees, case 5 and the RA stream digest (R13 items 10 to 13; R12 item 15).** Every tree check uses the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule); RA checks the staged tree right before the put-back start (a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start: `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, why TREE, `startSent` false [history; since draft 7 `startSent` is run-level and a refused start records `startRefused`, and since draft 8 it also records `startRefusedAt` (fix round 4 named it `startRefusedFor`; draft 10 followed that name; ruling R26 item 2 corrects it, item 97), section 6.6; ruling R22 items 8 and 9; ruling R23 item 7]); case 5 immediate checks run only in rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n, and an unknown loaded tree sends no start; a running stream with an unreadable process digest refuses at the RA gate, and an unreadable stream at the reload re-check is a failure, not a market change. Sections 6.2 and 8, 11.1 and 15.
40. **Health, port, torn last line, Live and host scan (R12 items 8, 11, 15 and 17; R13 items 15 and 16).** `HEALTH_503` is accepted only in RA, and only when the gate and the immediate read both classify the trading worker as not running; a port is busy on a LISTEN row or any row with a nonzero inode on the API's local port in tcp or tcp6; one torn last line with no line ending is read as an `ATTEMPTED` step of unknown kind; the admin health `liveEnabled` check moves to the owner's step; the A2M and AR in-flight scan counts a module-input process of another Node binary as a payload and fails closed; the residuals of R13 item 15 are recorded in section 12. Sections 6.4, 6.6 to 6.12, 8, 12 and 15.
41. **Failed first stop command in A2 (R14 item 2; audit P2-1; open item D3).** A failed first stop command whose snapshot read shows the API running with no job and the gate's main process ends A2 `A2_UNWOUND`, end row L0-n: nothing is started, the API stays running on `ba1a433` as found, its readiness is not re-proved, and the receipt carries `stopCommandFailed` true, `startSent` false and `apiAtEnd`. The next step is unchanged: Root review. Sections 6.6 (text and table), GO-A2, 15 and 18. The build follows in the next round.
42. **Failed first stop command with a failed API (R14 item 3; audit P2-2; amends R13 item 4).** A failed stop command whose snapshot shows the API failed with no job is RA case 5 (`RA_UNWOUND`, the failed API recorded) and ends A2 `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` (a failed API counts as stopped). `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` covers exactly a command timeout, a stop not proved within its wait, an error in the proof wait (R13 item 3) and a failed stop command whose snapshot shows neither no-job state. Sections 6.6 and 8 (Effects, case 7 code precedence, the outcome table), GO-RA and section 15. The build follows in the next round. [History pointer, ruling R22 item 8: "exactly" lists the stop causes of this item; the throw after a start command while readiness of that start is not proved also ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` (amendment R19a; section 8).]
43. **Termination codes (R14 item 4; audit P3-1).** A termination before the first effect ends `PRE_TERMINATED` with no effect; after the first effect it ends `EFF_TERMINATED`; a termination sends no command in any phase. The A2 termination row is split in two, which removes its overlap with the gate-refusal row. Sections 6.6, 8, GO-A2, GO-RA and 15; items 9, 17 and 27 are restated.
44. **GO-RA paths (R14 item 5; audit P3-2).** GO-RA names the START stop of R12 item 6 (a proved stop before the put-back), the proof-wait error rule of R13 item 3 and the NEW tree check before the case 2 put-back start (R13 item 11). Section 14 (GO-RA).
45. **Heartbeat and Live in revision 7 (R14 items 6 and 9; audit P3-3; open item D6).** Section 6.2 gains the Live-signal refusal, the unfiltered mode and Live check and `HEARTBEAT_UNREADABLE` for the A2 gate; section 8 (the heartbeat bullet and the GO-RA gate list) gains the empty set while the trading worker runs, `HEARTBEAT_UNREADABLE` and the Live signal; section 15 gains the tests.
46. **Section 12 reference and the START stop count (R14 items 7 and 8; audit P3-4, P3-5).** The A2 termination row refers to the section 12 residual (the next RA refuses `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED` after a termination at the pending-write `ATTEMPTED` line). Note (5) of section 11.1 excepts the in-run-limit route of R13 item 9, which is the section 12 residual "past the deadline".

The section 18 history is unchanged.

S0 to S4 are unchanged.
49. **Draft 4 base and scope (R15 items 1 and 7; draft 3 re-check).** Draft 3 is the base. The third text-fix pass applies R15 items 2 to 6 only: the P2 finding and the five P3 findings of the draft 3 changed-lines re-check (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 1 P2, 5 P3) and the open items D9 to D12. The fixed sentences S0 to S4 are unchanged (4 places each). Items 9, 17, 27 and 43 read the first effect as item 51 defines it. Section 18 (D13 and D14 are new).
50. **Next step after `PRE_TERMINATED` (R15 item 2; audit P2-1; open item D9).** Sentence S1 governs every termination: `PRE_TERMINATED` never counts as an end with the API ready, the next RA follows after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from that attempt's start, and the RA family treats it as a termination with no end code. The RA row says so and "open" is removed. In A2 it is handled like a gate refusal: a next A2 attempt uses a contingency job ID only when GO-A2 names one, otherwise it needs a new GO. Sections 6.6 and 8 (the tables) and 18. The build follows in the next round.
51. **First effect (R15 item 3; audit P3-2; open item D10).** The first effect is the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload. Sections 6.6 (Termination, Phases and the A2 table row) and 8 (Effects), GO-A2, GO-RA and section 15. The build follows in the next round: the effect flag flips at the first host-changing step in every mode, with tests in each mode.
52. **`A2_UNWOUND` after a failed first stop command (R15 item 4; open item D11; refines item 41).** This end never reopens the chain under S4, because readiness is not re-proved. The next step is Root review; a next A2 needs a Root ruling or a packet revision (section 9); RA in row L0-n stays available. The `A2_UNWOUND` row says so for this cause. Section 6.6 (table).
53. **Install wording (R15 item 5; audit P3-3; open item D12).** Items 24 and 30, sections 15, 17 and 18 take the install conditions of ruling R14 item 19 in its words: fix round 3 and its follow-up accepted; the latest text re-check passed; Root's final changed-lines look. The next re-check is the draft 4 re-check. The ADP review is no longer named (open item D13).
54. **P3 items (R15 item 6; audit P3-1, P3-4, P3-5).** The case 7 code precedence drops "of the first stop command": a command timeout of any stop command (the first stop, the START stop of ruling R12 item 6, or the stop before the case 2 put-back) ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`. The GO-A2 gate re-check list names the section 6.2 refusals (a Live signal above zero, an unreadable Live count and `HEARTBEAT_UNREADABLE`). The change map counts are corrected in the change map. Sections 8, 14 (GO-A2) and 18.

Draft 5 (the fourth text-fix pass; rulings R16 and R17). Each item names its source.

The fourth text-fix pass applies R16 item 3 and R17 items 2 and 3 only: the four P3 findings of the draft 4 changed-lines re-check (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 0 P2, 4 P3), the open items D13 and D14, and the manifest and binding sources of R17 item 1 with the drop-in digest text of R16 item 3. The fixed sentences S0 to S4 are unchanged (4 places each). Section 18 (D15 and D16 are new).
56. **A1 identifies the layers by name; private digest export (R16 items 2 and 3; R17 item 3).** A1 identifies the admission-off layer by its recorded name (it must be live and sort last) and the P5a flag layer by its recorded name and digest. It exports privately, in its private receipt and retained output and never a name, the admission-off layer content digest, the sorted list of the live drop-in content digests, the drop-in list digest (P5a form) and the live count; the tracked result names only their presence. GO-A1 says this in plain words next to "only digests, counts and the one boolean leave that process; never a name or content". Sections 5.2 and 14 (GO-A1). The build is A1 fix round 2 of ruling R16 item 4; this pass builds nothing.

The admission-off layer content is first read by A1 (a residual). Sections 6.2, 8, 12 and 17.

Section 10 says that the manifests pin the U-4 record and that no AR manifest exists before the inventory record. Section 12 gains the RA baseline residual after a production change. Sections 6.13, 10 and 12.

P3-3: the `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` row cause adds the stop before the case 2 put-back. P3-4: section 15 names the tests of the next RA after `PRE_TERMINATED`. Sections 8 (table), 15 and 18.

69. **A1 rules (R21 item 4; ruling R20 items 2 and 3).** Section 5.2: more than 64 live drop-ins is `PRE_DROPIN_SHAPE`, and duplicate live content is not a shape refusal, because names identify the two layers. Section 5.1 (new bullet) and section 6.1 item 1: Root accepts an A1 record for A2 and RA use only when `layerNameFits`, `admissionOffFound` and `p5aLayerFound` are true, the admission-off digest is present, the P5a layer digest appears exactly once among the drop-in digests, the live count equals the layer count, the drop-in list digest equals the collector c1 export value, the staged tree is exact and the end reading equals the gate; a record that misses any of these is a Root review item, and A2 is not requested. Section 15 (A1 tool bullet).
70. **Tree checks, stop claims, receipt fields and the failed-API unwind (R21 item 5(a) to (d); ruling R19 item 4(a) to (d)).** A tree check runs before every start: the A2 unwind start on the old tree, the RA put-back start and the RA case 5 restart; a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` or `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, why TREE). No end code claims that the API stopped without a proved stop; after the run limit one read-only class read records `apiAtEnd`, or `UNKNOWN` with `apiAtEndWhy` LIMIT. The receipt fields are `startSent` (run-level, never set back), `startRefused` and the start it refused, `stopProved`, `marketStopProved`, `apiAtEnd`, `apiAtEndWhy` and `stopCommandFailed`. The A2 unwind with a failed API sends no stop, runs the archive and its reload, sends no start and ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with the failed API recorded. Sections 6.6, 6.10, 8 and 14 (GO-A2, GO-RA), the outcome tables and section 15.
71. **503 rule, `PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT`, real-path rule, served-URL rule, wording check and `argvMax` (R21 item 5(e) to (j); ruling R19 items 2 and 4(e) to (l)).** `HEALTH_503` needs the trading worker to show the same not-running class (INACTIVE or FAILED) at the gate read and at the immediate read (section 8, GO-RA, section 15). The RA gate stream-digest refusal, and every place that named `STREAM_UNREADABLE`, use `PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT` (sections 6.4, 8 and 15). The tree walk takes the P5a real-path form (the resolved parent plus the tree name; the root is not a link) and a bound root must be a canonical string (sections 6.2 and 8). The served-URL rule is the P5a rule, a path with an optional `?v=` cache token (section 6.7). The wording of ruling R14 items 2 and 3 and ruling R15 items 2 and 3 matches ruling R19 item 4(i) to (k) and needs no change. Section 6.13 drops `argvMax` and states the roles-hash check.
72. **Stream configuration digest source (R21 item 6).** The stream configuration digest is the P4M m1 a03 manifest's market configuration digest of the stream, which the m1 a03 run found equal; the P4M binding holds no digest field. Sections 6.3, 6.12, 6.13 (new row) and 14 (GO-A2) say this. Item 7 of the revision 4 history stays as history, with a pointer.
73. **Section 12 residuals (R21 item 7).** The 4-second walk cap and the 50-second IMMEDIATE cap are not measured on the host; a slow walk refuses before an effect or sends no start, and a slow IMMEDIATE pass ends in a terminal code with no command. After the run limit `apiAtEnd` can be `UNKNOWN` (LIMIT). Section 12 (two new bullets).
74. **Draft 7 open items (R21 item 8; writer readings).** Writer readings that no ruling decides are in section 18 (D17 to D22), for Root to rule. Ruled by ruling R22 (draft 8, items 75 to 82). Items 70 and 71 above state the draft 7 wording; items 75 to 82 refine it where they say so.

76. **Scope of the no-false-"stopped" rule (R22 item 2; audit P2-1; amendment R19a).** The rule that a throw after a start command that is not followed by a proved stop ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` (RA) or `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with `stopProved` false (A2) applies only while readiness of that start is not proved. After readiness is proved, `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, the case 5 and case 10 codes, the READY rule and the A2 check codes stand. Sections 6.6, 8 (Effects and the two table rows), 12, GO-A2, GO-RA and 15.
77. **`stopProved` and `marketStopProved` (R22 item 3; D17; audit P3-2).** `stopProved` says whether the run's last API stop command was proved: true when proved, false when a stop was sent and not proved, null when no stop was sent. `marketStopProved` is the same fact for the stop sent in the A2 market branch, and null outside it; in the market branch both fields are equal. Sections 6.4, 6.6, 6.10, the A2 table row and 15. [History pointer, ruling R23 item 2: these two definitions, and "in the market branch both fields are equal", are replaced by the definitions of item 84; the fields are equal only when the branch sent a stop and no start followed it. Ruling R25 item 2, item 92: the definitions of item 84 are replaced in turn.]

The A2 reload re-check (section 6.4) and the RA market re-check (case 8) go back to `STREAM_UNREADABLE`, as in draft 6. The final pass checks each code against the built core. This refines item 71. [History pointer, ruling R25 item 5: the built code at the A2 reload re-check and the RA market re-check is `EFF_STREAM_UNREADABLE`, not `STREAM_UNREADABLE`; item 94.]
79. **A2 unwind with a failed API and no layer written (R22 item 5; D19).** With no layer written and no reload run, the end row is L0-n: nothing is renamed, no reload runs, and no stop or start is sent. The run ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with the failed API recorded; the next step is Root review, and RA in row L0-n, with the reset-failed grant, can recover. Sections 6.6 (bullet and table row), GO-A2 and 15.

Sections 6.6 and 8 say that each new tree walk is counted in its own path sum (section 11.1, final pass; the sums come with draft 9, item 88). Section 15 gains one test for the count guard.

84. **`stopProved` and `marketStopProved` (R23 item 2; audit-r8 P2-1; R24 item 4, F5).** `stopProved` is true when the run ends with the API proved stopped by its last stop command and no start command was sent after that proof; false when a stop command was sent and not proved, or when a start command was sent after the last proved stop (the API may run); null when the run sent no API stop command. A stop whose end class read is stopped or failed is proved, and the A2 failed-API unwind road sets `stopProved` true with `apiFailed` true. `marketStopProved` covers only the A2 market-branch stop: true when proved, false when sent and not proved, null when the branch sent no stop; the two fields are equal only when the branch sent a stop and no start followed it. The packet auditor checks both definitions at every assignment site against the fix round 5 core; where the code differs and the code is the safer reading, the text follows the code and Root records why, otherwise Root rules a narrow code fix. Sections 6.4, 6.6, 6.10, the A2 table row, 8 (Effects) and 15. Item 77 is history. [History pointer, ruling R25 item 2: the definitions of this item are replaced by the built rule of item 92 (absent key, true by a stop read after the last stop with no start after it, `apiAtEnd` never proof, not recorded on pass ends); the item 84 text on "null" and on "a stop whose end class read is stopped or failed" is superseded.]

89. **Section 12 and section 18 (R24 item 6; R23 items 3 to 5).** Section 12: the walk-cap residual names its two fail-closed places (the RA case 5 restart and the A2 unwind start leave the API stopped on a false timeout, why TREE), and a new bullet records the served-URL tightenings with Root's dry-admission condition. Section 18: the draft 8 open items D23 to D25 are marked ruled by R23, and the new questions are D26 to D29. [History pointer, ruling R25: D26 to D29 are ruled; the walk-cap residual names three fail-closed places since draft 10, item 95.]

Draft 10 (the ninth text-fix pass; ruling R25). Each item names its source.

90. **Draft 10 base, scope, contract sentence and status lines (R25 items 1, 5 and 6; D29; audit-r9 P3-5, P3-8).** Draft 9 is the base; it is not installed. The ninth text-fix pass applies ruling R25 items 2 to 6 and the G1, G2 and G5 behaviour of item 7 (which fix round 6 builds; the text states it as built) only. The contract sentence reads "Revision 6 with rulings R7 to R25 is the build contract until revision 7 is installed; revision 7 is the contract after installation" in the title, items 24 and 30, the Root rulings list (item 20) and section 15, and the section 15 label reads "Revision 7 (rulings R7 to R10 and R12 to R25)". The status lines read "the follow-up is fix rounds 5 and 6 (rulings R24 and R25), accepted by ruling R26" in items 24 and 30, section 15, section 17 Next (twice) and section 18 item 1 (D29; the title holds only the contract sentence). The section 18 heading names the rulings up to R25. The fixed sentences S0 to S4 are unchanged (4 places each). This pass builds, installs, tests and accepts nothing, and every GO and gate requirement is unchanged except where items 91 to 96 say.
91. **`startRefusedFor` (R25 item 5; D27; audit-r9 P2-2) [history; superseded].** Draft 10 named the receipt field `startRefusedFor`, the name that the fix round 4 report gave. [History pointer, ruling R26 item 2: fix round 5 renamed the field to `startRefusedAt`, and the fix round 6 core writes `startRefusedAt` at all 4 sites and `startRefusedFor` nowhere. Ruling R25 item 5 (D27) read snapshot r4 and is corrected. Since draft 11 the packet uses `startRefusedAt` in every current place; item 97.]
92. **`stopProved` and `marketStopProved` (R25 item 2; D26; audit-r9 P2-1; audit-r6 P3-1; second-r6 P3-1, P3-3, P3-4; G1).** The built rule replaces the definitions of item 84. `stopProved` is absent (no key) when the run sent no API stop command; true when, after the last API stop command, a stop read showed the API stopped or failed and no start was attempted after the last such read; false otherwise. A stop read is the stop's own proof poll or a stop-context read. `apiAtEnd` is never proof, so a run can end with `stopProved` false and `apiAtEnd` `INACTIVE` or `FAILED`. The field is not recorded on a pass end (`A2_PASSED`, and `RA_API_READY` with the case 5 restart). `marketStopProved` is written only in the A2 market branch, only when the branch sent its stop (true proved, false not proved), and is absent otherwise; RA never writes it. The RA asymmetry is kept: RA case 5 with a failed stop command and a stopped read ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` with `stopProved` false, where the A2 counterpart records true (section 12 residual). A failed API counts as stopped only with an empty job queue, as an inactive one does (G1); a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped, the stop poll continues and the end is not proved. Sections 6.4, 6.6, 6.10, the A2 table row, 8 (Effects, case 8 and Receipt fields), 12 and 15.
93. **F1 and F4 as built; RA with no effect (R25 items 3 and 4; audit-r9 P2-3; G2).** A `PRE_TIME_BUDGET` stop routes like `EFF_TIME_BUDGET` at the same step. Case 5 answers before the budget branch: a budget stop in the PRECHECK or FILE phases before the rename ends `RA_UNWOUND` with no command, and after the port check it ends `RA_BUDGET_STOP`. The F1 text of ruling R24 was wrong on this point. After a case 5 refusal for `TREE` or `TREE_UNKNOWN` the row's disk reads and one capped end class read (`apiAtEnd`) still run, no stop-if-runs check runs, no command is sent and the end code stays `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` (F4). G2: when the effect flag is false at a trigger in RA, no command is sent, in particular no stop-if-runs check in START; the end code is the one F1 gives or the termination code. Sections 8 (case 5, case 7 and both outcome tables) and 15.
94. **Codes, end form and wording (R25 item 5; D28; audit-r9 P3-1 to P3-4).** With the effect flag false the A2 route ends `PRE_TIME_BUDGET` or `PRE_TERMINATED` (D28). An A2 end with a PRE_ code and the flag false sent no command; its receipt still has `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, and the runner evaluates `A2_STOP_EFFECT` with `hostEffect` EFFECT (stricter, accepted). Such an end needs no RA; the next step is Root's review of the receipt confirming zero control commands, then a fresh A2 gate under a contingency job ID with its own GO, and the owner's current authority covers attempt a01 only (also noted in the `PRE_TERMINATED` row). The reload and market re-check code is `EFF_STREAM_UNREADABLE`; `STREAM_UNREADABLE` is only the A2 immediate-check token (P3-1). Only the snapshot, budget and termination codes take their PRE_ or EFF_ name from the effect flag; step codes are fixed names and can occur with the flag false (P3-2). `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT` when the cap forbids the read or the read fails at or after the cap, and `READ` when the read fails otherwise (P3-4). Sections 6.4, 6.6, 6.10, 8 and the first A2 table row.
95. **Walk paths, section 11.1 and section 12 (R25 items 5 and 7, G5; audit-r9 P3-7).** The A2 stop-grace path and the pre-write abort path end in the walked unwind start, so they include the 4-second walk: the stop-grace path is 30 + 70 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 149 (145 before) and the pre-write abort path is 30 + 5 + 15 + 4 + 40 + 5 = 99 (95 before). Both stay under 310, the largest term of the A2 worst in-run path stays 438, and no bound, limit, wait or other sum changes. Section 12: the walk-cap residual names three fail-closed places (the RA put-back start, the RA case 5 restart and the A2 unwind start), and a new bullet records the RA stop-command asymmetry of item 92.
96. **Section 18 and the change map (R25 item 5; audit-r9 P3-5, P3-6, P3-8).** The section 18 heading names the rulings up to R25 and holds the draft 10 bullet. D26 to D29 are marked ruled by R25 (D26 by item 2, D27 to D29 by item 5). The change map row K14 points at items 66 and 80. The new open items are D30 and D31. [History pointer, ruling R26 items 5 and 6: D30 and D31 are ruled, for the final packet audit r10 to confirm; items 99 and 100.]

Draft 11 (the tenth text-fix pass; ruling R26). Each item names its source.

97. **`startRefusedAt` (R26 item 2; second-r7 P2-1; D27 corrected).** Fix round 5 renamed the receipt field to `startRefusedAt`, the name of ruling R22 item 9, and the fix round 6 core writes `startRefusedAt` at all 4 sites and `startRefusedFor` nowhere. Ruling R25 item 5 (D27) read snapshot r4 and is corrected. The packet uses `startRefusedAt` in every current place (sections 6.6, 6.10, 8, 15 and 17). Items 91 and D27 are history with a pointer to this item; history notes may say that fix round 4 named the field `startRefusedFor`. The packet auditor checks the name against the fix round 6 core.
98. **RA with no effect: end codes and reading (R26 items 3 and 4; audit-r7 P3-1; second-r7 P3-1 and P3-2; coder item 1).** With the effect flag false at a trigger in RA no command is sent, and, outside cases 4, 5 and 9, which keep their own codes (section 8 case 7; ruling R27 item 2), the end codes are: a non-budget trigger at the start step `RA_API_NOT_READY`; a non-budget trigger at the start step of the market branch `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY`; any other budget or progress trigger `RA_BUDGET_STOP`; a termination the termination code. These codes, with `startSent` false and no `stopProved`, mean the host is as the gate found it. The RA receipt does not record the effect flag, so Root reads a no-command RA end from the trigger, `startSent` false, the absence of `stopProved` and a progress record with no archive or reload line. A gate that read class FAILED, with the reset-failed grant, can end `RA_API_NOT_READY`, or `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` in the market branch, with one benign reset-failed command sent and `startSent` false (ruling R27 item 3, P3-1). Such an end needs no unwind; the next step is Root's receipt review and then a fresh RA gate under its own GO. Section 8 (case 7 and both outcome tables).
99. **G1 command sentence (R26 item 5; D30; coder open item 3).** A failed API with a queued job is not stopped: the stop poll continues within its bound, sends no further command, and the end is not proved. A class check treats a failed API with a queued job like a running API and sends it the one stop of that step; when that stop is not proved, the end is `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` in A2 and `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` in RA, both with `stopProved` false (audit r10 against the fix round 6 core; ruling R27 item 3, P3-3). Under G1, `apiAtEnd` can read `RUNNING` after a failed unit with a queued job. The packet auditor confirms the end codes from the fix round 6 core and records them. Sections 6.6, 8 and 18 (D30).
100. **D31 sentences (R26 item 6; D31).** Two derived sentences stand, for the packet auditor to confirm against the fix round 6 core: when the market branch sent its stop, `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` carry the same value; with no branch stop, a failed class read with an empty job queue after an earlier stop gives `stopProved` true. If the core differs, the text follows the core when the core is the safer reading, and the auditor reports it. Section 18 (D31).
101. **timingProof names (R26 item 7; audit-r7 P3-3).** The text says that `timingProof` reports `a2InRun` 1129 and the A2 gap sum 309. No sum changes. Section 11.1.
102. **Section 12 residual; items not taken into the packet (R26 item 7; audit-r7 P3-2, P3-5; second-r7 P3-4).** Section 12 records "tool comments predate G1; behaviour follows G1" as a known cosmetic item, corrected in the next code round if one comes. The make-copied note N19 is deferred to the next rebuild round, and the coder's name-only search over the parent evidence folder is a Root process incident with no secret exposure; neither adds packet text.
103. **Contract sentence, status lines and accepted seals (R26 items 1 and 8; D29).** The contract sentence reads "Revision 6 with rulings R7 to R26 is the build contract until revision 7 is installed; revision 7 is the contract after installation" in the title, items 24 and 30, the Root rulings list (item 20) and section 15, and the section 15 label and the section 18 heading read "R12 to R26". The status lines (already in place) read "fix rounds 5 and 6 (rulings R24 and R25), accepted by ruling R26". Section 6.1 item 2, section 15, section 17 Next and section 18 item 1 name the accepted seals: A2RA SHA256SUMS c1ed1a24 (snapshot r6), ADP lane b4168240 (snapshot r5), A1 914e643d (snapshot r9). The constants step is mechanical: it changes only the four packet constants, then regenerates and re-seals, and needs no new review round. The fixed sentences S0 to S4 are unchanged (4 places each). This pass builds, installs, tests and accepts nothing.
104. **Final audit r10 text edits (R27; audit-r10 P2-1 and P3-1 to P3-6).** Root edited draft 11 into draft 12: the G2 end codes keep cases 4, 5 and 9 apart and name the start step and the market-branch start step (section 8 case 7; item 98); the reset-failed exception names both codes; the D30 pointer reads item 99 and sections 6.6, 8 and 18, and D30 and D31 are confirmed by audit r10; a class check sends a failed API with a queued job the one stop of that step (item 99; section 6.6); the A2 end in row L0-n with a failed API records it in `apiAtEnd` (section 6.6, prose and table; corrected by item 105); no budget stop occurs at the record directory or the progress file (section 8 cases 5 and 7, item 93 and section 15), and an argument-shape refusal of a start records `startSent` true (case 7); section 11.1 names the normal plus put-back plus stop path. No code, sum, bound, limit or wait changed; the contract sentence stays rulings R7 to R26.
105. **Packet audit r11 text edits (R28; audit-r11 P2-1 and P3-1 to P3-5).** Root edited draft 12 into draft 13: the A2 end in row L0-n with a failed API names both core paths, no `apiFailed` key when the first stop command ended failed and `apiFailed` true when an unwind read or an unwind stop found the API failed after a proved first stop (section 6.6, prose and table); the second section 11.1 line names the normal plus put-back plus stop path; the D30 and D31 intro reads confirmed by audit r10; the RA outcome table rows name the start step and the start step of the market branch; the case 5 exclusion in case 7 names a budget stop, a failed progress write or an archive failure before the rename; item 104 is corrected. No code, sum, bound, limit or wait changed; the contract sentence stays rulings R7 to R26.
106. **Packet audit r12 text edits (R29; audit-r12 P2-1 and P3-1).** Root edited draft 13 into draft 14: in section 6.6 (prose and table) the A2 end in row L0-n with a failed API says that nothing is renamed, no reload runs and no start is sent, and that the unwind sends no stop except one stop when its class read, after the proved first stop, finds the API not stopped; the no-`apiFailed` case names kind STOP_FAILED and a failed stop command whose snapshot shows the API failed with no job; the sentence on a second stop reads "to one API process". The GO-A2 template, item 79 and the D19 line describe only an API found failed at the unwind, where no stop is sent, and stay. ## Revision 6 changes (history)

Basis: revision 5 (sha256 `0669e34d`), the changed-lines check by the architecture auditor (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 0 P2, 6 P3), the second opinion by Opus 5.5 / high as the recorded Fable substitute (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 0 P2, 9 P3) and Root ruling R6, which adopts every P3 finding in this final text revision so that the A2, A2M and RA tool is built against one text. Finding IDs below are those of the two revision 5 reports, prefixed "A-" for the changed-lines check and "S-" for the second opinion. Each change names its finding IDs and the ruling item. Revision 5 text that no item changes is kept. The five fixed sentences S0 (outcomes), S1 (next RA), S2 (exclusion), S3 (terminal RA and the accepted RA receipt) and S4 (the A2 receipt that reopens the chain) stay word for word equal in section 8, section 9, the owner summary (items 5 and 6) and GO-RA; items 2, 4 and 5 change S0, S1, S2 and S3, and S4 is unchanged.

1. **Author readings confirmed (R6 item 1).** The revision 5 author readings (a) to (e) are confirmed. Revision 4 item (e) is ruled: the AR reference for the staging API configuration digest is the c1 `pairConfigDigests` value for the staging API, in the same form as the trading worker's, not the P5a record. Sections 6.12, 17 and 18.
2. **Case 5 runs the immediate checks (A-P3-1; R6 item 2).** In rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n the case 5 restart is on the post-P5a configuration, so RA runs the immediate checks after it and ends `RA_API_READY` or `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, like the main path; the tool then refuses a next RA. S0 now reads "without the API proved ready on `ba1a433` by RA's immediate checks". Section 8 (case 5, case 10, the outcome table, S0), section 9, the owner summary, GO-RA and section 15.
3. **Code precedence (A-P3-2, S-P3-6; R6 item 3).** A case keeps its code and records "API failed"; `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` is used only for a stop not proved within 100 seconds. Case 7 in row LL runs the case 1 put-back and reload with no start, keeps `RA_BUDGET_STOP` and ends LL-n or LL-r. Row LP with the rename on disk followed by a throw ends `RA_API_STOPPED` with end row LQ-n. A failed progress write of the A2 unwind ends `A2_UNWIND_PROGRESS_FAILED` with end row LP-n. Sections 6.6 and 8 (the case preamble, cases 4, 5 and 7, the outcome table), GO-A2, GO-RA and section 15.
4. **Production identity, one rule (A-P3-4, S-P3-4; R6 item 4).** A production-identity difference read at any RA step after the gate means RA starts nothing from that point and ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`; a difference at the gate ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` before any effect. The next RA is admitted only with Root's recorded review in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against. Root may accept a `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` after its review. S2 and S3 change. Sections 3, 8 (the gate, cases 1 and 9, the outcome table, the tool refusal), 9 and 12, the owner summary, GO-RA and section 15.
5. **Terminals (A-P3-3, S-P3-5; R6 item 5).** Sections 8 and 12 name every state with no automatic next step and its path: a terminal RA receipt that Root does not accept (a Root ruling or a packet revision); `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (a packet revision); the staging API stuck transitioning past the settle wait (a Root ruling); a repeated case 4 in an -n row with no progress (a Root ruling); a lost A2 receipt with an unreadable progress record (a packet revision). A terminated RA attempt never counts as "API ready"; the next RA is admitted after the 1335-second wait. S1 and S2 change. Sections 8, 9 and 12, the owner summary, GO-RA and section 15.
6. **Wording (A-P3-5; R6 item 6).** GO-RA: "market re-check after the file action (and the reload, if one ran)"; owner summary: "changes before the API start"; the GO-RA named cases add "or the restart on the configuration it found". Owner summary item 5 and GO-RA.
7. **Progress lines (S-P3-1, A-P3-6; R6 item 7).** An fsynced `<step> ATTEMPTED` progress line with the expected file form and digest precedes every rename, archive, put-back and reload, in A2 and RA; `<step> DONE` follows. The A2 and RA receipts carry the pre-rename pending digest line; rows LU (pending) and LQ match it from the reviewed receipt, or from the progress record when the receipt was lost. Sections 6.4, 6.6, 6.10 and 8, GO-A2, GO-RA and section 15.
8. **L0 and LP recognition (S-P3-7; R6 item 8).** Row L0 only when no listed A2 or RA record shows a write, rename or archive of the re-pin layer; row LP only when no listed record shows a rename; otherwise RA refuses before any effect and Root rules. Section 8, GO-RA and section 15.
9. **A2 end-outcome table (S-P3-9; R6 item 9).** Section 6.6 gets a table with the code, end row, API state and next step of every A2 end (new codes `A2_PASSED`, `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK`, `A2_UNWOUND`, `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` and `A2_UNWIND_PROGRESS_FAILED` beside the existing ones); the A2 receipt records its end row. Sections 6.6 and 6.10, GO-A2 and section 15.
10. **Heartbeat by tick time (S-P3-2; R6 item 10, replaces ruling R5 item 8).** A row is "own" when its tick is at or after the classified trading worker's main-process start minus one second; more than one fresh own row refuses; a fresh non-own row counts as a previous process's row only when its tick is before that start or, with no running trading worker, its tick does not advance across two reads one heartbeat interval apart; otherwise RA refuses before any effect. "No Live-mode row" stays in every class, and the R5a rule of exactly one own row is kept for a running trading worker (no guard lowered). Section 8, GO-RA and section 15.
11. **Crash-looping staging API (S-P3-3; R6 item 11).** The staging API is exempt from the settle-wait refusal: RA classifies it as restarting and stops it, and its own start, stop or restart job does not count as a pending job. The journal line cap applies only to the R5a 60-second read; the read since A2 is recorded only. Section 8, GO-RA and section 15.
12. **Numbers (S-P3-8; R6 item 12).** Section 11.1 adds the RA worst path with the 100-second stop after a failed unwind start (1135 ≤ 1140) and the RA from-stop reserve of 474 seconds (202 + 172 + 100), below which RA stops before any effect; the build asserts both. Sections 8 (Bounds) and 11.1.
13. **Review of revision 6 (R6, "Review of r6").** The architecture auditor checks that each item is applied, that S0 to S4 still agree word for word in their four places, and that no other text changed. Then Root installs revision 6 under `docs/` and takes `PACKET_SHA` from the LF Git blob. Sections 15, 17 and 18.

## Revision 5 changes (history)

Basis: revision 4 (sha256 `f0e4272c`), the changed-lines check by the architecture auditor (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 1 P2, 6 P3), the second opinion by Opus 5.5 / high as the recorded Fable substitute (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 2 P2, 6 P3) and Root ruling R5. Both reviewers found the lost-receipt inventory defect independently. Finding IDs below are those of the two revision 4 reports, prefixed "A-" for the changed-lines check and "S-" for the second opinion; the revision 4 note below uses the same prefixes for the two revision 3 reports. Each change names its finding IDs and the ruling item. Revision 4 text that no item changes is kept.

1. **Lost-receipt inventory (A-P2-1, S-P2-2; R5 item 1, amends R4 item 4).** Before its first effect on the staging API, A2 writes the gate inventory (the count and the sorted SHA-256 list of every drop-in directory file other than the live drop-in list and the derived-name forms; no names) as a gate line of its host progress record, and the A2 receipt carries the same. Under the lost-receipt clause RA reads that line, never the P5a a01 record. With no such line, A2 had no effect and no layer exists, and RA refuses before any effect. Sections 6.2, 6.4, 8, 11.1, 15 and 18, GO-A2 and GO-RA. New fakes: RA after a lost A2 receipt, admitted from the progress record, and refused on a count or digest difference.
2. **Exclusion by end state (S-P2-1, S-P3-3; R5 item 2).** `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` is used only for case 6 (an archived layer cannot be put back). The RD-32 state gets its own code, `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, defined once as any RA end with a production-identity difference. Case 4 adds the production-identity check after the reconciling reload (nothing started; with a difference, the RD-32 state). Every other unwind failure gets a named code and end row: `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`, `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, `RA_API_NOT_READY`, `RA_API_STOPPED`, `RA_BUDGET_STOP` and `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`, beside `RA_UNWOUND` and the market codes. The next RA is admitted when the files form a recognised row, the prior records show the needed facts and production equals the baseline. Section 8 gets a table of RA end outcomes and five fixed sentences (outcomes, next RA, exclusion, terminal RA and the accepted RA receipt, the A2 receipt that reopens the chain), used word for word in sections 8 and 9, the owner summary and GO-RA. Sections 3, 8, 9, 12 and 15, the owner summary and GO-RA. New fakes: each sub-outcome followed by an admitted next RA, a case 6 prior that refuses, and LU-r with a production difference after the reconciling reload.
3. **Terminal RA and the accepted RA receipt (A-P3-2, S-P3-5, S-P3-6; R5 item 3; revision 4 author item (f)).** An RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433` (`RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`, or the newly named immediate-check failure `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`) is terminal for the RA family; no next RA follows it, and the tool refuses one. An RA receipt is the accepted RA receipt (for the RX tripwire and the return of R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC) when its end row is L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n, the post-P5a configuration is loaded, production is unchanged and the API is ready; a market or check stop needs Root review first. A Root-reviewed A2 receipt with end row L0-n or LU-n and no pending file, with the API ready on `ba1a433`, the post-P5a configuration loaded and production unchanged (the fix of the second opinion), also reopens the chain, with no RA. The A2 rows of section 9 say that a next A2 needs a packet revision or Root ruling. After `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, RA is admissible only with a recognised row and production at the baseline; otherwise Root rules. Rulings item 7, sections 8, 9, 12 and 15, the owner summary and GO-RA.
4. **Need-reload facts (A-P3-1; R5 item 4).** Source: the Root-reviewed receipt, or, when it was lost, the host progress record. A rename or archive labelled `ATTEMPTED` or `DONE` counts as shown when the disk holds the file in the matching form with the expected digest; a reload counts as completed only when labelled `DONE`. Section 8, GO-RA and section 15.
5. **Rows (S-P3-1, S-P3-2, S-P3-4; R5 item 5).** Row LU requires the A2 receipt or progress record to show the unwind archive; an archive of the derived name that no listed A2 or prior-RA record explains refuses; the RA manifest binds the last prior RA attempt. The pending-file digest is recorded in the progress record before its rename (the A2 unwind and RA row LP); rows LU (pending) and LQ match that pre-rename line. Case table: `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` ends in any row; case 8 is retitled "market change at the post-file-action re-check"; the archived state is decided from the files on disk (a rename on disk followed by a throw is case 1; no rename on disk is case 5). Sections 6.6 and 8, GO-A2, GO-RA and section 15.
6. **Environment wording (A-P3-3; R5 item 6).** "Every unit property read other than the host-side environment-source read of the staging API's and the trading worker's own environment property (digest only)"; the build assertion is scoped to the class, process, configuration and hooks read lists, and a second assertion states that the environment property is read only in the host-side process. RA has the same exception for the staging API's own property only. Sections 6.2, 8 and 15, GO-A2 and GO-RA.
7. **A1 collector edges (A-P3-4; R5 item 7).** The section 6.2 sentence (a timer-started collector run is not transitioning) is copied into section 5.1 and GO-A1. Only end-reading differences of the staging API, the trading worker and the stream go to GO-AR; collector runs and timer trigger changes are recorded; a research worker no longer stopped, an inactive timer or a failed collector service at the end is a Root review item, not an AR. Sections 3, 5.1, 6.3, 12 and 15 and GO-A1.
8. **Heartbeat "other" row (A-P3-5; R5 item 8).** An "other" row is one whose process identity is neither the trading worker's classified main process nor, at the immediate checks, its current main process. A still-fresh row of a previous process that no longer exists is recorded, not refused. Section 8, GO-RA and section 15 (new fakes).
9. **Wording (A-P3-6; R5 item 9; one minor point of the second opinion).** The GO-RA and owner-summary exclusion words follow item 2 (A-P3-6(a)). Section 6.12 is retitled "AR reference reading (after a drift, at the A1 end reading, or once before GO-A2)" (A-P3-6(b)). The section 6.2 "every other file" set is defined relative to the live drop-in list and the derived-name forms, the same set as the compared file set of section 8 and the A2 inventory (A-P3-6(c)). The RA reset-failed clause is scoped to the main start, never an unwind start (second opinion, "Effects not named in GO-RA"). Sections 6.2, 6.12 and 8, the owner summary and GO-RA.
10. **Numbers (consequence of items 1 and 5; no finding).** No bound, wait or budget changes; section 11.1 states why.
11. **Revision 4 author items (R5: closed by these items).** (a) and (b) by item 1; (c) and (d) were agreed by both reviewers and are kept; (f) by item 3. (e) No item changes the text: the AR acceptance keeps "the post-P5a record" for the staging API, and section 18 names the confirmation that stays with Root.
12. **Review of revision 5 (R5, "Review of r5").** The architecture auditor checks the changed lines and the consistency of section 8, section 9, the owner summary and GO-RA; the second-opinion reviewer repeats the RA end-to-end enumeration. If only P3 findings remain, Root may accept revision 5 and carry them into the A2, A2M and RA tool build specification as named residuals. Sections 15, 17 and 18.

## Revision 4 changes (history)

Basis: revision 3 (sha256 `0295ead3`), the narrow re-check by the architecture auditor (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 1 P2, 9 P3), the second opinion by Opus 5.5 / high as the recorded Fable substitute (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 2 P2, 7 P3) and Root ruling R4. Both reviewers found the rollback-family defect independently. Finding IDs below are those of the two revision 3 reports, prefixed "A-" for the re-check and "S-" for the second opinion; the revision 3 note below uses the same prefixes for the two revision 2 reports. Each change names its finding IDs and the ruling item. Revision 3 text that no item changes is kept.

1. **Rollback-family rule widened (A-P2-1, S-P2-1; R4 item 1).** After any RA attempt whose outcome ends in a recognised row of the layer-state table with no production-identity difference (the API running, stopped or failed; the re-pin layer live after a put-back, archived, or never written), Root may issue the next RA job ID under a new GO-RA that lists the prior attempts, with the prior-attempt facts and, when a receipt was lost, the 1335-second wait. Only `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and the RD-32 state are excluded. Sections 3, 8, 9, 11.1, 12 and 15, the owner summary and GO-RA. New fakes: a next RA after case 1 with the API stopped, after case 2, after case 5 and after case 7, and a refused next attempt after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED`.
2. **Row LQ (S-P2-2, A-P3-1; R4 item 2).** New rows LQ-n and LQ-r: a pending file archived by an earlier RA attempt, with the archive digest equal to the one that attempt recorded; no live layer and no pending file; no file change; it counts as never written for the case table. Rows LQ and LR are told apart by the prior attempt's record of its own row, because a complete pending file has the layer digest. Named in the rollback-family rule, GO-RA and the fakes. Sections 8 and 15 and GO-RA.
3. **Reload-pending rows (A-P3-3, S-P3-1; R4 item 3).** An "-r" row is admitted only when the A2 progress record or a prior RA progress record shows a rename or archive of the live re-pin layer without a completed reload after it (LL-r, LU-r, LR-r). L0-r, LP-r and LQ-r, and any "-r" row without such a record, refuse before any effect; section 12 names the external case as a Root decision. Sections 8, 12 and 15 and GO-RA.
4. **Compared file set (A-P3-2; R4 item 4).** The layer-state rows compare the live drop-in list and the pending and archive forms of the re-pin layer's derived name; every other file of the drop-in directory is compared by count and digest with the A2 gate record (or the P5a a01 record under the lost-receipt clause). The A2 gate therefore records that count and those digests. Sections 6.2, 8 and 15, GO-A2 and GO-RA.
5. **Heartbeat (A-P3-4, S-P3-2; R4 item 5).** "No other fresh row" and "no Live-mode row" stay in every class; the RA gate refuses on a fresh row from a process other than the classified trading worker; the immediate checks require the trading worker's heartbeat only when it was fresh at the gate and the process is the same, and otherwise record it. One clause states the relaxation of the R5a `PRE_HEARTBEAT_STALE` refusal. Sections 8 and 15 and GO-RA.
6. **Environment properties (A-P3-5; R4 item 6).** AR, A2 and RA read the three market units, the staging API and the trading worker with the environment properties removed (the "no environment" list form of the collector start build); the build asserts it. The D4 environment-source reads of A2 and RA stay as they were. Sections 6.2, 6.12, 8 and 15, GO-A2, GO-RA and GO-AR.
7. **A1 references and edges (S-P3-3, A-P3-6; R4 item 7).** The A1 reference values are the c1 export facts that c6 recorded as unchanged; Root copies them into the private A1 manifest and the build asserts the form. AR may also follow a difference found at A1's end reading, and Root may accept the A1 staged tree with an AR record as the A2 reference. A transitioning collector service, timer or research worker at the A1 gate is a pre-effect stop with a contingency A1 ID, not a drift. Sections 3, 5.1, 6.3, 6.12, 12, 15 and 17, the owner summary, GO-A1 and GO-AR.
8. **Trading worker configuration (S-P3-4; R4 item 8).** Section 6.2 adds the trading worker's configuration and drop-in list digest check against the c1 `pairConfigDigests` and `pairDropInDigests` values; the AR acceptance criterion uses the same words. Sections 6.2, 6.12 and 15 and GO-A2.
9. **A2 unwind of a pending file (S-P3-5; R4 item 9).** The A2 unwind archives a pending file by no-clobber rename, records its digest and deletes nothing. Section 6.6 and GO-A2.
10. **Drift convergence (A-P3-7, S-P3-7(c); R4 item 10).** The A2, A2M and RA tool is accepted before GO-A1 is requested, so the window between A1 and A2 is short. One AR without a drift may run right before the GO-A2 request as the A2 reference; GO-AR and GO-A2 may come in one owner message, and each is admitted on its own. Root records the observed stream restart rate in the GO-A2 request. Rulings item 1, sections 3, 6.1, 6.3, 6.12, 11, 12, 15 and 17, the owner summary, GO-A2 and GO-AR.
11. **Wording (A-P3-9, A-P3-8, S-P3-6, S-P3-7(a), S-P3-7(b); R4 item 11).** Section 9 RA row: "A2 again only under a packet revision or Root ruling that defines the next A2 job ID" (A-P3-9(a), S-P3-6). GO-RA and section 8: "if it archived the live re-pin layer or the staging API needs a reload" (A-P3-9(b), S-P3-7(a)). A market start after RM or RC before A2 needs its own packet or ruling, and A2 waits for it (A-P3-8, S-P3-7(b); sections 6.3 and 12, owner summary item 6).
12. **Revision 3 open items adopted (R4 item 12).** The settle wait of 30 seconds (A2, AR, RA); the AR limits (P4 CHECK limits, host bound 240 seconds); the 1335-second wait from the A2 start before RA after a lost A2 receipt; the GO-A1 reference text "the c6 record (the c1 export facts) or the latest Root-accepted AR record"; A2M returns the API process digest and Root compares it. Sections 14 and 18.
13. **Numbers (consequence of items 1 to 4 and 9; no finding).** No bound, wait or budget changes; section 11.1 states why.
14. **Review of revision 4 (R4, "Review of r4").** The architecture auditor checks the changed lines and their consistency with the rest of the packet; the second-opinion reviewer checks the RA layer-state table and the rollback-family rule end to end. Then Root installs the packet under `docs/`. Sections 15, 17 and 18.

## Revision 3 changes (history)

Basis: revision 2 (sha256 `88e16f57`), the narrow re-check by the architecture auditor (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 3 P2, 6 P3; finding IDs prefixed "A-" below), the second opinion by Opus 5.5 / high as the recorded Fable substitute (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 0 P1, 5 P2, 9 P3; prefixed "S-") and Root ruling R3. Each change names its finding IDs and the ruling item.

1. **Reference chain through A1 (A-P2-2, S-P2-5, S-P3-2; R3 item 1).** A1 now exports the main-process digest and restart count of the staging API, the trading worker and the market stream in the form of the collector start c1 export (copied by value from the accepted collector start build). The A2 gate compares process continuity with the accepted A1 record, not with c6. A1 compares with the c6 record or the latest Root-accepted AR record. At the A1 gate the fallback collector service follows the A2 rule (inactive with result success, or in a recorded timer-started run). Sections 5.1, 6.2, 6.3, GO-A1 and GO-A2.
2. **Read-only reference job AR with GO-AR (A-P2-2, S-P2-5; R3 item 1).** New section 6.12: its own family, the P4 CHECK limits, the c6-form gate reads, no control verb. After a process-identity drift found at an A1 or A2 gate, Root requests GO-AR; the next attempt of the stopped job compares with the latest Root-accepted A1 or AR record, and its manifest names which. The A1 and A2 GO lines name the alternative reference, so their wording does not change after a drift. The owner summary states the cost of a drift. The c6-form path through the retired collector families is removed. Sections 3, 6.3, 6.12, 10, 11, 11.1, 12, 14 (GO-AR), 15 and the owner summary.
3. **RA layer-state table (S-P2-1, A-P2-1 scope; R3 item 2).** Section 8 gets an explicit table of re-pin layer states (none, pending only, live, archived by the A2 unwind, archived by an earlier RA attempt), each with and without a pending reload of the staging API. In a recognised need-reload row RA's one reload is the reconciling reload, and only the staging API is exempt from the need-reload gate. A pending file is archived by no-clobber rename, never deleted, and GO-RA names it. Sections 8, 15, 16, the owner summary and GO-RA.
4. **Lost A2 receipt (S-P2-2; R3 item 2).** RA without a reviewed A2 receipt works as R5a does without a P5a receipt: it reads the A2 host progress record, takes the post-P5a drop-in list and environment digest from the P5a a01 record and the layer digest from the A2 manifest, and classifies from the files on disk. Section 8 and GO-RA.
5. **Rollback-family rule (S-P2-3; R3 item 2).** After an RA attempt that ended with the API stopped and the re-pin layer archived, Root may issue the next RA job ID under a new GO-RA, after the lost-receipt wait when that receipt was lost. Sections 3, 8, 9 and GO-RA.
6. **Trading-worker heartbeat in RA (A-P2-1; R3 item 2).** The RA gate records the heartbeat rows and their freshness and refuses only on an unreadable queue or a Live-mode row, never on a stale heartbeat when the trading worker is classified inactive or failed. The RA immediate checks require the heartbeat only when the trading worker was running at the gate and is still the same process. Section 8, GO-RA and section 15 fakes.
7. **Reference digests (S-P2-4, S-P3-1; R3 item 3).** The trading worker's environment digest reference is the `envDigests.TRADING` value of the accepted P4 x4 a02 receipt, as compared at the P5a a01 gate. The stream configuration digest reference is the value in the P4M binding (history: the P4M binding holds no digest field; since draft 7 the source is the P4M m1 a03 manifest's market configuration digest of the stream, section 6.13 and ruling R21 item 6). Both digest forms are copied by value, and the build asserts them against fakes built from the recorded forms. Sections 6.2, 6.3 and GO-A2.
8. **N1 method version 2 (A-P2-3; R3 item 4).** The Paper write table set W is defined by name only; the creation-site condition is removed. Sections 2.2 and 4.
9. **Re-map case 4 by effect (A-P3-6, S-P3-8; R3 item 5).** Case 4 is keyed on the effect (a request range that newly computes on staging). The D pin now takes cases 4, 5 and 7. A0 records which ranges newly compute under the pin and Root's memory estimate. With no API memory limit the bound is the host available-memory floor. Sections 2.4, 4, 6.2 and 12.
10. **Wording and outcome fixes (R3 item 6).** GO-A2 opens its unwind with "On most failures" (A-P3-1). The GO-RA unwind trigger uses the section 8 words (A-P3-2). The edge after `A2_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`: Root asks the owner first, and A2M takes the immediate-check process as its reference (A-P3-3, S-P3-4). The two unnamed outcomes get names: `A2_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` and `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY`, and `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` (S-P3-5). The browser timing record holds only range labels, status codes and durations (S-P3-6). The importer list drops `qr2-response.js` (A-P3-4). The failed-market sentence of section 6.3 is corrected (A-P3-5). GO-A1 names the drop-in digests as an environment-source read under ruling D4 (S-P3-3). The owner summary states that each reload is host-wide and shared with production, that D makes Month, Year and All Time compute on the shared host for the first time, and that RX needs a fresh job ID and manifest while retired families stay retired (S-P3-7).
11. **Settle wait (S-P3-9; R3 item 6).** A2, AR and RA give a unit in automatic restart one bounded read-only settle wait of up to 30 seconds before they classify or refuse. It is counted in the step budgets of section 11.1 (A2 host worst path 1099 to 1129 seconds, RA 1005 to 1035 seconds, both within 1140). Sections 6.2, 6.3, 6.12, 8, 11.1 and the GO lines.
12. **Review panel (R3).** Revision 3 gets a narrow re-check by the architecture auditor and by the second-opinion reviewer (same roles as for revision 2), closing every revision 2 finding. Section 15 and 18.

Basis: revision 1 (sha256 `9933cfa6`), its independent review (GO_WITH_CONDITIONS: 1 P1, 7 P2, 10 P3) and Root ruling R2. Each change names its finding or open-item ID.

The two-way hooks-and-links rule stays. The timer window applies only when the timer is active, and a running collector service stays out of the reload. The post-reload market re-check compares with RA's own gate reading. GO-RC or GO-RM before GO-RA is admissible, and RA gives the market units and the trading worker no command in every class. Sections 6.4, 8, 9, 13 (item B), 14 (GO-RA) and 15 (tester fakes).
2. **P2-1 and O-1.** The pin is the integrated, reviewed and CI-green commit that carries F, the hint changes of ruling L2 and D. The `30cd728` fallback stays only as the rule of section 2.1 if D fails review. Root writes the A0 record at pin time and pins its sha in the A1, A2 and RA manifests. Sections 2.1, 2.2, 3, 4 and 14.
