5. **P2-4 and O-3.** A separate read-only memory job A2M under GO-A2 (own job ID, manifest, record directory and here-document file, no control verb) runs after the owner's browser steps. It returns the API resident memory, the host's available memory and whether the QR-2 worker thread exists. The owner's steps include a computing request (Week for the Bot with fills), and Root records the status code beside each timing. GO-A2 names A2M as a full clause. Sections 3, 6.9, 6.11, 11 and 14.
6. **P2-5.** GO-A1 and GO-RA name the shell's temporary here-document file. GO-A1 reads "the staging API drop-in names and the SHA-256 of each drop-in file inside the host-side process, never returning a name or content". Sections 5.2 and 14.
7. **P2-6.** Owner summary item 5 states the A2 failure outcomes as GO-A2 does.
8. **P2-7 and O-11.** The timer settings are cited from the c1 gate of the accepted collector start a01 record, and classes and process continuity from its c6 record. The compared process and restart values sit in the private receipt chain from P4M a03 to c6 in ignored local records; the packet names that chain, never its values. Production identity is `b5789332` as re-read at c6. Sections 5.1, 6.2, 6.3, 14 and 18.
9. **P3-1.** Re-map case 7 added ("QR-2 read-path source change, N4 holds"); cases 5 to 7 combine; A0 item 6 reads "case 5, 6 or 7". The D pin takes cases 5 and 7. Sections 2.4 and 4.
10. **P3-2.** The recorded timer cadence (about 300 seconds), accuracy, randomized delay and persistence are in section 6.3. W now includes the pre-write check and wait and the post-reload market re-check, and the author check asserts W plus accuracy plus delay plus margin below the period. The section 12 sentence on a catch-up run with persistence on is replaced by the recorded value, which the gate enforces.
11. **P3-3 and O-6.** All numbers are computed in section 11.1 with the arithmetic. The added steps exceed the P5a 280-second gap bound, so the A2 gap bound is raised to 310 seconds; RA's added steps exceed the R5a 345-second bound, so the RA gap bound is raised to 380 seconds. No wait is shortened. Host bounds: A1 300 seconds, A2 1200 seconds, A2M 240 seconds, RA 1200 seconds. Timer-window wait 420 seconds. The RA lost-receipt wait rises from 1035 to 1335 seconds because the RA host-process bound (1230 seconds) now exceeds 1035. Sections 3, 6.3, 8, 11, 11.1, summary item 3 and 14.
12. **P3-4.** The hooks-and-links property lists are named (PartOf added). Section 6.3 and GO-A2.
13. **P3-5.** A2 keeps the P5a journal reads (line counts, refusal-line and line-cap rules). Section 6.2.
14. **P3-6.** The in-process environment clause covers the trading worker's environment property (held in the host-side process, digest only). Section 6.2 and GO-A2.
15. **P3-7.** GO-RA names checklist item E.
16. **P3-8.** Optional RX tripwire adopted: while the re-pin layer is live, Root writes a consumed marker for the next RX job ID; a fresh RX job ID only after an accepted RA receipt. It does not replace the later RX gate hardening. Rulings item 7, sections 9 and 10.

The local rehearsal before GO-A2 covers C7 in English and Thai and the generic 422 hint. Ruling L1 Q6 applies. Summary items 1, 2 and 7, rulings item 10, sections 4, 6.9, 13 (item D), 14 and 15.

23. **O-8.** Review panel for revision 2: a narrow re-check by the architecture auditor and a second opinion by Opus 5.5 / high as the recorded Fable substitute. Section 15.
24. **O-10.** The restore source is the P4 x4 a02 pre-switch backup of 2026-10-26T04:49:30Z; Root states its age in the GO-A2 request. Section 9 and GO-A2.
25. **O-12.** RA keeps the one reload outside a collector run as a named adaptation, applied only when the timer is active. Section 8 and GO-RA.

> **This packet proposes two steps with effects on the owner's staging host, one read-only memory job, one read-only reference job (after a drift, or once as the A2 reference right before GO-A2) and one rollback.** Each runs only after a **new explicit owner GO that names S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1, the phase (A1, A2 with its memory job A2M, AR for a new reference, or RA for the rollback) and Staging QL-P2 Prototype**, the reviewed tool for that phase, and Root admission. Approval of this document, of earlier S6 packets or of any earlier GO does not authorize it. No tool, manifest, marker, job ID or receipt for this packet exists yet. Nothing in this packet has contacted the host.

Prepared on 28 October 2026 (Bangkok) for the Claude root by an architecture auditor (Opus 5.5 / high) under Root rulings R1 to R6, from the API-only design, the judge report, the named sections of the pair design, the revision 1 review, the two revision 2 reviews, the two revision 3 reviews, the two revision 4 reviews and the two revision 5 reviews. It extends the [main packet](S6_STAGING_PROTOTYPE_DEPLOY_PACKET_2026-10-24.md) (sha256 `e8a18a90`), the [follow-up packet](S6_STAGING_PROTOTYPE_FOLLOWUP_PACKET_2026-10-26.md) (sha256 `ebc707c8`) and the [collector restart packet](S6_STAGING_COLLECTOR_START_PACKET_2026-10-27.md) (sha256 `a296989e`), and never edits them.

## Owner summary

1. **Why.** Staging answers a long per-Bot performance range with HTTP 503 `QR2_REPORT_FAILED` today. Commit `281110e` makes that refusal a 422 `QR2_RANGE_ADMISSION_REFUSED` with the limit, and commit `30cd728` makes the panel explain it in English and Thai. You approved the bar-coverage degrade (decision OD-S5-D, 28 October 2026), so the pin also carries it (D): a report whose range is over a bar-coverage budget comes back with bar coverage marked "not checked" (class C7) instead of a refusal. The pin is commit `33e5a8c`, which Root set on 28 October 2026 after the A0 record. None of this is on staging yet.
2. **What changes for you.** Only the staging API moves to the new pin. The performance panel stays on. After the switch, a Week report shows bar coverage complete, and a Month or All Time report returns, with bar coverage "not checked" where the range is over a coverage budget, in English and Thai. The remaining 422 refusals show the generic hint. With D, Month, Year and All Time financial reports, and a range within 30 days that stops at a data-time limit of the bar-coverage check, compute on the shared host for the first time; the financial read limits and the QR-2 worker bounds still bound them.
3. **The API gap.** The staging API stops once and starts on the new tree. Expected about 4 to 13 seconds (P5a measured 4.4 seconds), bound 310 seconds for A2 and 380 seconds for RA. Staging alerts and webhooks are not served during the gap.
4. **What stays running.** The trading worker keeps its process and stays on `ba1a433`. The market stream and the fallback collector service and timer keep running and get no command. The research worker stays stopped. Production is out of scope and is identity-checked before and after. Each manager reload is host-wide and shared with the production units.
5. **What each GO allows.**
   - **GO-A1:** copy one new API tree onto the host and check it. Nothing starts, stops or reloads. It also tells Root, as a yes or no, whether the new layer has room in the drop-in order, and it records the process references that A2 compares.
   - **GO-A2:** the switch. Stop the staging API, add one drop-in layer that points it at the new tree, one manager reload (host-wide, shared with the production units), start the API, check it at once and again after about 5 minutes. On most failures before the API is ready the tool returns the API to `ba1a433` by itself. In named cases it leaves the API stopped and Root reports. If a market unit changes at the reload, it starts the API on the new pin and stops for your decision; if the API then does not become ready, it leaves the API stopped and stops for your decision. If a market unit changes at a later check, it leaves the API running on the new pin and stops for your decision. After your browser steps, a separate read-only job A2M under the same GO reads the API's resident memory, the host's available memory and whether the QR-2 worker thread exists.
   - **GO-AR (after a drift, or once before GO-A2):** a read-only reading of the unit processes, with no command to any unit. If the market stream, the staging API or the trading worker restarts between the reference reading and A1 or A2, that job stops before any effect and its GO is spent. Root then asks you for one extra read-only GO (GO-AR) to read a new reference, and for a new GO for the next attempt of the stopped job. If such a restart shows only at the end of A1, after the tree is staged, Root asks you for GO-AR and may then accept the staged tree with the new reference, with no new GO-A1. Root may also ask for one GO-AR right before GO-A2, so that A2 compares with a fresh reading; you may send GO-AR and GO-A2 in one message, and each is admitted on its own.
   - **GO-RA (only if needed):** undo A2. Archive the new layer, one manager reload (host-wide, shared with the production units), start the API on `ba1a433` with the panel still on. It works in every state of the market units and the trading worker, also after GO-RC or GO-RM, and gives them no command. It also works in every recognised state of the new layer that A2 can leave, also when the A2 receipt was lost. RA outcomes: `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` and `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` end with the API ready on `ba1a433`; `RA_UNWOUND`, `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`, `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, `RA_API_NOT_READY`, `RA_API_STOPPED`, `RA_BUDGET_STOP`, `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` and `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` end in a recognised layer state without the API proved ready on `ba1a433` by RA's immediate checks; `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` are excluded. After an RA attempt that did not end with the API ready on `ba1a433`, also after a termination, which never counts as an end with the API ready and is followed by the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds, Root may request the next RA attempt under a new GO-RA that lists the prior attempts; the tool admits it only when the files on disk form a recognised layer state, the prior records show the facts that state needs, and production equals the baseline. Excluded: after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (an archived layer could not be put back) Root rules by a packet revision before any next RA; after `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (a production-identity difference read at any RA step, at the gate before any effect, the RD-32 state) no next RA is admitted without Root's recorded review of the difference in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against. On most failures before the API is ready the tool puts the A2 layer back by itself and starts the API on it only if the API was running when RA began. In named cases it leaves the API stopped and Root reports. If a market unit changes before the API start, it starts the API on `ba1a433` and stops for your decision; if the API then does not become ready, it leaves the API stopped and stops for your decision. If an immediate check fails after the API is ready, it leaves the API running on `ba1a433` and stops for review by Root.
6. **How to undo.** GO-RA. While the new layer is live, Root requests no other rollback (R5a, R4, R4H or RX) before GO-RA; GO-RC or GO-RM may come before GO-RA. An RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433`, other than with `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, is terminal for the RA family, and no next RA follows it; its receipt is the accepted RA receipt, for the RX tripwire and the return of R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC, when its end row is L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n, the post-P5a configuration is loaded, production is unchanged and the API is ready, after Root accepts it, with a Root review first for a market or check stop; a `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` in such an end row may be accepted by Root in the same way after its review of the difference. A Root-reviewed A2 receipt with end row L0-n or LU-n, no pending file, the API ready on `ba1a433`, the post-P5a configuration loaded and production unchanged reopens that chain in the same way, with no RA. After an accepted RA receipt, or such an A2 receipt, R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC are valid again with their own rules; RX needs a fresh job ID and manifest; the families retired by this packet stay retired. If GO-RC or GO-RM is used before A2, starting the market units again needs its own packet or Root ruling, and A2 waits for it.
7. **Your steps after A2.** After Root reports, you open the per-Bot report for Week (the Bot with fills), Month and All Time in your browser while Root reads the request timings and status codes, view only. You check that Week shows bar coverage complete and that Month and All Time return the report, with bar coverage "not checked" where the range is over a coverage budget, in English and in Thai, and check that the admin health shows `liveEnabled` false (a check that moved from the tool's immediate checks to your step), and report to Root, including the `liveEnabled` value; Root records it in the A2 acceptance record. Root then runs A2M. Root accepts A2 only after both. If A2 stopped for a market change, Root asks you first whether these steps and A2M run or you decide on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM.
8. **Staging becomes a mixed pair.** The API runs the new pin and the trading worker runs `ba1a433`. Root proves from Git that every file the trading worker loads is the same at both revisions (section 4).

## Root rulings for this packet

These are Root decisions on the packet design, not owner GOs, and they authorize no phase.

1. **Design** (ruling R1 item 1). API-only re-pin with jobs A0 (Root, local), A1 (stage the API tree, inert), A2 (API-only switch, with the read-only memory job A2M), AR (read-only reference, after a process-identity drift or once right before the GO-A2 request; ruling R3 item 1, ruling R4 items 7 and 10) and RA (rollback). The trading worker stays on `ba1a433`.
2. **Pair rule** (R1 item 2). The main packet section 4 pair rule is scoped to the `40e3b6b` fence crossing. The general pair test (section 2.3) together with the neutrality record N1 to N4 (section 4) is the A0 gate for this and later re-pins. An unknown N1 counts as not neutral.
3. **Pin** (R1 item 3; ruling R2 O-1). The owner approved OD-S5-D on 28 October 2026. The pin is the integrated, reviewed and CI-green commit that carries F, the hint changes of ruling L2 and D; rule and fallback in section 2.1. A QR-2 budget change (option A) waits for a later pin.
4. **No dump, no counter baseline, no session wait, no session attribution in A2** (R1 item 4). The changed code has no write path and the neutrality record shows no write-path change. The A2 gate keeps the P5a read-only database checks and the startup probe from the staged tree.
5. **Market units not quiesced** (R1 item 5). They are gated as a named adaptation of the collector-start market-unit gate, with the two-way hooks-and-links rule and the timer-window rule around the one reload. The stream process, the timer state and the collector failure state are re-checked after the reload, after the start and at the delayed check. The timer's reload behaviour is read in the A2 gate as a record. RA classifies the market units and the trading worker and does not compare them with a reference (ruling R2, P1-1).
6. **Layer-name room** (R1 item 6). Root does not read private names. A1 returns one boolean and counts (section 5.2). A2 derives the name on the host and stops before any effect if none fits. A false A1 boolean blocks the A2 request.
7. **RX rule** (R1 item 7; ruling R2, P3-8). While the re-pin layer is live, no GO-RX is requested or admitted; GO-RA comes first. Full-rollback order in section 9. Root records the rule in the A2 receipt review, the handoff memory and every later GO-RX request. As a tripwire, Root writes a consumed marker line for the next RX job ID while the re-pin layer is live and issues a fresh RX job ID only after an accepted RA receipt or a Root-reviewed A2 receipt that reopens the chain (section 9; ruling R5 item 3). Hardening the RX gate to refuse on an unknown live drop-in is a later packet.
8. **Retirement inventory** (R1 item 8; ruling R2 O-4 and O-5). The list in section 10 is written and pinned before GO-A2. Root confirms from the accepted RM and RC snapshots that their gates compare no pair digest and no configuration digest of the staging API, and whether their GO-record check pins the GO wording, before GO-A2 (blocker; section 10).
9. **Restore source** (R1 item 9; ruling R2 O-10). An online backup refresh is not a prerequisite for GO-A2. The GO-A2 request names the restore source time and its age.
10. **RC-Q36 target re-measure** (R1 item 10, ruling L1 finding; ruling R2 O-2 and O-3). A required A2 step (section 6.9) with the memory job A2M after the owner's browser steps (section 6.11). With the D pin the owner check is per-Bot Week (bar coverage complete), Month and All Time (report returns; C7 where the range is over a coverage budget; English and Thai). A2 is accepted only after the owner's browser check and the A2M result.
11. **GOs** (R1 item 11). GO-A1 and GO-A2 are separate effect GOs. The owner may send both in one message; each is admitted on its own. GO-RA only if needed. Each effect job is admitted on a fresh usage reading under main ruling D8.
12. **Review panel** (R1 item 12; ruling R2 O-8). Section 15.
13. **Scratch output** (R1 item 13). Every brief for this packet keeps scratch output inside its writable path.
14. **Precedents that still apply.** Main packet rulings D1 to D24, rulings D25 to D50 and the main packet execution rules (its section 7) apply unless an item above changes them.
15. **Revision 2** (ruling R2). Every finding of the revision 1 review is adopted with the auditor's fix, and open items O-1 to O-12 are ruled as listed in the revision note.
16. **Revision 3** (ruling R3). Every finding of the revision 2 re-check and of the revision 2 second opinion is adopted with its reviewer's fix and the choices of ruling R3, as listed in the revision 3 note: the reference chain through A1 and the job AR, the RA layer-state table, the lost-receipt clause, the rollback-family rule, the trading-worker heartbeat adaptation, the reference digests, N1 method version 2, re-map case 4 by effect, the wording and outcome fixes, and the settle wait.

## 1. Purpose and limits

Purpose: put the QR-2 refusal fix (`281110e`), the panel hint (`30cd728`) with the hint changes of ruling L2, and the bar-coverage degrade D on the staging API, while the trading worker keeps its `ba1a433` tree and its process.

Limits:
- Spot/Paper only; Live hard-locked; no change to Pine, Risk Manager, the research flags or any budget value.
- Production units, the production database and the loopback quant bridge are untouched and identity-checked before and after each effect, against the r6 a02 baseline (combined digest `b5789332`, as re-read at c6) or the latest Root-reviewed receipt that carries it.
- One manager reload in A2 and one in RA; at most two in either job with an in-run unwind; no other reload. Each reload is host-wide and shared with the production units. A1, A2M and AR run no reload.
- The trading worker, the market stream, the fallback collector service, the fallback collector timer and the research worker get no control verb.
- The QR-2 flag stays effective 1 from the P5a flag layer after A2 and after RA.
- No migration, no database write by the tools, no dump.
- One-use runner pattern of main packet section 7: manifest, consumed marker, nonce, receipt, envelope, retained output, 120-second Root argv GO window.
- Host work only by the single designated operations worker, serially, outside any fan-out.
- Private values stay out of tracked files.

## 2. Pin rule, pair test and re-map rule

### 2.1 Pin (placeholder `[pin]`)

The pin is one exact commit, written `[pin]` in this packet until Root sets it (ruling R1 item 3; ruling R2 O-1). The owner approved OD-S5-D on 28 October 2026:
- **Pin:** the integrated, reviewed and CI-green commit that carries F (`281110e` and `30cd728`), the hint changes of ruling L2 and D. Every GO line states "includes the QR-2 bar-coverage degrade D: yes".
- **Fallback, only if D fails review:** `30cd728`, or a later docs-only commit whose runtime paths equal those of `30cd728`. This fallback is not in the GO wording of section 14. It needs a packet revision of the GO lines (D: no), of the owner check (the 422 answer with the limit and the hint in English and Thai) and of the rehearsal, and a new review.

Before the pin is set, Root re-reads, from the pushed revision, exact-revision CI with every check of the named set completed with success (9 of 9 today), and the Root acceptance records for every slice between `ba1a433` and the pin that touches `src`, `public`, `scripts`, `migrations` or the package files. `ba1a433` must be an ancestor of the pin, and `a877733` must not be. Artifacts are built from Git blobs of the pin only. Every revision-dependent constant (tree name, manifest, served list, cache tokens, auth-route set, engine identities, startup-probe inputs) is derived from the pin, never copied. Root writes the A0 neutrality record at pin time (section 4).

**Pin set.** Root set the pin to `33e5a8c3c31b0fca61fa44792aad7d1cea769c35` on 28 October 2026, after the A0 record (sha256 `89c5c7a195ec724f95a2c2965e2bc83e20656517a6c558e70c3e3eac645eae42`). It is the integrated, reviewed and CI-green commit that carries F, the hint changes of ruling L2 and D (exact-revision CI 9 of 9); D passed review, so the fallback above is not used. `ba1a433` is an ancestor of it and `a877733` is not. The placeholder `[pin]` stays in the GO templates of section 14, and Root fills it with that commit when it writes each GO request.

Every GO line shows the pin and both revisions: the staging API at the pin, the trading worker at `ba1a433`. Any change of the pin after a GO voids that GO (ruling D1).

- **Case 4, the ranges that newly compute on staging:** (1) a span over `union_minutes` 43200: the calendar Month of 31 days, Year, All Time, and Custom over 30 days (up to 3660 days); (2) a span within 43200 with a data-time stop on a degradable limit: stored rows over the row capacity (about 15 days of 1-minute bars), `derivation_visits` over 100000, `output_intervals` over 4096 and `coverage_output_bytes` over 1048576. Still refused: `unique_windows` over 20, `coverage_input_bytes`, the zero-envelope byte refusals and the read limits (`ledger_rows`, `grid_instants`, `audit_rows`, `pending_orders`).
- **Root's estimate for case 4: 384 MB of memory.** Basis: one compute per API process (single slot, refused not queued); the worker heap caps (old generation 256 MB, young generation 32 MB, stack 4 MB) plus thread overhead, and the main-thread snapshot and its clone to the worker bounded by the read limits; Root allows about 92 MB for overhead and main-thread data. Local evidence: the rehearsal working set was 73 to 110 MiB (empty ledger) and the gate L runner tree at most 135 MiB for a month of coverage. The bound existed before the pin for in-budget ranges; D lets more ranges reach the same bounded compute. CPU: one core for at most 10 seconds per compute (deadline 10000 ms), one compute per process. The estimate is checked against the A2 gate API memory headroom with 384 MB in place of 300 MB (the estimate is larger), or, when the staging API has no memory limit, against the host available-memory floor plus 384 MB; the A2 manifest case 4 memory estimate is 384. No guard value is lowered.

### 2.3 General pair test

The API and the trading worker move as a pair when, between the trading worker's running tree and the pin, any of these differ: a file in the worker's runtime import closure; `schema.sql` or any migration; the fence module; the database layer; the form of a table that one unit writes and the other reads; an environment key read by the worker closure. Otherwise the API may move alone. The main packet section 4 rule ("move as a pair") rests on the `40e3b6b` fence crossing and is scoped to it.

### 2.4 Re-map rule for a later or different pin

Compare the pin with `ba1a433` for the trading worker closure, and with the running API tree for the API. Apply the first matching case, most restrictive first:
1. **N2 change** (migration, SQL file, startup assert, environment key, route, worker setting): this design is void; the main packet full form applies.
2. **N1 change, or N1 unknown:** move to the pair variant. That is a new packet revision, with the counter baseline and the new-rows refusal rules back.
3. **N3 change** (package files or Python inputs): rebuild and review the dependency archive; the design is otherwise unchanged.
4. **A request range that newly computes on staging** (ruling R3 item 5; A-P3-6, S-P3-8): any change, in any file, that lets a request compute that the running tree refuses, including a budget change in `src/portfolio/qr2-coverage-limits.js`. Stays API-only, but A0 records which ranges newly compute under the pin and Root's estimate of compute memory and CPU on the shared host. The A2 gate re-checks the API memory headroom with that estimate in place of 300 MB when it is larger. When the staging API has no memory limit, the bound is the host available-memory floor: the host's available memory must exceed that floor by at least the estimate. A guard value is never lowered to fit. For the D pin, the ranges that newly compute are Month beyond the coverage budgets, Year and All Time, and a range within 30 days (43200 minutes) with a data-time stop on a degradable coverage limit (section 2.2).
5. **`public/` change:** rebuild the served list and cache tokens.
6. **Docs-only change:** rebuild the constants.
7. **QR-2 read-path source change, N4 holds:** stays API-only; rebuild the manifest and constants.

Cases 4 to 7 combine: a pin with changes of more than one of these kinds takes each matching case. The D pin takes cases 4, 5 and 7.

A pin change after a GO voids that GO. A second re-pin while this packet's re-pin layer is live needs its own packet revision: its expected list is the post-A2 list, and the layer it supersedes is this packet's layer.

## 4. A0: Root, local, read only

Root does A0 in the checkout at pin time, from Git blobs only (never working copies), and writes one ignored local A0 record under `.qa-local/`. Root pins the sha256 of that record in the A1, AR, A2 (with A2M) and RA manifests. A0 has no host step.

**Neutrality record.** A pin P is neutral against the trading worker's tree `ba1a433` when all of these hold:
- **N1. Worker and Paper write closure unchanged.** No file in the transitive static import closure of the trading worker entry, and no file in the Paper write-path closure defined below, differs between `ba1a433` and P. The scan follows relative `import ... from`, `export ... from`, bare `import`, literal `import()`, `new URL(..., import.meta.url)` and `.sql` reads. A non-literal dynamic import in any closure file makes N1 unknown, and unknown counts as not neutral.
- **N2. No migration or startup change.** No migration module, SQL file, migration or rollback script, `src/config.js`, startup assert (`verifySchema`, `requirePaperLedgerFence`, the runtime and maintenance locks), environment key read, route table or worker setting differs.
- **N3. Dependencies unchanged.** `package.json`, the lock file and the Python inputs are field-equal (the P1 dependency rule), and the dependency archive built from the pin is byte-equal to the one staged by P1 attempt a02.
- **N4. Changes only in public or QR-2 read files.** Every changed runtime file is in `public/` or in the QR-2 read path (modules imported only by the QR-2 routes, readers and compute worker).

**Paper write-path closure, method version 2** (ruling R2, P2-2; ruling R3 item 4, A-P2-3). Derived from Git at both `ba1a433` and P with this rule:
1. **Paper write table set W, by name only, wherever the table is created:** every table whose name begins with `paper_` (for example funding, cash journal, snapshots, the fence and capital models), `ledger_` or `pine_bridge_`, plus `fills`, `positions`, `signals`, `daily_stats`, `bot_sessions` and `bot_session_archive`, matched schema-qualified or not. The creation site is not a condition. (Method version 1 required creation in `src/postgres/schema.sql` or in a `.sql` file of the API closure; that left out the fence table, which a JavaScript template string creates, and the capital-model table, whose `.sql` file is not in the API closure because a migration module reads it through a non-literal URL helper.)
2. **Entry modules:** every JavaScript module of the API closure whose source contains a write statement (`INSERT INTO`, `UPDATE ... SET`, `DELETE FROM`, `TRUNCATE`, `MERGE INTO` or `COPY ... FROM`) that names a table of W, schema-qualified or not; every module of the API closure with a write statement whose target is not a literal table name (included, never dropped); and, by path, `src/postgres/pine-bridge-receiver.js`, `src/postgres/paper-ledger-fence.js`, `src/postgres/ledger.js`, `src/paper-accounting.js` and `src/postgres/db.js`.
3. **`src/postgres/server.js`** is not an entry, because its closure is the whole API. It must be byte-equal between `ba1a433` and P; its own route handlers are then unchanged, and its imports of changed modules are covered by N4.
4. **Closure:** the union of the transitive closures of the entry modules at both revisions, under the scan rules of N1.

Derive-b of the A0 record (Opus 5.5 / high, independent, from Git) is the independent re-derivation of the N1 counts and list hashes that A1 acceptance needs, because A0 is the legal basis of the API-only design (ruling R10 item 3(c)).

**Contents of the A0 record:**
1. The pin, `ba1a433`, and the method version of the closure scan (version 2).
2. N1: the worker closure file count and the SHA-256 of the sorted path list at both revisions; the result of `git diff --quiet ba1a433 <pin> -- <closure files>`; the same for the Paper write-path closure, with the table set W, the entry-module list and its SHA-256, and the `src/postgres/server.js` unchanged-bytes result; the count of non-literal dynamic imports (must be 0).
3. N2: the path list checked and an empty difference over it.
4. N3: the field-equality result and the dependency archive digest against the P1 attempt a02 digest.
5. N4: each changed runtime file with its class (`public/` or QR-2 read path) and, for each QR-2 file, its importer set. The request ranges that newly compute under the pin (case 4; for D: Month beyond the coverage budgets, Year and All Time, and a range within 30 days with a data-time stop on a degradable coverage limit; section 2.2) and Root's estimate of compute memory and CPU on the shared host, with the bound it is checked against (the API memory limit headroom, or the host available-memory floor when the API has no memory limit).
6. The re-map case or cases taken (section 2.4), which for a neutral pin is case 4, 5, 6 or 7 (cases 4, 5 and 7 together for the D pin), or case 3 with a reviewed archive.
7. Exact-revision CI: the check set, each conclusion and the revision read; the acceptance record of each runtime slice.
8. The local rehearsal (ruling D34 form, not a staging check), with the flag 1 and a session: a range over a coverage budget returns the report with bar coverage "not checked" (C7) in English and in Thai; a remaining 422 case answers 422 `QR2_RANGE_ADMISSION_REFUSED` with `{limit, value, max}` and the panel shows the generic hint in English and in Thai, without the `timestamp_rows` and `union_minutes` lines (ruling L1 Q6). The record names every item that could not be shown locally.

Any N item that fails or is unknown stops this design at A0 (section 2.4).

**Status for the pin `33e5a8c`.** A0 is done for this pin. Root wrote the A0 record on 28 October 2026 (sha256 `89c5c7a195ec724f95a2c2965e2bc83e20656517a6c558e70c3e3eac645eae42`). Its verdict is NEUTRAL: N1 to N4 hold, the re-map cases are 4, 5 and 7, exact-revision CI is 9 of 9, and the local rehearsal and Root's browser check on the rehearsal server passed (section 2.2 has the facts). Derive-b of the A0 record (Opus 5.5 / high, independent, from Git) is the N1 re-derivation that this section requires before A1 acceptance (ruling R10 item 3(c)). Root pins the record's sha256 in the A1, AR, A2 (with A2M) and RA manifests.

## 5. A1: inert stage of the API tree

### 5.1 Gate and effects

Copied by value from the accepted P1 tool (r2 snapshot), with named adaptations: the targets are reduced to the API tree; the required ancestor is `ba1a433`; the dependency archive must be byte-equal to the P1 attempt a02 archive, and a difference stops the build.

- **Build, local only.** Deterministic package from Git blobs of the pin (P1 form: built twice in memory and byte-compared, exclusive-create outputs). Served list, cache tokens and auth-route set rebuilt from the pin.
- **Gate, read only.** Host health floors as in P1 (memory, load, free disk for one more API tree, no heavy job, no live payload, the expected Node major); no pending systemd job and no unit needing a reload; production identity against the baseline; the staging API, trading worker, research worker, market stream, fallback collector service and fallback collector timer as states and main processes only, against the reference that the A1 manifest names: the c6 record of the accepted collector start a01 (the c1 export facts, which c6 recorded as unchanged), or the latest Root-accepted AR record (section 6.12) (ruling R3 item 1; ruling R4 item 7, S-P3-3). The c6 record holds equality booleans, not the values; the compared values are the c1 export facts (`streamDigest`, `streamNRestarts`, `pairDigests` and `pairNRestarts`), which Root copies by value into the private A1 manifest, and the build asserts their form against a fake built from the recorded form. The staging API, the trading worker and the stream must be the same main process with the same restart count as the reference; the research worker stopped; the timer active; the fallback collector service inactive with result success, or in a recorded timer-started run (the A2 rule; ruling R3, S-P3-2). A unit in automatic restart or another transitioning state refuses, with no settle wait. A fallback collector service in a timer-started run is not transitioning for this rule; it is recorded (ruling R5 item 7; A-P3-4(a)). For the staging API, the trading worker or the stream that is a process-identity drift, and the path of section 6.3 applies. For the fallback collector service, the timer or the research worker it is not a drift: it is a pre-effect stop, and the next A1 attempt gets a contingency A1 job ID with no AR (ruling R4 item 7; A-P3-6(b)).
- **Effects.** One fresh incoming directory (mode 0700) and one copy of the package files; on the host: incoming exact, archive checked against the manifest, extraction into a partial directory with exclusive create per file, tree exact (files, bad, extra, missing), `node --check` on the API entry set (Root derives the count from the pin), rename into the existing stage directory under a name derived from the pin that collides with no existing name, exact again.
- **No activation.** No drop-in file, daemon reload, start, stop, restart or reset-failed; no environment read other than the drop-in digests of section 5.2; no database connection; no delete. Old trees stay.
- **Record.** At the end reading the staging API, the trading worker and the stream equal the gate (state, main process and restart count). A collector service run started by the timer, and a change of the timer's last trigger or next elapse, are recorded, never a difference. A research worker no longer stopped, a timer no longer active or a failed fallback collector service at the end is a Root review item: Root reports it, and it does not lead to AR (ruling R5 item 7; A-P3-4(b)). A difference of the staging API, the trading worker or the stream at the end reading comes after the stage, so A1 has had effects and gets no contingency job ID. Root reports it, and AR may follow it (section 6.12). Root may then accept the staged tree, when it is exact, with the Root-accepted AR record as the A2 reference in place of the A1 reference export, with no new A1 (ruling R4 item 7; A-P3-6(a)).
- **Acceptance of the A1 record** (ruling R20 item 3, extending ruling R8 N1). Root accepts an A1 record for A2 and RA use only when all of these hold: `layerNameFits`, `admissionOffFound` and `p5aLayerFound` are all true; the admission-off digest is present; the P5a layer digest appears exactly once among the drop-in digests; the live count equals the layer count; the drop-in list digest equals the collector c1 export value (`pairDropInDigests` for the staging API); the staged tree is exact, and the end reading equals the gate. A record that misses any of these is a Root review item, and A2 is not requested. This is Root's acceptance check of the record; it is not tool behaviour, and the tool tests of section 15 do not cover it (ruling R22 item 9).
- **Reference export** (ruling R3 item 1; A-P2-2, S-P2-5). A1 exports, as read at the end and equal to the gate, the main-process digest and the restart count of the staging API, the trading worker and the market stream, in the form of the collector start c1 export: SHA-256 over the canonical JSON of the main process, its start ticks and the invocation, copied by value from the accepted collector start build. The accepted A1 record is the process reference of A2 (section 6.2), unless Root accepted the staged tree with an AR record (Record above) or a later AR record replaces it. The values stay in the private receipt; the tracked result record names only their presence.
- **Record directory.** One record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory; the incoming directory, the staged tree and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool are the only other writes.
- **Bounds.** Host bound 300 seconds, carried by value from the accepted P1 revision 2 limits (section 11.1).
- **Rollback.** None needed. The tree stays inert. Its removal, if wanted, needs its own packet.

### 5.2 The layer-name boolean (ruling R1 item 6)

A1 reads the staging API drop-in names and the SHA-256 of each drop-in file inside the host-side process, never returning a name or content. The P4, P5a and admission-off layers, and possibly earlier release layers, carry environment assignments, so hashing their bytes is an environment-source read under ruling D4; only digests, counts and the one boolean leave that process; never a name or content (ruling R3, S-P3-3). It identifies the admission-off layer by its recorded name (the layer must be live and sort last among the live layers, otherwise it is not found) and the P5a flag layer by its recorded name and its recorded digest (exactly the live file with that name must have that digest, otherwise it is not found, which guards against a renamed or edited layer). Both names come in through the private A1 binding and never leave the host-side process (ruling R16 item 2). It applies the single reviewed name-derivation function that A2 also uses (copied by value into both tools), with a fixed candidate order. Copying that function by value is not enough: the composite rule below is applied by A1 and, in the same form, by the A2 gate (section 6.2). It returns:
- one boolean, `layerNameFits`, true only under the composite rule (ruling R8 item 3, N6): the P5a flag layer and the admission-off layer are both found among the live layers; no live layer other than the admission-off layer sorts after the P5a flag layer; and the name-derivation function returns a valid name, one that matches the accepted layer-name form, sorts after every live layer including the P5a flag layer, sorts before the admission-off layer, and equals no live, pending or archived file name. In every other case it is false;
- counts: live layers, live layers that sort after the P5a flag layer other than the admission-off layer (expected 0), and archived files.

**Private digest export** (ruling R16 item 2(d)). A1 also exports, in its private receipt and retained output only and never a name: the content digest of the admission-off layer; the sorted list of the content digests of every live drop-in file; the drop-in list digest over the base names, in the P5a form, computed in the host-side process from the live paths; and the live count. The tracked result names only that these exports are present. After Root accepts the A1 record, the A2 and RA manifests take their drop-in digests from it (section 6.13); Root checks that the list digest equals the c1 `pairDropInDigests` value for the staging API, and a difference is a drift on which Root rules.

**Shape rule** (ruling R20 item 2). More than 64 live drop-ins is `PRE_DROPIN_SHAPE`, which keeps the returned frame under the output limit. Duplicate live content is not a shape refusal, because names identify the two layers; the acceptance conditions of section 5.1 (acceptance of the A1 record) cover the gap that this leaves.

No name leaves the host-side process. A false boolean blocks the A2 request; the order rule changes only by a new Root ruling and packet revision.

## 6. A2: API switch

### 6.1 Preconditions (Root, before the GO request)

1. A1 accepted, with the boolean true and the record conditions of section 5.1 (acceptance of the A1 record; ruling R20 item 3); the A0 record pinned; the A2 manifest names the process reference (the accepted A1 record, or the latest Root-accepted AR record after a drift, after a difference at the A1 end reading or from the AR of item 9).
2. The A2, A2M, RA and AR tool (AR is built with the A2 tool package; ruling R9 item 13) built, reviewed and accepted against this packet (rollback readiness) before GO-A1 is requested, so that the window between A1 and A2 holds no build or review (ruling R4 item 10; A-P3-7); a dry admission passes. Ruling R26 accepts fix rounds 5 and 6: the A2RA build at SHA256SUMS c1ed1a24 (snapshot r6) with the ADP lane at b4168240 (snapshot r5) is accepted for GO use, and the A1 tool at 914e643d (snapshot r9) is accepted (ruling R20). This item is met once the four packet constants are set in the tools and the tools are re-sealed; that step changes only those four values and needs no new review round (ruling R26 item 1). The nine adapters are built in their own lane (ADP), in its own directory, before any GO, are reviewed inside the A2RA fix rounds (ruling R17 item 2; the lane keeps its own suite and mutants), and are integrated into the generator only after the build fix round and ADP are both accepted; no mode, A2M and AR included, is accepted for a real run before then (ruling R9 item 11e).
3. The retirement inventory of section 10 written, with its marker lines, and pinned (ruling R2 O-5).
4. **Blocker (ruling R2 O-4):** Root has recorded, from the accepted RM and RC snapshots, that their gates compare no pair digest and no configuration digest of the staging API, and whether their GO-record check pins the GO wording (section 10). Without that record GO-A2 is not requested. Recorded on 28 October 2026 for both snapshots (section 10).
5. The local rehearsal recorded in A0 (C7 in English and Thai, and the generic 422 hint), with the items not shown locally named in the GO-A2 request.
6. The GO-A2 request names the restore source, the P4 x4 a02 pre-switch backup of 2026-10-26T04:49:30Z, and its age (section 9).
7. A fresh usage reading under main ruling D8 covers A2, its delayed check, A2M and RA.
8. No other S6 job of any family in flight.
9. **Stream restart rate and A2 reference** (ruling R4 item 10; A-P3-7, S-P3-7(c)). The GO-A2 request states the observed restart rate of the market stream from the private receipt chain (P4M a03 to c6, A1 and any AR record), as a count over a period. Root may request one GO-AR without a drift right before or together with the GO-A2 request, as the A2 reference (section 6.12). Both GO lines may come in one owner message, and each is admitted on its own: AR runs first, and A2 is admitted only after Root accepts the AR record and writes the A2 manifest that names it.

### 6.2 Fresh gate (read only, before the first effect)

Every item stops before any effect when it fails. The drift rule of main packet section 7 applies against the accepted P5a a01 record (configuration and API environment digests and the P5a flag layer digest), the accepted A1 record (the drop-in list digest, the per-file drop-in digests and the admission-off layer digest; ruling R16 item 3; the list digest is checked equal to the c1 `pairDropInDigests` value for the staging API, and a difference is a drift on which Root rules), the c1 gate of the accepted collector start a01 record (timer settings) and its c1 export facts (the trading worker's configuration and drop-in list digests; ruling R4 item 8), the process reference that the A2 manifest names (the accepted A1 record, or the latest Root-accepted AR record; ruling R3 item 1) for unit classes, main processes and restart counts, and the A1 record for the staged tree. The main-process and restart values that the tool compares sit in the private receipt chain from P4M a03 to c6, A1 and any AR record, in ignored local records; this packet names that chain and never its values.

- **Settle wait** (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-9). A unit found in automatic restart or in another transitioning state, or with a pending job of its own, gets one read-only settle wait of up to 30 seconds in total per gate, with polls, before the gate classifies it. A unit still transitioning at the bound refuses before any effect. A unit that settles into a new main process is a process-identity drift (section 6.3). A fallback collector service in a timer-started run is not transitioning for this rule; section 6.3 records it.
- **Host.** Health floors, Node major, systemd version, the login manager's setting for killing user processes at logout (stop if on or unknown), no pending job, no unit needing a reload.
- **Production identity** against the baseline.
- **Staging API.** Active as the same main process with the same restart count as the process reference; drop-in list digest (the post-P5a list) and every drop-in digest equal to the accepted A1 record (sources in section 6.13); working directory the `ba1a433` API tree; QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer only; five research flags 0 from the admission-off layer; no heap-size option from any source; the QR-2 flag set in no environment file (ruling D34 source rule); memory limit, if any, at least 300 MB above resident memory, or at least Root's re-map case 4 estimate when that is larger; with no memory limit, the host's available memory above the host available-memory floor by at least that estimate (section 2.4). The resident memory and the host's available memory are recorded as numbers (RC-Q36 "before" reading).
- **Journals** (kept from the P5a gate; ruling R2, P3-5). The staging API and trading-worker journals are read for line counts only under the P5a refusal-line and line-cap rules; an unreadable journal or a P5a refusal-line stop stops A2 before any effect.
- **Environment sources** (ruling D4(b), P5a form). From every source other than the unit's own environment property, only the QR-2 flag, the five research flags and the Node options are kept, plus the database connection key for the startup probe. The unit's own environment property is held and compared as a whole inside the host-side process; only its digest without the QR-2 key is returned. The trading worker's own environment property is held inside the host-side process the same way; only its digest is returned. The database connection key, the encryption keys and the PostgreSQL client keys never leave that process and are never returned, printed or stored. Not a binding read.
- **Unit property reads** (ruling R4 item 6; A-P3-5; ruling R5 item 6, A-P3-3). Every unit property read other than the host-side environment-source read of the staging API's and the trading worker's own environment property (digest only), in the gate, the hooks-and-links reads and the later checks, for the three market units, the staging API and the trading worker, uses the "no environment" list form of the collector start build: the environment properties are removed from the read list for every role, including the collector service, which the collector start gate reads with them. The build asserts that no class, process, configuration or hooks read list holds an environment property, and asserts that the environment property is read only in the host-side process, with only its digest leaving that process. The only environment reads are the environment-source reads above, inside the host-side process.
- **Trading worker.** Same main process and restart count as the process reference, active, fresh heartbeat, environment property digest equal to the reference: the `envDigests.TRADING` value of the accepted P4 x4 a02 receipt, as compared at the P5a a01 gate (ruling R3 item 3; S-P2-4). Digest form, copied by value from the accepted P5a tool: the map parsed from the unit's environment property, canonical JSON, SHA-256, with every key kept (the API digest form removes the QR-2 key; the trading worker's does not). The value is copied into the private A2 manifest; the build asserts the form against a fake built from the recorded form. Configuration and drop-in list digests (ruling R4 item 8; S-P3-4): equal to the c1 `pairConfigDigests` and `pairDropInDigests` values for the trading worker, in the c6 form, which c6 recorded as equal; Root copies them by value into the private A2 manifest, and the build asserts the form against a fake built from the recorded form.
- **Heartbeat rows and Live** (ruling R12 item 7; ruling R14 item 6). The gate checks mode and Live on all rows, unfiltered, and refuses on a Live signal above zero (the Live signal count must be a non-negative integer, otherwise it is unreadable); it refuses with `HEARTBEAT_UNREADABLE` when any row lacks a string role. The role filter serves only the own-row and freshness rules.
- **Research worker** loaded and stopped.
- **Market units:** section 6.3.
- **Staged tree** exact against the A1 record, checked with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) before the first database read: a FIFO entry, a linked root, a file over the byte cap or a count over the cap refuses before any database read and any command (ruling R13 item 10). The walk takes the P5a real-path form: the expected real path is the resolved parent of the bound root plus the tree name, the tree root itself must not be a link, and an unreadable parent fails the check; a bound root that is not a canonical string (a trailing `/`, `//`, `/./` or `/../`) is refused when the bound is validated (ruling R19 item 4(g); ruling R21 item 5(g)).
- **Layer name** derived from the live list (section 7); no pending or archived file with that name. The gate states the composite layer-fit rule of section 5.2 explicitly (ruling R8 item 3, N6; ruling R9 item 3): the P5a flag layer and the admission-off layer are both found, no live layer other than the admission-off layer sorts after the P5a flag layer, and the name-derivation function returns a name; a fake with another live layer after the P5a flag layer ends `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME` before any effect. If none fits, or the composite rule fails, stop (`A2_NO_LAYER_NAME`). The gate also records the count and the SHA-256 of every other file of the staging API drop-in directory (every file other than the live drop-in list and the pending and archive forms of the derived name; for example archives of earlier packets), inside the host-side process and never a name or content (an environment-source read under ruling D4, as in section 5.2), for the compared file set of RA (section 8; ruling R4 item 4; ruling R5 item 9, A-P3-6(c)). Before its first effect on the staging API, A2 writes that gate inventory (the count and the SHA-256 list, sorted non-decreasing, duplicates allowed, at most 64 entries, no names) as a gate line of its host progress record, and the A2 receipt carries the same (ruling R5 item 1; A-P2-1, S-P2-2). One shared function writes the inventory in A2 and reads it in RA. With more than 64 files A2 refuses before any effect with `PRE_INVENTORY_TOO_LARGE` (ruling R9 item 8).
- **Database**, read only through the migration connection selector (ruling D4(a)): schema version 14, fence INSTALLED, research queue idle. No counter baseline.
- **Startup probe** (P4 step 1 form): a reviewed read-only probe from the staged API tree calls only `verifySchema` and `requirePaperLedgerFence` and returns booleans and counts. Any refusal stops.
- **Retirement inventory** present and pinned.
- **Time budget.** The remaining host bound covers the worst case from this point, including the timer-window wait (section 11.1).

### 6.3 Market-unit gate, hooks and links, timer window

Named adaptation of the collector-start market-unit gate (ruling D47 P2-1 precedent), with the role list passed as an argument:
- **Stream.** Active, same main process and restart count as the process reference (section 6.2), configuration digest equal to the P4M m1 a03 manifest's market configuration digest of the stream, which the m1 a03 run found equal (`configEqual` true); the P4M binding holds no digest field (ruling R3 item 3; S-P3-1; ruling R21 item 6; section 6.13). The digest is in the P4M tool's configuration digest form, copied by value. Root copies the value into the private A2 manifest at build time; the tool reads no binding on the host. The build asserts the form against a fake built from the recorded form.
- **Timer.** Active with a next elapse; trigger count, accuracy, randomized delay and persistence equal to the c1 gate of the accepted collector start a01 record: one calendar trigger, accuracy 1000 ms, randomized delay 0, persistence off (calendar text not compared).
- **Collector service.** Inactive with result success, or in a timer-started run (recorded).
- No failed or transitioning market unit after the settle wait of section 6.2; no pending job of any unit; no unit needing a reload. A failed market unit refuses A2; the owner decides on GO-RC or GO-RM, and A2 then needs the market units started again and a new process reference (a Root-accepted AR record) (ruling R3, A-P3-5). A start of the market units after GO-RC or GO-RM, before A2, needs its own packet or Root ruling, because the start families are retired before GO-A2 (section 10) and RM and RC only stop; A2 waits for it (ruling R4 item 11; A-P3-8, S-P3-7(b)).

**Recorded cadence** (collector start a01, 28 October 2026): the timer became ready at 07:33:06.933Z; its first run was 113067 ms later (07:35:00Z) and its last trigger at c6 was 413067 ms after readiness (07:40:00Z). The calendar period is about 300 seconds; the collector run at c1 completed in under one second (summary line at 624 ms).

**Process identity drift** (ruling R2, P3-9; ruling R3 item 1, A-P2-2, S-P2-5). A normal stream restart (the stream exits on a venue tick or step change and restarts), or a restart of the staging API or the trading worker, since the process reference stops A1 or A2 at its gate, before any effect, and spends that GO. The reference of A1 is the c6 record (the c1 export facts, section 5.1) or the latest Root-accepted AR record; the reference of A2 is the accepted A1 record or the latest Root-accepted AR record; each manifest names which. Root's path, the only one: Root reports the drift and its cost to the owner, requests GO-AR (section 6.12), reviews and accepts the AR record, and then requests a new GO for the next attempt of the stopped job under a contingency job ID (the stop had no effect), with a manifest that names that AR record. The A1 and A2 GO lines already name "the latest Root-accepted AR record", so their wording does not change. Cost: one extra read-only GO (GO-AR) and the new GO of the next attempt. The retired collector c1 and c6 families are not used. A difference of the staging API, the trading worker or the stream found at A1's end reading, after the stage, takes the same path to GO-AR (collector runs and timer trigger changes are recorded, and the other A1 end-reading items are Root review items; section 5.1), after which Root may accept the staged tree with the AR record as the A2 reference, with no new A1 (section 5.1). A transitioning collector service, timer or research worker at the A1 gate is not a drift (section 5.1). Root may also request one AR without a drift right before the GO-A2 request (section 6.1 item 9) (ruling R4 items 7 and 10).

**Two-way hooks-and-links rule.** No stop, start or reload step of the staging API reaches a market unit, and no market unit's start, stop or failure handling names the staging API. Properties read (ruling R2, P3-4):
- **Staging API side:** `Conflicts`, `ConflictedBy`, `PropagatesStopTo`, `PropagatesReloadTo`, `BoundBy`, `ConsistsOf`, `RequiredBy`, `WantedBy`, `OnFailure`, `OnSuccess` name no market unit.
- **Market side** (each of the stream, the collector service and the collector timer): `Wants`, `Requires`, `Requisite`, `BindsTo`, `PartOf`, `Upholds`, `Conflicts`, `OnFailure`, `OnSuccess`, `StopPropagatedFrom`, `ReloadPropagatedFrom` name no staging API.

Names stay in the host-side process; only booleans and counts are returned. Paths through targets and mounts are not read (named risk).

**Timer reload behaviour, as a record** (ruling R1 item 5, U-2). The gate records the timer's persistence, accuracy, randomized delay, last trigger and next elapse. After the reload and at the delayed check the tool records them again, and whether the next elapse changed and whether a collector run started. A changed next elapse is recorded, never a stop.

**Timer-window rule.** The switch window W is the worst case from the API stop command to the end of the post-reload market re-check: stop 30 seconds, read-only grace 70 seconds, pre-write check 5 seconds, pre-write wait 15 seconds, layer write 3 seconds, reload 15 seconds, loaded-configuration gate 10 seconds, market re-check 10 seconds, margin 10 seconds; W = 168 seconds (section 11.1). The author check asserts W plus the timer accuracy plus the randomized delay below the recorded period (168 + 1 + 0 = 169 < 300), and the build asserts the same with the tool's limits; otherwise the gate could never pass and GO-A2 would be spent.
1. At the end of the gate and again right before the stop command, the timer's next elapse must be later than now plus W plus the timer accuracy and randomized delay, the collector service must not be in a run, and the job list must be empty.
2. Otherwise the tool waits, read only, re-reading the timer and the collector service, until a run that fires has ended and the next elapse is outside the window. The wait is bounded at 420 seconds (ruling R2 O-6; section 11.1). At the bound it stops before any effect (`A2_TIMER_WINDOW`).
3. After the proved API stop and before the layer write, the tool re-checks (at most 5 seconds): no collector run in progress and no pending job. A run in progress gets a read-only wait of up to 15 seconds. If it has not ended, the tool takes the unwind with no layer written (section 6.6).
4. The unwind reload, when needed, is not gated by the timer: restoring the API comes first, and the collector state is recorded.

### 6.4 Effects, in order (P5a pattern)

1. Create the record directory (progress record and result file) in the dedicated protected backup directory, and write the gate inventory line of section 6.2 into the progress record before step 2 (ruling R5 item 1).
2. Stop the staging API and prove the stop: 30-second bound, then a read-only wait of up to 70 seconds with no second stop command.
3. Pre-write timer and job re-check (section 6.3 item 3).
4. Write the re-pin layer as a pending file (exclusive create, mode 0600, fsync) and rename it in.
5. One manager reload.
6. Loaded-configuration gate (section 6.5) and production identity.
7. Market re-check after the reload: the stream the same main process with no restart and no control-group event, the timer active, the collector service not failed.
8. Start the staging API; readiness within 40 seconds.

**Restart count at the start** (ruling R13 item 2, which amends ruling R12 item 14). n0 is the restart count read right before the start command. n1 is the count in the snapshot that ends the start poll (unit running, no job). The start is not ready unless n1 is 0 or equal to n0, and the count read after readiness must equal n1; a count that is not an integer is not ready (ruling R19 item 4(e); ruling R22 item 6). The rule must be right under both behaviours of the service manager, a start that resets the count and a start that keeps it, because Root has no host fact on the reset rule (reading one is target contact, which needs a finalized packet and a GO) and the manager version is unknown; section 12 names the residual. It applies to every start of the staging API in A2 and RA: the main start, the market-branch start, the unwind and put-back starts and the case 5 restart. The readiness poll is a named adaptation of the P5a loop, which keeps polling inside the alive window; its coarse failure tokens are NOT_READY and TIMEOUT, and the receipt keeps the adapter's failed list as the detail (ruling R13 item 7).

**Progress lines** (ruling R6 item 7; S-P3-1). Before every rename (step 4, and every archive of the unwind) and every reload (step 5, and the unwind reload), the tool writes an fsynced progress line `<step> ATTEMPTED` that carries the expected source and target form and the expected digest, never a name, and after the step a line `<step> DONE`. A write of either line that fails is a failed progress write, and any failed A2 progress write before readiness is an unwind trigger (section 6.6; ruling R7 item 3, P3-2). The digest line of a pending file before its rename (section 6.6) is such a line, and the A2 receipt carries it. **Torn line** (ruling R9 item 10l): after a failed or partial write, the writer starts the next line on a fresh line and writes a `NOTE TORN_PREVIOUS` line; the progress reader tolerates exactly that pattern, counts the torn step as `ATTEMPTED` and lets the disk decide, and refuses on any other line it cannot parse. One torn last line with no line ending, at the end of the file, is read as an `ATTEMPTED` step of unknown kind and the disk decides; a torn line anywhere else still refuses, and if the row's facts are missing because of it, row recognition refuses (ruling R12 item 15).

**Market change after the reload** (confirmed by ruling R2 O-7). If step 7 finds a market unit changed (stream not the same process, timer not active, collector failed), the API code is not the cause, and a second reload would add risk. The tool therefore does not unwind and runs no extra reload: it gives the market units no command, starts the API on the new tree, runs the immediate checks and ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` without the delayed check. The branch runs under the consumable allowance of section 6.6 (ruling R10 item 2; ruling R9a): granted when the change is seen, one start, one stop (only after a failed start or readiness, proved) and nothing else. An unreadable stream at the reload re-check is a failure (`EFF_STREAM_UNREADABLE`), not a market change (ruling R12 item 15; ruling R22 item 4: `PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT` is the code of the RA gate before any effect only; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-1: `STREAM_UNREADABLE` without a prefix is only the A2 immediate-check token). Root reports; the owner decides on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM. GO-RC or GO-RM before GO-RA is admissible: RA classifies the market units and gives them no command (section 8).

**Named outcomes of the market branch and of the later checks** (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-5).
- **API not ready in the market branch.** If, after a market change at the reload, the API start or readiness fails, the tool runs no unwind and no extra reload and gives the market units no command. It stops the API if it runs and proves the stop (30 seconds, then the read-only wait of up to 70 seconds), starts nothing, and ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` with the API stopped (or failed) on the re-pin layer. This stop is the one stop of the market allowance (section 6.6). When the start leaves the API failed with an empty job queue, the tool sends no stop and the failed state counts as stopped (packet r6, ruling R6 item 3; a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped, ruling R25 item 7, G1); the receipt records the failed state (ruling R13 item 8). When the stop is not proved, the end is `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`, also though no unwind ran; the receipt carries `marketStopProved` false and `stopProved` false (the branch sent a stop and no start followed it, so the two fields are equal) and `apiAtEnd` (ruling R13 item 6; ruling R25 item 2; section 6.6). When the branch sent no stop (the start left the API failed), `marketStopProved` is absent (no key is written), and `stopProved` follows the rule of section 6.6: that class read is a stop read, so the value is true when the run sent a stop earlier, the read shows the API failed with an empty job queue and no start was attempted after the read, and absent when the run sent no stop. Root reports; the owner decides on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM. RA admits that state (section 8, row LL).
- **Market change at the immediate or the delayed check.** If the immediate checks (section 6.7) or the delayed check (section 6.8) find a market unit changed, the tool gives no command to any unit, runs no unwind and no reload, leaves the API running on the new tree and ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` (at the immediate checks without the delayed check). Root reports; the owner decides on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM.
- **Owner steps and A2M after a market stop** (ruling R3, A-P3-3). After `A2_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`, Root asks the owner first whether the browser steps and A2M run or the owner decides on GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM. If A2M runs, its process reference is the process of the last check that ran (the immediate-check process when the delayed check did not run); when the A2 receipt holds no such reference, Root records the answer as unknown (section 6.11).

### 6.5 Loaded-configuration gate

On the staging API after the reload:
- drop-in list equal to the post-P5a list plus the re-pin layer, every earlier drop-in digest unchanged;
- working directory the staged tree;
- a single start command whose last argument is the staged tree's `src/postgres/server.js`, with the other arguments equal to the P4 form;
- QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer only;
- five research flags 0;
- no heap-size option from any source;
- environment property digest without the QR-2 key equal to the P5a record;
- the QR-2 flag set in no environment file.

Also after the reload: production identity; the trading worker's main process unchanged.

### 6.6 In-run unwind (before readiness)

Any failure before readiness, other than a market change after the reload (section 6.4), starts the unwind: a stop that is not proved or fails, a busy port, the pre-write re-check, the layer write, the reload, the loaded-configuration or production-identity check after the reload, the API start or readiness, a step-budget or in-run-limit stop, or a failed progress write. A step-budget or in-run-limit stop before readiness and a failed progress write are failures and unwind. A termination is not unwound: it sends no command in any phase, as the paragraph after the named cases below states (ruling R12 item 5, which withdraws the split of ruling R10 item 3(b); ruling R7 item 3, P3-2). **No effect yet** (fix round 5, ruling R24 item 4, F2): when the effect flag is false at a trigger (a budget stop, a clock refusal, a termination or a throw), A2 ends with the PRE code of that trigger, sends no command and runs no unwind. The codes that can occur are `PRE_TIME_BUDGET` (a budget stop or a clock refusal) and `PRE_TERMINATED` (a termination); the audit of the build found these the only reachable triggers (ruling R25 item 5, D28). The effect flag flips before a host-changing command is sent, and a command that fails, times out or errors after it was sent counts as an effect, so such a trigger takes the unwind below. **End form** (ruling R25 item 5; audit-r9 P3-3): an A2 end with a PRE_ code and the flag false sent no command, but its receipt still has `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, and the runner evaluates it as `A2_STOP_EFFECT` with `hostEffect` EFFECT; this is stricter than the facts and is accepted. Such an end needs no RA. The next step is Root's review of the receipt, confirming zero control commands, and then a fresh A2 gate under a contingency job ID with its own GO; the owner's current authority covers attempt a01 only. A port is busy when it has a LISTEN row, or any row with a nonzero inode, on the API's local port in tcp or tcp6 (ruling R12 item 11). On most failures the tool returns the API to `ba1a433` by itself:
- The tool stops the API and proves the stop.
- It archives the re-pin layer by no-clobber rename if it was written. A pending file left before the rename is archived the same way, by no-clobber rename, with its digest recorded in the progress record before the rename; nothing is deleted (ruling R4 item 9; S-P3-5; ruling R5 item 5, S-P3-2). Row LU of section 8 matches that pre-rename line. The A2 receipt carries that pre-rename line, and each archive and the unwind reload get their `ATTEMPTED` and `DONE` lines (section 6.4; ruling R6 item 7; A-P3-6, S-P3-1). If the pre-rename line cannot be written, the pending file stays in place, the tool starts nothing and ends `A2_UNWIND_PROGRESS_FAILED` (row LP-n; ruling R6 item 3; S-P3-6).
- It runs one more manager reload if the layer was renamed into place or a reload was attempted. A2 therefore runs at most two reloads.
- When the layer was written or a reload was attempted, it checks that the post-P5a configuration is loaded (the `ba1a433` tree, the QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer, research flags 0) and that the production identity is unchanged, and only then starts the API on it.
- When no layer was written and no reload was attempted, it starts the API after the proved stop on the unchanged configuration.

In these named cases it leaves the API stopped and Root reports:
- a stop that is still not proved after the read-only wait, or a failed API: the tool starts nothing and sends no second stop command; an error in the proof wait other than a termination (a snapshot fault or a budget stop) counts as not proved, sends no further command, and the end is `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with the API class read (ruling R13 item 3);
- a failed unwind reload, a failed loaded-configuration check after it, or a failed unwind start or readiness: the tool starts nothing more (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`; ruling R6 item 9);
- an unwind that finds the API failed with an empty job queue sends no stop to it (packet r6, ruling R6 item 3; a failed API counts as stopped only with an empty job queue, ruling R25 item 7, G1). As RA case 2 does (ruling R13 item 5), the archive rename and its reload still run, so the disk and the loaded configuration agree; no start is sent, and the run ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with `apiFailed` and `apiAtEnd` FAILED, the end row being the row of an archived and reloaded layer (ruling R19 item 4(d); ruling R21 item 5(d); ruling R13 item 3). An unwind stop that leaves the API failed takes the same road: the archive, the reload and no start (ruling R24 item 3, reading 3). On this road the run sets `stopProved` true with `apiFailed` true, because the stop read shows the API failed with an empty job queue, which counts as stopped (ruling R24 item 4, F5; ruling R25 items 2 and 7, G1). A failed API with a queued start job is not stopped (fix round 6, G1): the class check does not count it as stopped, the stop poll continues within its bound, sends no further command, and the end is not proved (ruling R26 item 5). A class check treats a failed API with a queued job like a running API and sends it the one stop of that step; when that stop is not proved, the end is `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` in A2 and `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` in RA, both with `stopProved` false (audit r10 against the fix round 6 core; ruling R27 item 3, P3-3). When no layer was written and no reload ran, the end row is L0-n: nothing is renamed, no reload runs and no start is sent, and the unwind sends no stop except one stop when its class read, after the proved first stop, finds the API not stopped (a process an outside actor started); the run ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with the failed API recorded in `apiAtEnd` FAILED and `stopProved` true; when the first stop left the API failed (kind STOP_FAILED, or a failed stop command whose snapshot read shows the API failed with no job) this end writes no `apiFailed` key, and when the unwind read or the unwind's one stop finds the API failed it writes `apiFailed` true (ruling R27 item 3, P3-4; ruling R28 item 2; ruling R29 item 2); the next step is Root review, and RA in row L0-n, with the reset-failed grant, can recover (ruling R22 item 5). No path sends a second stop command to one API process (ruling R29 item 2);
- a failed progress write of the unwind other than the pre-rename digest line: the tool starts nothing more and ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`, with the end row read from the files on disk and the reload label (row LP-n included; ruling R7 item 3, P3-2);
- an archived layer that cannot be confirmed, or a production-identity difference after the unwind reload: `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, nothing started (RD-32 form).

**Failed stop command** (ruling R13 item 4, amended by ruling R14 items 2 and 3). A command timeout ends as a stop not proved (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`). Any other failure of the first stop command gets one snapshot read, and A2 applies the same rule as RA case 5 with its codes: the end is a stop-not-proved end (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`; a snapshot that shows the API failed with no job counts as stopped and ends here too, ruling R14 item 3) unless the API is running with no job and the gate's main process. In that case nothing is started, the API stays running on `ba1a433` as found, its readiness is not re-proved, and A2 ends `A2_UNWOUND`, end row L0-n (ruling R14 item 2). If the adapter cannot tell a timeout from another failure, every failure takes the snapshot rule. The receipt records `stopCommandFailed` and `apiAtEnd`; for the `A2_UNWOUND` end it carries `stopCommandFailed` true, `startSent` false and `apiAtEnd`.

**Tree check before the unwind start, no false stop and the limit read** (ruling R19 item 4(a) to (c); ruling R21 item 5(a) to (c)). Right before the unwind start on the old tree (the `ba1a433` tree), A2 checks that tree with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) against its bound digest and file count. A mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start: the run ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with why TREE, `startRefused` `TREE` and `startRefusedAt` `UNWIND` (`TREE_UNKNOWN` does not arise in the A2 unwind; it occurs only in RA case 5), and the API stays stopped. The walk is counted in its own path sum (section 11.1, 4 seconds in the A2 unwind path 176). No end code claims that the API stopped without a proved stop. While readiness of that start is not proved (the readiness poll and any check before readiness of that start is proved; ruling R19a; ruling R23 item 3), any throw after a start command that is not followed by a proved stop ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with `stopProved` false. The scoped rule applies to every start command of the run (the unwind start, the main start and the market-branch start among them; the market-branch rules of section 6.4 otherwise stand; ruling R23 item 4). After readiness is proved, the existing codes stand: the A2 immediate-check and delayed-check codes (`A2_STOP_AT_CHECK` and `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`, section 6.7) and the READY rule (ruling R19a; ruling R22 item 2). After the in-run limit or, while unwinding, the unwind limit (section 11) is passed, the run makes one read-only class read of the API, capped to end before the host deadline minus the receipt-write reserve, and records `apiAtEnd`; when that read cannot run in time, `apiAtEnd` is `UNKNOWN` and `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT`, and no command follows. The receipt-write reserve is the 10-second timing margin, and the read needs more than its 5-second call time before the cap; `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT` when the cap forbids the read, or when the read fails at or after the cap, and `READ` when the read fails otherwise (ruling R24 item 3, reading 4; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-4). **Receipt fields**: `startSent` is run-level, true once any start command was attempted and never set back to false; a start that a check refused records `startRefused` (`TREE` or `TREE_UNKNOWN`; `TREE_UNKNOWN` occurs only in RA case 5) and `startRefusedAt` (the start it refused: `PUT_BACK`, `CASE5` or `UNWIND`); the receipt also carries `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` as defined here (ruling R25 item 2, which replaces the wording of ruling R23 item 2), `apiAtEnd`, `apiAtEndWhy` and `stopCommandFailed` (ruling R24 item 4, F5; ruling R22 item 9). **`stopProved`** is absent (the key is not written; read it as null) when the run sent no API stop command. It is true when, after the last API stop command, a stop read showed the API stopped or failed (ruling R14 item 3) and no start command was attempted after the last such read. Otherwise it is false. A stop read is either the stop's own proof poll or a stop-context read, such as the class read on the A2 failed-API road. A failed API counts as stopped only with an empty job queue, as an inactive API does (fix round 6, G1): a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped: the stop poll continues within its bound, sends no further command, and the end is not proved (ruling R26 item 5). Under G1, `apiAtEnd` can read `RUNNING` after a failed unit with a queued job. `apiAtEnd` is never proof, so a run can end with `stopProved` false and `apiAtEnd` `INACTIVE` or `FAILED`. The field is not recorded on a pass end (`A2_PASSED`, or `RA_API_READY` including the case 5 restart), because readiness of the started tree is proved there. **`marketStopProved`** is written only in the A2 market branch, and only when the branch sent its stop: true when that stop was proved, false when it was not; it is absent otherwise, and RA never writes it. When the branch sent a stop, that stop is the run's last stop and no start follows it, so both fields carry the same value; when the branch sent none, `marketStopProved` is absent and `stopProved` follows its own rule. **RA asymmetry, kept**: in RA case 5, where the stop command failed and the snapshot read shows the API stopped with no job, the run ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` with `stopProved` false, while the A2 counterpart (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`) records true; RA fails closed and follows the list of ruling R14 item 3 (section 12). The packet auditor checks both definitions at every assignment site against the fix round 6 core; where the code differs and the code is the safer reading, the text follows the code and Root records why, and otherwise Root rules a narrow code fix. RA uses the same fields, except `marketStopProved` (section 8).

**Termination** (ruling R12 item 5; ruling R14 item 4). A termination sends no command in any phase and starts nothing.
