Before the first effect (phase GATE) A2 ends `PRE_TERMINATED`, with no effect; after the first effect A2 ends `EFF_TERMINATED`. The first effect is the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload (ruling R15 item 3). Both ends carry the end row from the files on disk and the API state as read, and A2 never claims the API stopped; the progress record gets a `NOTE` line. **Effect flag and `effectPhase`** (ruling R24 item 3, reading 1): the receipt field `effectPhase` follows the creation of the record directory, while the snapshot, time-budget and termination codes take their `PRE_` or `EFF_` name from the effect flag, which flips before the first host-changing command is sent. Step codes are fixed names and can occur with the flag false: `EFF_PRE_ARCHIVE`, `EFF_PORT_BUSY`, `EFF_LOADED_CONFIG`, `EFF_PROGRESS_WRITE` and the `EFF_RECORD_` codes (ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-2). A termination between the record directory and the first host-changing step therefore ends `PRE_TERMINATED` with `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, and the runner evaluates it as a stop with an effect. RA reads the same two facts the same way (section 8).

After readiness there is no automatic rollback; a rollback needs GO-RA.

**Phases and error routing** (ruling R9 item 2). A run has four phases: GATE (no effect yet; the phase ends at the first effect, which is the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload; ruling R15 item 3), EFFECT (the first effect up to proved readiness), READY (readiness proved) and MARKET (a market change seen after the reload). In READY the tool sends no control command at all: no stop, start, reset-failed, reload, rename, archive or pending write. In MARKET the guard uses a consumable allowance, granted when the market change is seen (ruling R9a; ruling R10 item 2): the market branch's one start, its readiness poll and, after a failed start or readiness, one proved stop (in RA also one reset-failed under the optional clause, ruling R9 item 10g); nothing else is admitted in MARKET, and nothing at all in READY. This is enforced twice: in the error routing below, and by a guard in the control-command layer that refuses without sending when the ready flag is set, or when the market flag is set and the allowance for that command is spent (an internal assert that ends the run by phase). A reload is refused in MARKET and in READY. Phase START runs from the sending of the start command until readiness is proved or the start ends failed (ruling R12 item 6; ruling R14 item 12; section 8, case 7).
- A2 in READY: any throw, step-budget stop or in-run-limit stop at an immediate or delayed check ends `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK`, or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` when a market change was seen. A throw in the start or readiness poll after a market change ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` with no unwind and no extra reload.
- A termination sends no command in any phase; it ends `PRE_TERMINATED` before the first effect (phase GATE) and `EFF_TERMINATED` after it (ruling R12 item 5; ruling R14 item 4); the tool writes a progress `NOTE` line.
- The steps that section 6.4 names for the market branch itself (the start, the readiness poll and, after a failed start or readiness, the stop) are the allowance above: the guard admits each of them once and refuses every other command, also a second start or a second stop (ruling R10 item 2; revision 7 author reading (a), section 18).
- Tests inject a throw, and separately a step-budget stop and an in-run-limit stop, at every await point after readiness and after a market change, in A2 and in RA (every reachable row), and assert zero control commands after readiness together with the end code and the end row (section 15).

The market branch is not an unwind; its outcomes are named in section 6.4.

**A2 end outcomes** (ruling R6 item 9; S-P3-9). The A2 receipt records the end code and the end row (a row of the section 8 layer-state table, read from the files on disk and the staging API's need-reload flag at the end). A hard kill leaves no end code; the A2 progress record then holds the facts.

| Outcome | When | End row | API at the end | Next step |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| a gate refusal code (including a drift), `A2_NO_LAYER_NAME`, `PRE_INVENTORY_TOO_LARGE` or `A2_TIMER_WINDOW` | a stop before any effect (a refusal; a termination at the gate is the `PRE_TERMINATED` row below); also a budget stop, a clock refusal or a throw while the effect flag is false, which ends with the PRE code of the trigger (`PRE_TIME_BUDGET`), sends no command and runs no unwind; such an end still carries `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, and the runner evaluates it as `A2_STOP_EFFECT` with `hostEffect` EFFECT (section 6.6, "No effect yet"; ruling R24 item 4, F2; ruling R25 item 5) | L0-n (no file written) | running on `ba1a433`, unchanged | a contingency A2 job ID (section 3); after a drift, GO-AR first (section 6.3); after a `PRE_TIME_BUDGET` end no RA is needed: Root reviews the receipt and confirms zero control commands, then a fresh A2 gate under a contingency job ID with its own GO (the owner's current authority covers attempt a01 only) |
| `A2_PASSED` | the delayed check passed | LL-n | running on the pin | the owner's browser steps, A2M and Root's acceptance (section 6.9); RA only if needed |
| `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK` | an immediate or delayed check other than a market change fails on the pin (the receipt records which), or a throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop occurs at a check (phase READY; no command) | LL-n | running on the pin, as found | Root review; the owner decides on GO-RA (RA in row LL-n) |
| `A2_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`, `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` | section 6.4; for `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` also a throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop at a check after a market change was seen | LL-n | running on the pin | Root asks the owner first (section 6.4); GO-RA, GO-RC or GO-RM |
| `A2_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` | section 6.4, also a throw in the start or readiness poll after a market change (no unwind, no extra reload); a stop not proved ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`, and a start that leaves the API failed sends no stop | LL-n | stopped or failed on the re-pin layer | the owner decides; RA in row LL-n |
| `A2_UNWOUND` | the unwind started the API and it became ready; or a failed first stop command whose snapshot read shows the API running with no job and the gate's main process (nothing is started, the API stays as found and its readiness is not re-proved; the receipt carries `stopCommandFailed` true, `startSent` false and `apiAtEnd`; ruling R14 item 2) | L0-n (no layer written) or LU-n (the live layer or a pending file archived) | ready on `ba1a433`, or running on `ba1a433` as found after a failed stop command (readiness not re-proved) | Root review; the A2 receipt that reopens the chain (section 8) when its conditions hold, otherwise RA in row L0-n or LU-n; a next A2 only under a packet revision or Root ruling (section 9); after a failed first stop command this end never reopens the chain, because readiness is not re-proved: the next step is Root review, a next A2 needs a Root ruling or a packet revision (section 9), and RA in row L0-n stays available (ruling R15 item 4) |
| `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` | a named case above that leaves the API stopped or not proved stopped: a stop not proved after the read-only wait (also an error in the proof wait other than a termination), a failed stop command whose snapshot read does not show the API running with no job and the gate's main process (a snapshot that shows the API failed with no job counts as stopped and ends here; ruling R14 item 3), a failed API at the unwind, or an unwind stop that leaves the API failed (no stop for the first; the archive and its reload run; no start; `apiFailed` recorded, `stopProved` true (the failed API read with an empty job queue; fix round 6, G1) and `apiAtEnd` FAILED; ruling R19 item 4(d); ruling R24 items 3 and 4, F5; ruling R25 item 2; with no layer written and no reload run, nothing is renamed, no reload runs and no start is sent, the unwind sends no stop except one stop when its class read finds the API not stopped after the proved first stop, and the end records the failed API in `apiAtEnd`, with no `apiFailed` key when the first stop left the API failed (kind STOP_FAILED, or a failed stop command whose snapshot shows the API failed with no job) and with `apiFailed` true when the unwind read or the unwind's one stop found it failed; ruling R22 item 5; ruling R27 item 3, P3-4; ruling R28 item 2; ruling R29 item 2), a tree check right before the unwind start that finds a mismatch or an unreadable tree (nothing started; why TREE; `startRefused` TREE and `startRefusedAt` UNWIND; ruling R19 item 4(a)), a throw after a start command, while readiness of that start is not proved, that is not followed by a proved stop (`stopProved` false; ruling R19 item 4(b), scoped by amendment R19a; after readiness is proved the check codes stand), or a failed unwind reload, loaded-configuration check, unwind start or unwind progress write (other than the pre-rename digest line); this code also covers a market-branch end with the stop not proved, though no unwind ran (the receipt carries `marketStopProved` false, `stopProved` false (the branch sent a stop and no start followed it, so the two fields are equal) and `apiAtEnd`; ruling R13 item 6; ruling R25 item 2) | from the files on disk: L0-n, LP-n, LL-n, LL-r, LU-n or LU-r (for a failed API, the row of an archived and reloaded layer, or L0-n when no layer was written and no reload ran) | stopped, failed or not proved stopped; after the run limit `apiAtEnd` may be `UNKNOWN` (LIMIT) | RA in the row found (an -r row with the progress-record fact); after a failed API with no layer written and no reload run, Root review, and RA in row L0-n with the reset-failed grant can recover (ruling R22 item 5) |
| `A2_UNWIND_PROGRESS_FAILED` | the pre-rename digest line of a pending file cannot be written | LP-n | stopped | RA in row LP-n |
| `A2_UNWIND_FAILED` | an archived layer that cannot be confirmed, or a production-identity difference after the unwind reload | a recognised row, or none | stopped | RA only when the files form a recognised row and production equals the baseline; otherwise Root rules (section 9) |
| hard kill (no end code) | the host bound, a kill-after or a signal that is not caught | from the files on disk: L0-n, LP-n, LL-n, LL-r, LU-n or LU-r | any | RA after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds, from the receipt or the progress record |
| graceful termination before the first effect (`PRE_TERMINATED`; no end code) | a termination in phase GATE: no command is sent and no effect has happened (ruling R14 item 4); the phase ends at the first host-changing step (ruling R15 item 3) | L0-n (no file written) | as read | no command was sent; a progress `NOTE` line; Root review, handled like a gate refusal: no effect happened, so a next A2 attempt uses a contingency A2 job ID (section 3) only when GO-A2 names one, otherwise it needs a new GO (ruling R15 item 2); a `PRE_TERMINATED` end that came after the record directory was made carries `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, which the runner evaluates as a stop with an effect, and its next step is the same (ruling R25 item 5) |
| graceful termination after the first effect (`EFF_TERMINATED`; no end code) | a termination in any later phase: no command is sent and nothing more is started (ruling R12 item 5; ruling R14 item 4) | from the files on disk | as read; the tool never claims the API stopped | no command was sent; a progress `NOTE` line; Root review (for a termination at the pending write see section 12: the next RA then refuses `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED`); the owner decides on GO-RA |

### 6.7 Immediate checks

Every served URL fetched and byte-equal to the pin's manifest (including `index.html` with the pin's cache tokens); protected routes, including the three QR-2 paths, refuse without a session (ruling D17: this cannot show the flag; the loaded configuration shows it); health 200 in Paper mode; no Live signal in the heartbeat rows (a Live signal above zero refuses; ruling R12 item 7), the admin health `liveEnabled` false check being the owner's step (section 6.9; ruling R12 item 17); loaded code bytes equal to the staged tree; the staging API journal shows no fence, replication-role or schema refusal line (counts only); the trading worker the same process with a fresh heartbeat; research queue idle; the market units as in section 6.4 step 7 (a change ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`, section 6.4); production identity. A throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop at these checks sends no command and ends `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK`, or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` when a market change was seen (section 6.6; ruling R9 item 2). **Served-URL rule** (ruling R19 item 4(n); ruling R21 item 5(h)): the served list takes the P5a rule, a path with an optional `?v=<token>` cache token, with three tightenings (ruling R24 item 3, reading 6): at most 1024 bytes, no `..` segment and no leading `//`. A list in the real form (`/`, a page and assets with `?v=` tokens) passes in A2 and in RA; a query other than `v=<token>`, an absolute URL, a `..` segment, a missing leading `/`, a leading `//` and a path of more than 1024 bytes each refuse. A trailing slash and a `.` segment are inside the P5a character set and pass.

### 6.8 Delayed check (same job, about 300 seconds after readiness)

Same API main process, no restarts, no control-group events, health unchanged; the trading worker the same process with a fresh heartbeat; the stream the same process with no restart; the timer active with a next elapse (re-recorded); the collector service not failed (a market change ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK`, section 6.4); production identity. It reads the staging API's resident memory and the host's available memory as numbers (RC-Q36 "after switch" reading). A throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop at this check sends no command and ends `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK`, or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` when a market change was seen (section 6.6; ruling R9 item 2).

### 6.9 RC-Q36 target re-measure and the owner's browser check (required)

Contract RC-Q36 requires the target server to be measured again, with its memory headroom read, before the route is turned on anywhere. That was not recorded at P5a (ruling L1). A2 closes it (ruling R2 O-2 and O-3):
1. **Memory numbers:** the gate reading (before the switch), the delayed-check reading (after the switch) and the A2M reading (after the owner's requests, section 6.11), each with the API resident memory and the host's available memory. A2M also returns whether the QR-2 worker thread exists, so the reading with a computation in the process lifetime is known.
2. **Route timings.** After Root reports the job result, the owner opens the per-Bot report for Week (the Bot with fills; this request computes), Month and All Time in the owner's browser, through the owner's own port forwarding, which only the owner opens. Root reads only the request timings and status codes of those requests, view only, in the owner's Chrome session, and the read and compute times the panel shows. Root records the status code beside each timing, so that a 422 timing is not read as a compute timing. Root enters no credential and changes no setting. The timing record holds only the range label, the status code and the durations; it never holds a URL, port, query string, header, cookie or identifier (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-6).
3. **Owner check.** Week shows bar coverage complete; Month and All Time return the report, with bar coverage "not checked" (class C7) where the range is over a coverage budget; the texts are checked in English and in Thai; and the admin health shows `liveEnabled` false (a check that moved from the A2 immediate checks to the owner's step; ruling R12 item 17). The owner reports the `liveEnabled` value at the browser step, and Root records it in the A2 acceptance record (ruling R14 item 14). Ruling L1 Q6 applies: the `timestamp_rows` and `union_minutes` lines of the 422 hint are removed and the generic hint stays for the remaining 422 cases, which the local rehearsal covers (section 4, item 8).
4. **A2M.** Root runs A2M after steps 2 and 3 (section 6.11).
5. **Record.** Root records the memory numbers, the Week, Month and All Time timings with their status codes (range label, status code and durations only), the owner's report and the A2M result in the A2 result record.

After a market stop code of section 6.4, Root asks the owner first whether steps 2 to 4 run (section 6.4).

Root accepts A2 only after steps 2, 3 and 4. If A2M stops or its receipt is lost, Root reports and the owner decides; a new A2M needs a new GO line.

### 6.10 Receipt export and labels

The A2 receipt keeps hashed post-release API facts (working directory and argument digests) for a later binding update; this packet updates no binding. It records its end code and end row (section 6.6) and carries the gate inventory line and any pre-rename pending digest line (ruling R5 item 1; ruling R6 items 7 and 9). Where they apply it also records `startSent` (run-level, never set back), `startRefused` (`TREE` in A2; `TREE` or, in RA case 5 only, `TREE_UNKNOWN`) with `startRefusedAt` (`PUT_BACK`, `CASE5` or `UNWIND`), `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` (absent, true or false, as section 6.6 defines them; `stopProved` is not recorded on a pass end, and `marketStopProved` only in the A2 market branch), `stopCommandFailed`, `apiFailed`, `apiAtEnd` with `apiAtEndWhy` (`LIMIT` or `READ`), and the API class at a budget stop (ruling R13 items 3, 4, 6 and 9; ruling R19 item 4(b) and (c); ruling R22 item 9; ruling R23 items 2 and 7; ruling R24 item 3, reading 4; section 6.6). The private A2 receipt body is the RA manifest record form (ruling R9 item 11c), and the worst-case A2 receipt body (64 inventory entries, every progress line and the maximum prior records) fits the receipt frame limit, which a test asserts (ruling R9 item 9). It also exports the API main-process digest in the c1 form (section 5.1) at the immediate checks and at the delayed check, as the A2M reference (section 6.11). Every later GO, receipt, result record and handoff names both revisions: the staging API at the pin, the trading worker at `ba1a433`.

### 6.11 A2M: read-only memory job after the owner's browser steps

Ruling R2 O-3. A separate job under GO-A2, with its own job ID (attempt a01), manifest, consumed marker, nonce, receipt and envelope, built and accepted with the A2 and RA tool.
- **When.** After the owner's browser steps and Root's timing record, before the GO-A2 `notAfter`. If the browser steps end after that time, A2M needs a new read-only GO line.
- **Gate, read only.** Host health is recorded and never refused. A2M refuses only on another S6 job in flight or the staging API not active (ruling R9 item 6). The in-flight scan counts a `--input-type=module` process of another Node binary as a payload, and skips an ENOENT on a process executable link only when the process directory is also gone; otherwise the process is unknown and the scan fails closed (ruling R12 items 15 and 17).
- **Returns,** as numbers, booleans and one digest only: the staging API's resident memory; the host's available memory; the API main-process digest in the c1 form (section 5.1); whether the QR-2 worker thread exists (by a method the tool review accepts; an unknown answer, also a throw in that read, is recorded as unknown, never as false; ruling R9 item 6).
- **Process reference** (ruling R3 item 6; A-P3-3, S-P3-4). The A2M manifest is built before A2 and cannot hold the reference. Root compares the returned digest with the API process digest of the reviewed A2 receipt: the delayed-check process, or the immediate-check process when the delayed check did not run (`A2_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`, or `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` at the immediate checks). When that receipt holds no such digest, Root records the answer as unknown.
- **No effect.** No control verb, no reload, no environment read, no database connection. The tool region holds no control-command shape, reachable or not (ruling R9 item 6).
- **Writes.** One record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory, and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool.
- **Bounds.** Host bound 240 seconds (section 11.1). No retry.

### 6.12 AR reference reading (after a drift, at the A1 end reading, or once before GO-A2)

Ruling R3 item 1 (A-P2-2, S-P2-5). AR replaces the c6-form path through the retired collector families. It runs only after a process-identity drift stopped A1 or A2 at its gate or showed at A1's end reading (section 6.3), or once without a drift right before the GO-A2 request as the A2 reference (section 6.1 item 9; ruling R4 items 7 and 10), under its own GO-AR, with its own job family, job IDs (attempt a01 first), manifest, consumed marker, nonce, receipt, envelope and record directory. It is built, reviewed and accepted with the A2, A2M and RA tool, not with the A1 tool (ruling R9 item 13).
- **When.** After Root has reviewed the stopped A1 or A2 receipt (or the A1 receipt with a difference at its end reading) and reported the drift and its cost to the owner; or, without a drift, once right before or together with the GO-A2 request. Before the A1 request, the reference is c6 (the c1 export facts); AR is not run without a drift at any other time.
- **Reads, read only, in the c6 form of the collector start build** (copied by value, with the "no environment" read lists of section 6.2 for every unit; ruling R4 item 6): host health (recorded); no other S6 job in flight and no pending job (refuses otherwise); the settle wait of section 6.2 for a unit in automatic restart; production identity against the baseline (recorded); the class of the staging API, the trading worker, the research worker, the market stream, the fallback collector service and the fallback collector timer; the main-process digest in the c1 form (section 5.1) and the restart count of the staging API, the trading worker and the stream; the configuration and drop-in list digests of the staging API, the trading worker and the three market units; the timer's trigger count, accuracy, randomized delay, persistence, last trigger and next elapse; the collector service's result.
- **Refuses** only on a read that fails, another S6 job in flight, a pending job, or a unit still transitioning after the settle wait. Every other value is recorded, never compared. Host health is recorded and never refused (ruling R9 item 6). The in-flight scan follows the rule of section 6.11 (ruling R12 item 17).
- **Returns** states, counts, digests and booleans only.
- **No effect.** No control verb, no reload, no drop-in file, no environment read, no database connection, no delete. No file is read beyond unit properties and process start times. The tool region holds no control-command shape, reachable or not (ruling R9 item 6).
- **Writes.** One record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory, and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool.
- **Bounds.** P4 CHECK limits by value: host bound 240 seconds (section 11.1). No retry.
- **Root acceptance as a reference.** Root accepts the AR record as the process reference of the next A1 or A2 attempt, of A2 after a difference at the A1 end reading, or of A2 after the AR before GO-A2, only when production equals the baseline, the research worker is stopped, the staging API, the trading worker and the stream are running, the timer is active, the collector service is not failed, and the configuration digests equal their records (the staging API's the c1 `pairConfigDigests` value for the staging API, in the c6 form, as for the trading worker (ruling R6 item 1); the trading worker's configuration and drop-in list digests the c1 `pairConfigDigests` and `pairDropInDigests` values for the trading worker, in the c6 form, which c6 recorded as equal, as the A2 gate uses them (ruling R4 item 8; S-P3-4); the stream's the P4M m1 a03 manifest's market configuration digest of the stream, as in section 6.3 and section 6.13). Otherwise Root reports, and the owner decides; a changed configuration is not a drift and needs a packet revision.

### 6.13 Manifest sources (ruling R17 item 1; ruling R16 item 3)

Root builds every A2, A2M, RA and AR manifest and private binding. This subsection names, for each field that ruling R17 item 1 sources, for `api.envDigest` and `api.p5aFlagSha`, and for the stream configuration digest (ruling R21 item 6), the record and the field it comes from; every other field follows the section that names it. It names records and fields only, never a value, a path, a tree name or a digest beyond those already in this packet. Root copies each value by value from the named record into the private manifest or binding, checks the equalities noted here by equality only, and prints no value.

| Field (mode) | Source record and field |
| --- | --- |
| `hb.freshMs` (all) | The heartbeat window `heartbeatMs` of the accepted P5a tool and of the accepted R5a tool (the same window). |
| `hb.intervalMs` (all) | The interval input of sections 8 and 11.1 (the packet default), the same in every mode. |
| `u4Record` (all) | The U-4 record of section 10. |
| `retirementRecord` (A2, A2M, RA) | The retirement inventory record of section 10. |
| `retirementRecord` (AR) | The same record. No AR manifest is built before the inventory record exists; when an AR is needed before GO-A2, Root writes the inventory first (section 10). An AR needs its own GO-AR (section 14). |
| `api.oldTree` (A2, RA) | The working directory that the accepted P5a tool's post-switch configuration function (`postLoadedOf`) sets for the staging API: the P4 stage directory joined with the P1 tree name of `ba1a433`. The stage directory is the P4 x4 a02 binding field `p4.stageDir`, equal to the P1 a02 field `p1.apiStageDir` and the collector c1 field `pm.stageDir`. |
| `api.argvOld`, `api.nodePath` (A2, RA) | The start entry of the same function, path and arguments, computed from the P4 x4 a02 binding field `units.API.loaded` and the working directory above. This is the configuration that the P5a a01 gate passed. |
| `api.newTree` (A2, RA) | The A1 binding field `a1.apiStageDir` joined with the A1 final name for the pin (`jobNames.final`), exactly as the accepted A1 receipt reports it. |
| `api.listDigest`, `api.fileDigests`, `api.admissionOffSha` (A2, RA) | The accepted A1 record fields `dropIns.listDigest`, `dropIns.fileDigests` and `dropIns.admissionOffSha` (ruling R16 item 2(e)). `listDigest` must equal the collector c1 field `ready.pairDropInDigests.API`; a difference is a drift, and Root rules. |
| `api.envDigest`, `api.p5aFlagSha` (A2, RA) | The accepted P5a a01 record: the API environment digest without the QR-2 key, and `export.layerSha`. |
| `adp.apiPort`, `adp.selector` (A2, RA) | The P4 x4 a02 binding fields `p4.apiPort` and `p4.migrationSelector`, without the `form` key of the selector. These are the values that P5a used. |
| `adp.trees.OLD` (A2, RA) | The P5a a01 exec directory: `build-summary.json` fields `treeDigest`, `counts.treeFiles` and `authRoutes`, and `served-list.json` assets as `{url, size, sha256}`. P5a pinned both files (`pins.buildSummary`, `pins.servedList`). |
| `adp.trees.NEW` and `tree` (A2, RA) | The accepted A1 record and the A1 manifest package fields `treeDigest` and `treeFiles`. The served list and the auth routes come from the pin's Git blobs by the rule of section 2.1. The canonical tree form must equal A1's; Root checks the A1 receipt value. |
| `adp.sinceA2Sec` (RA) | The reviewed A2 receipt field `startedUtc`, in epoch seconds. After a lost A2 receipt, the A2 manifest field `dispatchedAt`, which is earlier and so gives a wider read. |
| `ra.records[].startedAt` (RA, PROGRESS record after a lost receipt) | That attempt's manifest field `startBy`: the latest possible start, so the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds counted from it is never shorter than the real one. |
| `baseline` (RA) and `ra.review.baseline` | The delayed check r6 a02 with its unit rows, while the latest receipt reports production equal. No accepted record holds per-unit rows of a later reading; after a production change or an `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, RA needs a Root ruling and a packet revision that names a source of unit rows (section 12). |
| Binding `channel` (all) | The accepted A1 private binding channel (equal to the c1 channel apart from the known-hosts path), with the known-hosts copy of this exec directory. |
| Binding `ra.recordRoot` and `ra.roles` (all) | The A1 binding fields `a1.recordRoot` and `a1.roles` (equal to the c1 field `pm.backupDir` and the P1 and P4 roles). The order of the four production units in the role list must match `baseline.units`. |
| Stream configuration digest (A2; Root's AR acceptance check, section 6.12) | The P4M m1 a03 manifest's market configuration digest of the stream (the stream's entry under `preconditions.market.configDigests`), which the m1 a03 run found equal (`configEqual` true). The P4M binding holds no digest field (ruling R21 item 6). |

The A2 manifest has no `argvMax` key after the follow-up round, and the runner then checks that `manifest.rolesSha` equals the hash of the canonical binding role list, before any host contact in every mode (ruling R17 item 4; ruling R19 item 4(l); ruling R21 item 5(j); ruling R22 item 9). Both are build items of the follow-up round (section 17); they are not built.

## 7. Layer rule

Live API drop-in order today, by role: earlier release layers; the P4 layer (working directory, start command, flag 0); the P5a flag layer (flag 1 only); the admission-off layer (five research flags 0), last.

**Re-pin layer text**, exactly: `[Service]`, `WorkingDirectory=<staged tree>`, `ExecStart=` (reset), `ExecStart=<argv>`. The argv is the P4 argv with only its last element replaced by the staged tree's server entry. The layer has no `Environment=` line.

**Name.** Matches the accepted layer-name form; sorts after every live layer other than the admission-off layer, including the P5a flag layer; sorts before the admission-off layer; collides with no live, pending or archived name. The accepted order rule is applied to the live list read at the gate, never to a constant. A name built from a fixed prefix plus revision hex can sort wrongly (main packet P4 step 2 hazard), so the name is derived from the live list. If no name fits, stop; the order rule changes only by ruling.

**Effective values after A2.**
- Working directory and start command: the setters in byte order are earlier layers, the P4 layer, then the re-pin layer, which is last, so the staged tree is in force.
- QR-2 flag: the P4 layer sets 0 and the later P5a flag layer sets 1; the re-pin and admission-off layers do not set it. Effective 1, from the P5a flag layer only.
- Research flags: 0 from the admission-off layer, still last.
- An environment-file value overrides a unit `Environment=` value, so the gate re-checks that no environment file sets the flag.

**Why the re-pin layer must not set the flag.** Setting 0 would turn the panel off. Setting 1 would make the flag depend on two layers, and R5a would no longer turn it off.

**After RA.** The re-pin layer is archived and not loaded; the loaded list equals the post-P5a list; the P4 layer is again the last setter of the working directory and start command, so the `ba1a433` tree is in force; the flag stays effective 1.

## 8. RA rollback (its own GO-RA)

Built and accepted together with A2 (rollback readiness). Copied by value from the accepted R5a tool, with named adaptations (ruling R2, P1-1, P2-3, O-7 and O-12; ruling R3 item 2: the layer-state table, the lost-receipt clause, the rollback-family rule and the trading-worker heartbeat; ruling R5: the A2 inventory line, the named end outcomes and the exclusion by end state; ruling R6: the case 5 immediate checks, code precedence, the one production rule, the named terminals, the progress lines, the L0 and LP recognition, the heartbeat by tick time and the crash-looping staging API; rulings R7 and R9: the P3 build requirements, the phase-aware routing, the lost-receipt records, the termination rule, the heartbeat interval and the shared inventory function; rulings R10, R12 and R13: the market-branch allowance, the termination rule without a command, the budget stop in the start phase, the restart-count rule, the two-sided HEALTH_503 rule, the tree walk and the put-back tree check).

- **Gate.** Host, production identity, staging API drop-in layers, environment sources (the same keys as A2, without the database connection key) and the database read only through the migration connection selector, as A2 (section 6.2, without the startup probe), against the Root-reviewed A2 receipt and the last accepted record before it, or under the lost-receipt clause below. Production baseline: the Root-reviewed A2 receipt, or the r6 a02 baseline when that receipt has no post-switch rows, was lost, or reports a production change Root has not reviewed. After a prior `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, the baseline is the production reading that Root's recorded review in the RA manifest names (ruling R6 item 4). A production-identity difference at the RA gate ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` before any effect (case 9). The research worker stopped. The staging API and trading-worker journals must be readable, and the R5a 60-second read must not be filled to the line cap; the read since A2 is recorded only (ruling R6 item 11; S-P3-3); their counts are recorded, never a stop. The settle wait of section 6.2 applies before the classification. The staging API is exempt from its refusal: a staging API still in automatic restart, in the activating state with a start sub-state, or with its own pending start, stop or restart job at the bound (for example a pin that crash-loops) is classified restarting and stopped, and its own job is not a pending job (ruling R6 item 11; S-P3-3; ruling R9 item 5); a staging API still in another transitioning state at the bound (for example still stopping) refuses before any effect, and Root rules (ruling R6 item 5). Any state of the re-pin layer that is not a row of the layer-state table below refuses before any effect. The `ba1a433` tree is checked exact with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) before the first database read; its digest and file count come from the on-disk measurement that the accepted P5a tool verified for that tree (ruling R12 item 10; ruling R13 item 10). The walk takes the P5a real-path form of section 6.2 (ruling R19 item 4(g)). The drop-in list digest, the per-file drop-in digests and the admission-off layer digest come from the accepted A1 record, as in section 6.2 (ruling R16 item 3); the P5a a01 record stays the source of the environment digest and the P5a flag layer digest. After a production change that Root reviewed, RA needs a unit-row source and a packet revision (section 6.13 and section 12).
- **Need-reload gate** (ruling R3 item 2; S-P2-1). No pending job of any unit other than the staging API's own start, stop or restart job (ruling R6 item 11; S-P3-3), and no unit needing a reload, except the staging API in a recognised need-reload row of the layer-state table. Any other unit that needs a reload refuses. A need-reload row is recognised only when the A2 record or a prior RA record shows a rename or archive of the live re-pin layer (A2's rename into place, the A2 unwind's archive, an RA archive or an RA put-back) without a completed reload after it in that record or a later one: rows LL-r, LU-r and LR-r (ruling R4 item 3; A-P3-3, S-P3-1). The source of that fact is the Root-reviewed receipt, or, when it was lost, the host progress record. A rename or archive labelled `ATTEMPTED` or `DONE` counts as shown when the disk holds the file in the matching form with the expected digest; a reload counts as completed only when labelled `DONE` (ruling R5 item 4; A-P3-1). A torn progress line counts as `ATTEMPTED` and the disk decides (section 6.4; ruling R9 item 10l). One torn last line with no line ending, at the end of the file, is read as an `ATTEMPTED` step of unknown kind; if the row's facts are missing because of it, row recognition refuses (ruling R12 item 15). The A2 and RA tools write these lines before and after every rename, archive, put-back and reload (sections 6.4 and 6.6 and Effects below; ruling R6 item 7; S-P3-1). L0-r, LP-r and LQ-r, and any "-r" row without such a record, refuse before any effect, because the reload would load a change from outside this packet (section 12).
- **Classification gate for the units RA does not control** (P1-1; R5a unit-class style). The market stream, the fallback collector service, the fallback collector timer and the trading worker are each in a known class: running (with any main process), inactive, or failed. The class, the main-process continuity and the restart count of each are recorded, never compared with the c6, A1, AR or A2 values. Only an unknown state, a state still transitioning after the settle wait, or a pending job of any unit other than the staging API's own start, stop or restart job, refuses. A running stream whose process digest cannot be read refuses before any effect (`PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT`), as in A2 (ruling R13 item 13; ruling R19 item 2; ruling R21 item 5(f)).
- **Trading-worker heartbeat** (ruling R3 item 2; A-P2-1; ruling R4 item 5, A-P3-4, S-P3-2). The adaptation of the R5a heartbeat refusal (`PRE_HEARTBEAT_STALE`) and the R5a heartbeat check (`CHK_HEARTBEAT`: the own fresh row, no other fresh row, no Live-mode row): the RA gate records the heartbeat row count and freshness and refuses only on an unreadable queue, a Live-mode row, a Live signal above zero, an empty set of rows of the trading worker role while the trading worker runs, or a fresh row that the tick-time rule does not admit. The queue is also unreadable when any row lacks a string role (`HEARTBEAT_UNREADABLE`). Mode and Live are checked on all rows, unfiltered, and the gate and the immediate checks refuse on a Live signal above zero (`liveSignals` must be a non-negative integer, otherwise it is unreadable). At the RA gate an empty set of rows of the trading worker role refuses when the trading worker runs and is recorded when it does not (ruling R12 item 7). **Tick-time rule** (ruling R6 item 10, which replaces ruling R5 item 8; S-P3-2): a heartbeat row holds a per-instance identifier, a tick and a role, not a process identity, so the rule uses the tick time of the accepted form. A row is "own" when its tick is at or after the classified trading worker's main-process start minus one second (at the immediate checks, the start of its current main process); more than one fresh own row refuses; a fresh non-own row counts as a previous process's row, and is recorded, not refused, only when its tick is before that start or, with no running trading worker, its tick does not advance across two reads at least twice the heartbeat interval plus 250 ms apart; any other fresh non-own row refuses before any effect. Where RA requires the trading worker's own heartbeat (the immediate checks below), the R5a rule of exactly one own row is kept for a running trading worker, so no guard is lowered. A stale heartbeat is recorded and never refused, in every class, also for a running trading worker; this is a named relaxation of R5a `PRE_HEARTBEAT_STALE`, because RA gives the trading worker no command. The RA immediate checks keep "no other fresh row" (under the tick-time rule) and "no Live-mode row" in every class, and require the trading worker's own fresh heartbeat, as exactly one own row, only when it was fresh at RA's gate and the trading worker is still the same process; otherwise they record it. RA admits the states after GO-RC (collector service and timer stopped), after GO-RM (stream, collector service and timer stopped), with a failed collector service, after a stream restart and after a trading-worker restart.
- **Heartbeat reads and interval** (ruling R7 item 4, whose environment override is replaced by ruling R9 items 10m and 11b). The core filters the heartbeat rows to the trading worker role for the own-row and freshness rules only; mode and Live are checked on all rows (ruling R12 item 7). The heartbeat interval is a manifest input from 50 to 5000 ms (the worker interval defaults to 250 ms at the pin, as ruling R7 item 4 set, and the default stays); it replaces only the environment override of ruling R7 item 4 (ruling R10 item 3(f)). The two reads that test for advancing are at least 2 × interval + 250 ms apart, and the timing proof asserts the A2 and RA gate budgets (section 11.1) at the 5000 ms maximum interval.
- **Hooks and links.** The two-way rule of section 6.3, with the same property lists, in every class. Every unit property read of RA other than the host-side environment-source read of the staging API's own environment property (digest only) uses the "no environment" read lists of section 6.2; the build asserts this for the class, process, configuration and hooks read lists, and asserts that the environment property is read only in the host-side process (ruling R4 item 6; ruling R5 item 6, A-P3-3).
- **Timer window, only when the timer is active** (named adaptation, O-12). The rule of section 6.3 with RA's window W_RA = 167 seconds (section 11.1): the next elapse must be later than now plus W_RA plus the accuracy and randomized delay; otherwise a read-only wait of at most 420 seconds, then a stop before any effect (`RA_TIMER_WINDOW`). When the timer is inactive or failed, no timer-window wait applies.
- **Collector run kept out of the reload.** A collector run in progress at the gate is waited out, read only, within the same 420-second bound, otherwise a stop before any effect. After the proved API stop and before the archive, RA re-checks (at most 5 seconds) that no collector run and no job is pending; a run in progress gets a read-only wait of up to 15 seconds.
- **API state (R5a unit-class rule).** The staging API may be running, restarting (also in automatic restart, in the activating state with a start sub-state, or with its own pending start, stop or restart job, at the settle bound, for example a crash loop; ruling R6 item 11; ruling R9 item 5), stopped or failed; only an unknown state, or another state still transitioning at the settle bound, refuses. A failed API is cleared with reset-failed right before its main start only, never before an unwind start, and only under the GO-RA reset-failed clause. The manifest carries a boolean `resetFailedGranted`, true only when the GO-RA carries that optional clause; a failed API with the flag false refuses before any effect with `PRE_RESET_FAILED_NOT_GRANTED` (ruling R9 item 10g).
- **Layer-state table** (ruling R3 item 2; S-P2-1, S-P2-3). RA decides its row from the files on disk and the staging API's need-reload flag, never from an in-memory flag. In every row, before any effect, the staging API drop-in files on disk must equal the row's expected list and digests (the post-P5a list, plus the re-pin layer in rows LL); a need-reload row is recognised only then, and only with the progress-record fact of the need-reload gate above. In a recognised need-reload row, RA's one reload is the reconciling reload. **Compared file set** (ruling R4 item 4; A-P3-2): a row compares the live drop-in list and the files that carry the re-pin layer's derived name in its pending or archive form. Every other file of the drop-in directory (every file other than the live drop-in list and the pending and archive forms of the derived name; for example archives of earlier packets) is compared by count and digest with the A2 gate inventory (section 6.2), read from the Root-reviewed A2 receipt or, under the lost-receipt clause, from the gate line of the A2 host progress record, never from the P5a a01 record (ruling R5 item 1; A-P2-1, S-P2-2); RA reads it with the shared function of section 6.2 (digests sorted non-decreasing, duplicates allowed, at most 64; ruling R9 item 8); any difference refuses before any effect.

  | Row | Files on disk | API needs a reload | RA action after the proved stop and the pre-archive re-check |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | L0-n | none: no re-pin layer, no pending file, no archive (A2 never wrote), admitted only when no listed A2 or RA record shows a write, rename or archive of the re-pin layer, and any listed write, rename or archive line of it, labelled `ATTEMPTED` or `DONE`, refuses (ruling R6 item 8; S-P3-7; ruling R7 item 3, P3-1) | no | no file change, no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | L0-r | as L0-n | yes | refuses before any effect (no progress-record fact; revision 4; section 12) |
  | LP-n | pending file only (A2 ended after the pending write and before the rename); any digest; admitted only when no listed A2 or RA record shows a rename of it: a rename counts as shown only when labelled `DONE`, or labelled `ATTEMPTED` with the file renamed on disk, and an `ATTEMPTED` line with the pending file still pending is row LP (ruling R6 item 8; S-P3-7; ruling R7 item 3, P3-1) | no | record the pending file's digest in the progress record, then archive the file by no-clobber rename (no delete; ruling R5 item 5); no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LP-r | as LP-n | yes | refuses before any effect (no progress-record fact; revision 4; section 12) |
  | LL-n | live re-pin layer with the layer digest; no pending file; no archive (A2 ended after the rename, or an earlier RA put the layer back or stopped before it changed the layer) | no | archive the layer by no-clobber rename; one reload; loaded-configuration gate (the normal path) |
  | LL-r | as LL-n (A2 ended after the rename and before its reload, or an RA put-back reload failed or was not reached), admitted only with the progress-record fact | yes | archive the layer by no-clobber rename; one reload, which is also the reconciling reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LU-n | archived by the A2 unwind, which the A2 receipt or the A2 progress record shows (archive digest equal to the layer digest for an archived live layer, or to the pre-rename pending digest line, from the reviewed A2 receipt or, when that receipt was lost, from the A2 progress record, for an archived pending file); no live layer; no pending file (ruling R5 item 5; S-P3-1, S-P3-2; ruling R6 item 7, A-P3-6) | no | no file change, no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LU-r | as LU-n (the unwind reload failed or was not reached), admitted only with the progress-record fact | yes | the reconciling reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LR-n | archived by an earlier RA attempt (archive digest equal to the layer digest; that attempt's record shows its row LL), admitted under the rollback-family rule; no live layer; no pending file | no | no file change, no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LR-r | as LR-n, admitted only with the progress-record fact | yes | the reconciling reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LQ-n | a pending file archived by an earlier RA attempt (archive digest equal to that attempt's pre-rename pending digest line, from its reviewed receipt or, when that receipt was lost, from its progress record (ruling R6 item 7; A-P3-6); that attempt's record shows its row LP), admitted under the rollback-family rule; no live layer; no pending file (revision 4) | no | no file change, no reload; loaded-configuration gate |
  | LQ-r | as LQ-n | yes | refuses before any effect (no progress-record fact; revision 4; section 12) |

  Any other combination (for example a live layer with another digest, a pending file beside a live layer or an archive, two archives within the compared file set, or a drop-in file set that differs from the row) refuses before any effect. An archive-form file of the derived name that no listed A2 or prior-RA record explains refuses before any effect (ruling R5 item 5; S-P3-1). Files that look like row L0 while a listed A2 or RA record shows a write, rename or archive line of the re-pin layer (labelled `ATTEMPTED` or `DONE`), and files that look like row LP while a listed record shows its rename (labelled `DONE`, or labelled `ATTEMPTED` with the file renamed on disk), refuse before any effect, and Root rules (ruling R6 item 8; S-P3-7; ruling R7 item 3, P3-1). Ruling R9 item 11d accepts the archive suffix forms; this packet names no file form, and RA decides the row from the files on disk and the records, never from a suffix alone (ruling R10 item 3(g)). RA archives the layer itself only in rows LL; only there can the put-back of cases 1 and 2 run. The pending-file archive is never put back; rows LP and LQ count as "never written" for the case table (ruling R4 item 2; S-P2-2, A-P3-1). Rows LR and LQ are told apart by the prior attempt's record of its own row (LL or LP), never by the digest alone, because a complete pending file has the layer digest. No job of this packet deletes a file, so a rename or archive of the live re-pin layer always leaves a file of the derived name in its live or archive form; no progress record can therefore show the need-reload fact beside the files of rows L0, LP or LQ, and rows L0-r, LP-r and LQ-r always refuse (ruling R4 item 3). After the file action and any reload, every row continues with the loaded-configuration gate, production identity, the market re-check, the start and readiness, and the immediate checks.
- **Lost A2 receipt** (ruling R3 item 2; S-P2-2). With no Root-reviewed A2 receipt, RA works as R5a does with no P5a receipt: it reads the A2 host progress record on the host; it takes the post-P5a drop-in list digest from the accepted A1 record (ruling R16 item 3) and the environment digest from the accepted P5a a01 record (the re-pin layer has no environment line, so the digest is unchanged), the layer digest from the A2 manifest, the production baseline from the r6 a02 baseline, and the compared file set from the gate inventory line of the A2 host progress record, never from the P5a a01 record; with a readable record that holds no such line, A2 had no effect and no layer exists, so RA refuses before any effect and Root reports (ruling R5 item 1; A-P2-1, S-P2-2); an unreadable or missing A2 progress record refuses before any effect and needs a packet revision (ruling R6 item 5; S-P3-5); and it classifies from the files on disk under the layer-state table. A row whose facts the progress record does not show refuses. RA after a lost A2 receipt is admitted only after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from the A2 start, so that the A2 host process has ended (section 11.1). **Records** (ruling R9 item 10h): a record whose source is PROGRESS (A2 or RA) carries sha null and the job ID. RA reads the progress record that the job ID names in the dedicated protected record directory, checks the job ID and the form, and records the record's sha256 in its gate line and in its receipt; Root reviews that record afterwards. A record whose source is RECEIPT needs a sha and non-null lines.
- **Rollback-family rule** (ruling R3 item 2; S-P2-3; ruling R4 items 1 and 2, A-P2-1, S-P2-1, S-P2-2; ruling R5 items 2, 3 and 5, S-P2-1, S-P3-1, S-P3-3, S-P3-5, S-P3-6, A-P3-2; ruling R6 items 2, 4 and 5). The five sentences below are used word for word in section 9, the owner summary and GO-RA.
  - **Outcomes.** RA outcomes: `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` and `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` end with the API ready on `ba1a433`; `RA_UNWOUND`, `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`, `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, `RA_API_NOT_READY`, `RA_API_STOPPED`, `RA_BUDGET_STOP`, `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` and `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` end in a recognised layer state without the API proved ready on `ba1a433` by RA's immediate checks; `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` are excluded.
  - **Next RA.** After an RA attempt that did not end with the API ready on `ba1a433`, also after a termination, which never counts as an end with the API ready and is followed by the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds, Root may request the next RA attempt under a new GO-RA that lists the prior attempts; the tool admits it only when the files on disk form a recognised layer state, the prior records show the facts that state needs, and production equals the baseline.
  - **Exclusion.** Excluded: after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (an archived layer could not be put back) Root rules by a packet revision before any next RA; after `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (a production-identity difference read at any RA step, at the gate before any effect, the RD-32 state) no next RA is admitted without Root's recorded review of the difference in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against.
  - **Terminal RA and the accepted RA receipt.** An RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433`, other than with `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, is terminal for the RA family, and no next RA follows it; its receipt is the accepted RA receipt, for the RX tripwire and the return of R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC, when its end row is L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n, the post-P5a configuration is loaded, production is unchanged and the API is ready, after Root accepts it, with a Root review first for a market or check stop; a `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` in such an end row may be accepted by Root in the same way after its review of the difference.
  - **A2 receipt that reopens the chain.** A Root-reviewed A2 receipt with end row L0-n or LU-n, no pending file, the API ready on `ba1a433`, the post-P5a configuration loaded and production unchanged reopens that chain in the same way, with no RA.

  The tool refuses before any effect when a prior attempt's record shows `RA_UNWIND_FAILED`, an outcome with the API ready on `ba1a433` other than `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (`RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` or `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, also after case 5), or `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` without Root's review of the difference recorded in the manifest. A Root ruling after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` acts only through a packet revision (ruling R6 item 5; A-P3-3). A terminated attempt has no end code and never counts as an outcome with the API ready, also when its progress record shows readiness; the next RA is admitted after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from that attempt's start (ruling R6 item 5; S-P3-5). The gate applies this wait to every prior RA record whose end code ends in `_TERMINATED` (`PRE_TERMINATED` and `EFF_TERMINATED`), as it does to a PROGRESS record; a terminated record with no start time refuses (ruling R24 item 4, F3; reading 2). The RA manifest binds the last prior RA attempt (its job ID and the digest of its receipt; for a lost receipt a PROGRESS record with sha null and the job ID, which the tool reads and whose sha256 it records; ruling R9 item 10h), as the R5a manifest binds its prior attempt (ruling R5 item 5; S-P3-1). The next attempt reads the prior attempt's Root-reviewed receipt, or, when that receipt was lost, its host progress record after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from that attempt's start, and takes its row from the files on disk: row LL when the layer is live (put back, or never archived; the layer digest from the A2 manifest); row LR when an RA attempt archived the live layer; row LQ when an RA attempt archived a pending file; otherwise row LU, LP or L0. Without that prior-attempt fact, an archive by an earlier RA is not a recognised row and refuses. This is the only contingency rule of this packet after an attempt with effects.
- **States with no automatic next step** (ruling R6 item 5; A-P3-3, S-P3-5). Each of these needs Root before any next RA, by the path named: (a) a terminal RA receipt (`RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` or `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, also after case 5) that Root does not accept: a Root ruling or a packet revision; (b) `RA_UNWIND_FAILED`: a packet revision; (c) a staging API still transitioning at the settle bound in a state other than automatic restart, or the activating state with a start sub-state (for example still stopping): RA refuses before any effect, and Root rules; (d) a repeated case 4 in an -n row with no progress (the next RA is admissible, but it runs no reload, so a lasting loaded-configuration difference fails it again): a Root ruling; (e) a lost A2 receipt with an unreadable or missing A2 progress record: RA refuses before any effect, and a packet revision decides. After `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` the next RA waits for Root's recorded review in its manifest, or Root accepts a receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` after its review (case 9). A terminated RA attempt is not such a state: it never counts as an end with the API ready, and the next RA follows after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds.
- **Effects.** Stop the API and prove the stop when it runs or restarts (100 seconds, no grace; if not proved or the API becomes failed, no further control call; an error in the proof wait other than a termination, also a snapshot fault or a budget stop, counts as not proved and sends no further command, and RA ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`; a command timeout also ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`, and any other failure of the stop command gets one snapshot read, with `RA_UNWOUND` (case 5: nothing started, the API on the configuration found, a failed API recorded) when the snapshot shows the API running with no job and the gate's main process or failed with no job, otherwise `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` (ruling R14 item 3; a failed API counts as stopped only with an empty job queue, so a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped, the stop poll continues within its bound, sends no further command and the end is not proved: section 6.6; ruling R25 item 7, G1; ruling R26 item 5); if the adapter cannot tell a timeout from another failure, every failure takes the snapshot rule; the receipt records `stopCommandFailed` and `apiAtEnd`; ruling R13 items 3 and 4); the pre-archive re-check; the file action of the layer-state row (archive the live re-pin layer, or a pending file, by no-clobber rename; never a delete); one manager reload when RA archived the live re-pin layer or the row needs the reconciling reload (ruling R4 item 11); loaded-configuration gate (drop-in list equal to the post-P5a list, working directory and start command of the `ba1a433` tree from the P4 layer, QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer, research flags 0, no heap option); production identity; market re-check against RA's own gate reading (each market unit in the same class as at RA's gate; a stream that ran at the gate the same main process with no restart; a timer that was active still active; a collector service that was not failed not failed); start the API (with the restart-count rule of section 6.4); readiness; immediate checks against the `ba1a433` served manifest, with the trading worker's heartbeat required only as stated above. Health at the immediate checks is 200 in Paper mode; the token HEALTH_503 (a 503 with Paper-only mode) is accepted only in RA, and only when the trading worker shows the same not-running class (INACTIVE or FAILED) at the RA gate read and at the immediate read, and A2 never accepts it; both directions are tested, the gate running and now stopped, and the gate stopped and now running, and a different not-running class at the two reads is not accepted (ruling R12 item 8, amended by ruling R13 item 16 and ruling R19 item 4(e)). RA gives the market units and the trading worker no command in every class. Before every rename, archive, put-back and reload, RA writes an fsynced `<step> ATTEMPTED` progress line with the expected source and target form and the expected digest, never a name, and after the step a `<step> DONE` line; the pending-file digest line of row LP is such a line, and the RA receipt carries it (ruling R6 item 7; S-P3-1, A-P3-6). A failed or partial progress write is followed by a fresh line and a `NOTE TORN_PREVIOUS` line, and the reader counts the torn step as `ATTEMPTED` and lets the disk decide (section 6.4; ruling R9 item 10l). **Phases** (ruling R9 item 2): in READY (the API proved ready) RA sends no control command at all: no stop, start, reset-failed, reload, rename, archive, put-back or pending write. In MARKET (a market change seen) the guard admits only a consumable allowance, granted when the change is seen (ruling R9a; ruling R10 item 2): the branch's one start, its readiness poll, after a failed start or readiness one proved stop, and one reset-failed under the optional clause; nothing else in MARKET, and nothing at all in READY. The error routing of cases 8 and 10 and a guard in the control-command layer enforce this, and a termination in any phase sends no command (a progress `NOTE` line). **Termination** (ruling R12 item 5, which withdraws the split of ruling R10 item 3(b)): a graceful termination never sends a command, in any phase, so there is no stop, no put-back and no start; RA ends `PRE_TERMINATED` before the first effect (no effect; ruling R14 item 4) and `EFF_TERMINATED` after it (the first effect is the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload; ruling R15 item 3); the RA family treats `EFF_TERMINATED` as "termination (no end code)", and it never counts as the API ready (ruling R14 item 17). Both ends carry the end row from the files on disk and the API state as read, and RA never claims the API stopped; RA writes a `NOTE TERMINATED` progress line and the result says terminated. The effect flag and `effectPhase` read as in section 6.6: only the snapshot, time-budget and termination codes take their `PRE_` or `EFF_` name from the effect flag (step codes such as `EFF_PRE_ARCHIVE`, `EFF_PORT_BUSY`, `EFF_LOADED_CONFIG`, `EFF_PROGRESS_WRITE` and the `EFF_RECORD_` codes are fixed names and can occur with the flag false; ruling R25 item 5), `effectPhase` follows the record directory, and a termination between the two ends `PRE_TERMINATED` with `effectPhase` EFFECT and a made record, which the runner evaluates as a stop with an effect (ruling R24 item 3, reading 1). A step-budget or in-run-limit stop keeps case 7. **Tree check before every start** (ruling R19 item 4(a); ruling R21 item 5(a)): right before each start RA checks the tree that start uses with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) against its bound digest and file count: the put-back start (cases 1 and 2, as below) and the case 5 restart (case 5, as below). A mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start; each walk is counted in its own path sum (section 11.1: the RA put-back unwind 176 and the case 5 restart path 259 each hold a 4-second walk). **No false stop and the limit read** (ruling R19 item 4(b) with amendment R19a; ruling R21 item 5(b); ruling R22 item 2): no end code claims that the API stopped without a proved stop. While readiness of that start is not proved (the readiness poll and any check before readiness of that start is proved; ruling R23 item 3), any throw after a start command that is not followed by a proved stop ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`. The scoped rule applies to every start command of the run; the put-back start, the case 5 restart and the market-branch start are examples, and the market-branch rules of case 8 otherwise stand (ruling R23 item 4). After readiness is proved, the existing codes stand: `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, the case 5 and case 10 codes, the READY rule (no command at all) and the check phase. After the in-run limit or, while unwinding, the unwind limit (section 11) has passed, RA makes one read-only class read of the API, capped to end before the host deadline minus the receipt-write reserve, and records `apiAtEnd`; when that read cannot run in time, `apiAtEnd` is `UNKNOWN` and `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT`, and no command follows. The receipt-write reserve is the 10-second timing margin, and the read needs more than its 5-second call time before the cap; `apiAtEndWhy` is `LIMIT` when the cap forbids the read, or when the read fails at or after the cap, and `READ` when the read fails otherwise (ruling R24 item 3, reading 4; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-4). **Receipt fields** (ruling R19 item 4(c); the fields of section 6.6): `startSent` is run-level and never set back to false; a start that a check refused records `startRefused` (`TREE` or `TREE_UNKNOWN`; `TREE_UNKNOWN` occurs only in RA case 5) and `startRefusedAt` (`PUT_BACK`, `CASE5` or `UNWIND`); the receipt also carries `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` (absent, true or false, as section 6.6 defines them), `apiAtEnd`, `apiAtEndWhy` and `stopCommandFailed` (ruling R25 item 2; ruling R22 item 9). In RA `marketStopProved` is never written: the market-branch stop of that field is the A2 stop. `stopProved` is not recorded on `RA_API_READY`, case 5 restart included. In RA case 5, where the stop command failed and the snapshot read shows the API stopped with no job, the end is `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` with `stopProved` false, while the A2 counterpart records true (ruling R25 item 2; section 12).
- **Failure cases (R5a case table, by value with the RA names; end codes named by ruling R5 items 2, 3 and 5; the case 5 checks, code precedence and the one production rule by ruling R6 items 2 to 4).** Before the API is ready, a failure unwinds as follows. The unwind decides from the files on disk, never from a flag (R5a ruling D34 item 3): a rename of the live layer that is on disk counts as archived, also when the step then threw (case 1, or case 7 for a time-budget stop or a failed progress write); a rename of a pending file that is on disk followed by a throw is the row LP sub-case of case 4; an archive step with no rename on disk is case 5.
  1. When RA archived the layer itself (the rename is on disk) and the archive step, the manager reload, the loaded-configuration check or the production-identity check fails, RA renames the layer back, runs one more reload and checks the A2 configuration and the production identity; it starts the API on it only if the API was running when RA began, otherwise the API stays stopped with the re-pin layer in place (`RA_UNWOUND`, row LL-n). If the put-back reload or the A2 configuration check after it fails, RA starts nothing (`RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`; row LL-r when the reload is not labelled `DONE`, otherwise row LL-n). If the start or readiness after the put-back fails, RA stops the API, proves the stop and starts nothing more (`RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, row LL-n). When a production-identity difference has been read, the put-back and its reload still run and RA then starts nothing (`RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, case 9; ruling R6 item 4). Right before the put-back start of cases 1 and 2, RA checks the staged tree with the lane walk (the P5a walk copied by value, with the P5a EXACT rule) against the bound digest and file count of the staged tree; a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start, and RA ends `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` with why TREE, `startRefused` TREE and `startRefusedAt` `PUT_BACK`, the API staying stopped or failed (`startSent` is run-level and keeps its value; ruling R21 item 5(c); ruling R22 item 9). The walk's worst case enters the budget table and the timing proof (section 11.1; ruling R13 items 10 and 11).
  2. When the API start or readiness fails after RA archived the layer itself and the API was running when RA began, RA stops the API, renames the layer back, reloads and starts the API on the A2 configuration (`RA_UNWOUND`, row LL-n); the put-back sub-outcomes of case 1 apply. If the API becomes failed during that stop, the put-back rename and its reload still run, so the disk and the loaded configuration agree, and no stop and no start follows; RA ends `RA_UNWOUND` with the failed API recorded, which the outcome table allows. The words "no further control call" in packet r6, ruling R6 item 3, mean no command to the API unit; the manager reload is not one (ruling R13 item 5).
  3. In every other case of a failed start or readiness, RA stops the API and leaves it stopped with the re-pin layer archived (or never written, in rows L0, LP and LQ) (`RA_API_NOT_READY`; end row LR-n, LU-n, LQ-n or L0-n); the rollback-family rule applies.
  4. When the re-pin layer was already archived or never written (rows L0, LP, LQ, LU and LR) and the reconciling reload, the loaded-configuration check or the production-identity check fails, RA changes nothing further and starts nothing, so an API that was running stays stopped (`RA_API_STOPPED`; end row LU-r or LR-r when the reconciling reload is not labelled `DONE`, otherwise L0-n, LQ-n, LU-n or LR-n). A time-budget stop or a failed progress write at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r counts as a failed reconciling reload (`RA_API_STOPPED`, end row LU-r or LR-r unless the reload is labelled `DONE`), not case 7 (ruling R7 item 3, P3-4). With a production-identity difference this is the RD-32 state (case 9; ruling R5 item 2, S-P3-3). In row LP, when the rename of the pending file is on disk and the step then throws for a reason other than a time-budget stop or a failed progress write (for example the directory fsync or the digest re-read), RA likewise changes nothing further and starts nothing (`RA_API_STOPPED`, end row LQ-n; ruling R6 item 3; S-P3-6).
  5. When RA stops before it has changed the layer (a busy port after the proved stop, as section 6.6 defines it, a pre-archive re-check whose run has not ended after the 15-second wait, or a time-budget stop, a failed progress write or an archive failure at the stop or archive step, with no rename on disk), it starts the API again on the configuration it found, only when the API was running when RA began (`RA_UNWOUND`, end row the row RA found). Right before that restart RA checks the tree it uses with the lane walk against its bound digest and file count; a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start, and RA ends `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` with why TREE, `startRefused` TREE and `startRefusedAt` `CASE5` (ruling R19 item 4(a); ruling R21 item 5(a); ruling R22 item 9). The same ending holds for a refusal for `TREE_UNKNOWN` below. No start is open then, so RA calls no stop-if-runs check and sends no command after the refusal, and the end code stays `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`; the API stays as the case 5 stop left it. The row's disk reads and one capped end class read (`apiAtEnd`) still run after the refusal, as for every end (ruling R24 item 4, F4, as built and accepted by ruling R25 item 4). A time-budget stop of either prefix in the PRECHECK or FILE phases, before the rename, is case 5 and is answered here before the budget branch of case 7 (ruling R25 item 3). No budget stop can occur at the record directory or the progress file: a failure there ends with its own `EFF_RECORD_` or `EFF_PROGRESS_WRITE` code (ruling R27 item 3, P3-5). In rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n the configuration found is the post-P5a configuration, so RA then runs the immediate checks, like the main path, and ends `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` (case 10) or `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (case 9) in the row it found, and never `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` (ruling R6 item 2; A-P3-1; ruling R7 item 3, P3-5); in the other rows the restart ends `RA_UNWOUND`. If that start or readiness fails, RA stops the API, proves the stop and starts nothing more (`RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, the same row). The case 5 immediate checks run only in rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n, so `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` after a case 5 restart is possible only there; in every other row case 5 ends `RA_UNWOUND` with no immediate checks, and an unreadable row there gives `RA_UNWOUND` with `rowNone`. With an unknown loaded tree (the loaded working directory read gives neither tree) case 5 sends no start and ends `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` with `treeUnknown` true, `startRefused` TREE_UNKNOWN and `startRefusedAt` `CASE5` (ruling R13 item 12; ruling R21 item 5(c); ruling R22 item 9). `TREE_UNKNOWN` occurs only here.
  6. When RA cannot put an archived layer back (the archive is missing or has another hash), it ends `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and starts nothing, also when the API was stopped at the start of RA. This is the only use of `RA_UNWIND_FAILED`; the files then form no recognised row (ruling R5 item 2; S-P2-1).
  7. A time-budget stop or a failed progress write later in the effect rows, after a file action, other than at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r (case 4; ruling R7 item 3, P3-4), ends `RA_BUDGET_STOP` with nothing started. A `PRE_TIME_BUDGET` stop routes like `EFF_TIME_BUDGET` at the same step (ruling R25 item 3, as built; it replaces the F1 text of ruling R24 item 4). Case 5 answers before the budget branch: a budget stop in the PRECHECK or FILE phases, before the rename, ends `RA_UNWOUND` with no command (case 5); after the port check it ends `RA_BUDGET_STOP`. **No effect, no command** (fix round 6, G2; ruling R25 item 7): when the effect flag is false at a trigger in RA, no command is sent; in particular there is no stop-if-runs check in START with the flag false, also when the API reads running because an outside actor started it. The end codes with the flag false are these (ruling R26 item 3, as built; scope by ruling R27 item 2). Cases 4, 5 and 9 keep their own codes: a budget stop or a failed progress write at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r ends `RA_API_STOPPED` (case 4); a budget stop, a failed progress write or an archive failure before the rename ends `RA_UNWOUND` (case 5); a non-budget trigger at the pre-archive check, the loaded-configuration check, the re-check or the production-identity check ends with the case 5, 4 or 9 code of that step. Outside those cases, a non-budget trigger at the start step ends `RA_API_NOT_READY`; a non-budget trigger at the start step of the market branch ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY`; any other budget or progress trigger ends `RA_BUDGET_STOP`; a termination ends with the termination code. A non-budget trigger at a start step is a failed read before its command; an argument-shape refusal of a start records `startSent` true, the safe direction, and cannot occur with the bound shapes (ruling R27 item 3, P3-5). These codes, with `startSent` false and no `stopProved`, mean the host is as the gate found it. **Reading a no-command RA end** (ruling R26 item 4): the RA receipt does not record the effect flag, so Root reads a no-command RA end from the trigger, from `startSent` false, from the absence of `stopProved` and from a progress record with no archive or reload line. One exception: a gate that read class FAILED, with the reset-failed grant, can end `RA_API_NOT_READY`, or `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` in the market branch, with one benign reset-failed command sent and `startSent` false (ruling R27 item 3, P3-1). Such an end needs no unwind. The next step is Root's review of the receipt, then a fresh RA gate under its own GO; this is the RA analogue of the A2 end form in section 6.6. In row LL, after RA's own archive (the rename on disk), RA first runs the case 1 put-back and one more reload, with no start (the R5a deadline behaviour), and the end row is LL-n, or LL-r when that reload is not labelled `DONE` (case 6 when the layer cannot be put back) (ruling R6 item 3; A-P3-2). In the other rows the end row is decided from the files on disk, and a row whose facts the records do not show refuses the next RA under the need-reload gate and the lost-receipt clause (ruling R5 item 2; S-P2-1). A stop that is not proved within 100 seconds, at the first stop or in an unwind, ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`: no further control call and nothing started; the end row is decided from the files on disk. **Case 7 in START** (ruling R12 item 6; ruling R13 item 9): after the start command is sent and before readiness is proved (phase START), a step-budget or in-run-limit stop first gets one proved stop. The case 7 put-back in row LL runs only after a proved stop, and no start follows. If the stop is not proved, the end is `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED`. When the in-run limit, not the readiness step, runs out inside the readiness poll, the core throws the budget code and takes this route, and the receipt records `startSent` and the API class. A budget stop during the first stop's proof sends no further command, and RA ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` (ruling R13 item 3). **Code precedence** (ruling R6 item 3; A-P3-2; ruling R14 item 3): `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` is used for exactly these: a command timeout of any stop command (the first stop, the START stop of ruling R12 item 6, or the stop before the case 2 put-back; ruling R15 item 6), a stop not proved within its wait (100 seconds), an error in the proof wait (ruling R13 item 3), a failed stop command whose snapshot shows neither of the two no-job states (the API running with no job and the gate's main process, or the API failed with no job), and a throw after a start command, while readiness of that start is not proved, that is not followed by a proved stop (the scoped throw rule of Effects above; amendment R19a; ruling R23 items 4 and 6). An API that becomes failed keeps the code of the case in which that happens, with no further control call, and the receipt records "API failed"; at the first stop, before any file action, that is case 5 (`RA_UNWOUND`, the row RA found, nothing started).
  8. **Market change at the post-file-action re-check** (A2 rule, O-7; retitled by ruling R5 item 5, because the re-check also runs in rows without a reload): if the market re-check finds a market unit changed from RA's own gate reading, RA does not unwind and runs no extra reload: it gives the market units no command, starts the API on `ba1a433`, runs the immediate checks and ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`; if an immediate check then fails, the end stays `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` (case 10) with `marketChanged` true (ruling R9 item 10f). Root reports; the owner decides on GO-RC or GO-RM. If, in this branch, the API start or readiness fails (also a throw in the start or readiness poll after a market change, with no put-back and no extra reload; ruling R9 item 2), RA runs no unwind and no extra reload and gives the market units no command; it stops the API if it runs and proves the stop (100 seconds, no grace), starts nothing, and ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` with the API stopped (or failed) on the post-P5a configuration, in any row (the row that RA's file action left, for example LR-n from row LL; ruling R5 item 5, S-P3-4) (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-5). This stop is the one stop of the market allowance, and a reset-failed before the branch's start is its one reset-failed (ruling R9a). When the start leaves the API failed with an empty job queue, RA sends no stop and the failed state counts as stopped (packet r6, ruling R6 item 3; a failed API with a queued start job is not stopped, ruling R25 item 7, G1); the receipt records the failed state (ruling R13 item 8). An unreadable stream at the market re-check is a failure (`EFF_STREAM_UNREADABLE`), not a market change (ruling R12 item 15; ruling R22 item 4: `PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT` is the code of the RA gate before any effect only; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-1: `STREAM_UNREADABLE` without a prefix is only the A2 immediate-check token). Root reports; the owner decides, and the rollback-family rule applies.
