  9. **RD-32 state** (ruling R5 item 2, S-P3-3; one rule by ruling R6 item 4, A-P3-4, S-P3-4): a production-identity difference read at any RA step after the gate means RA starts nothing from that point and ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, which takes the place of every other end code; in row LL after RA's own archive, the case 1 put-back and its reload still run, with no start. A difference read at the RA gate ends `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` before any effect. After it, the next RA is admitted only with Root's recorded review of the difference in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against. A difference read at the immediate checks leaves the API running and ready on `ba1a433`; Root may accept that receipt after its review (the terminal sentence above).
  10. **Immediate-check failure after readiness** (ruling R5 item 3; S-P3-5): if an immediate check other than the production identity fails, throws, or hits a step-budget or in-run-limit stop after the API is ready on `ba1a433` (also after the case 5 restart; ruling R6 item 2), RA gives no command to any unit, runs no unwind and no reload, leaves the API running and ends `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK`, also after a market change, with `marketChanged` true (ruling R9 items 2 and 10f); Root reviews it.

  At most two reloads. Root reports every case that leaves the API stopped, and the owner decides. The rollback-family rule above decides the next step after every outcome (ruling R4 item 1; ruling R5 item 2).

  **RA end outcomes** (ruling R5 items 2 and 3; ruling R6 items 2 to 5). Every outcome names its end row; the next step follows the rollback-family rule above. With all immediate checks passed, RA ends `RA_API_READY`. The RA receipt carries `marketChanged` in every end (ruling R9 item 10f).

  | Outcome | Case | End row | API at the end | Next step |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `RA_API_READY` | none (immediate checks passed), or 5 in rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n | L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n | ready on `ba1a433` | terminal; the accepted RA receipt after Root accepts it |
  | `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` | 8 (never case 5) | L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n | ready on `ba1a433` | terminal; Root review first; the owner decides on GO-RC or GO-RM |
  | `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` | 10 (a failed check, a throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop at a check), also after case 5 and after a market change | L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n | ready on `ba1a433` | terminal; Root review first |
  | `RA_UNWOUND` | 1, 2, 5 | LL-n (cases 1 and 2) or the row RA found (case 5: a restart in rows other than L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n, no restart because the API was not running at RA's start, or a failed API at the first stop, or a failed stop command whose snapshot shows the API running with no job and the gate's main process or failed with no job; a budget stop of either prefix before the rename, which case 5 answers first (no command when the effect flag is false; ruling R25 item 3); case 2 with a failed API, recorded) | running on the configuration found or put back when it was running at RA's start, otherwise stopped, or failed (recorded) | next RA |
  | `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED` | 1, 2 | LL-r, or LL-n when the reload is labelled `DONE` | stopped | next RA |
  | `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` | 1, 2, 5 (also why TREE at the put-back tree check and at the case 5 restart tree check, and `treeUnknown` in case 5; `startRefused` is `TREE`, or `TREE_UNKNOWN` in case 5 only, and `startRefusedAt` names the start refused, `PUT_BACK` or `CASE5`) | LL-n, or the row RA found (case 5) | stopped or failed | next RA |
  | `RA_API_NOT_READY` | 3; also a non-budget trigger at the start step with the effect flag false (G2: no command; ruling R26 item 3) | LR-n, LU-n, LQ-n or L0-n | stopped or failed; as the gate found it when `startSent` is false and no `stopProved` is recorded | next RA; after a no-command end Root reviews the receipt and a fresh RA gate runs under its own GO, no unwind (ruling R26 item 4) |
  | `RA_API_STOPPED` | 4, also row LP with the rename on disk followed by a throw, and a time-budget stop or failed progress write at the reconciling reload in rows LU-r and LR-r | LU-r or LR-r, otherwise L0-n, LQ-n, LU-n or LR-n; LQ-n after row LP | stopped | next RA; a repeated case 4 in an -n row with no progress: Root rules |
  | `RA_BUDGET_STOP` | 7, with a `PRE_` or an `EFF_` budget-stop code alike, after the port check; before the rename case 5 answers first and the end is `RA_UNWOUND` (ruling R25 item 3); with the effect flag false no command is sent (G2) | LL-n or LL-r after the put-back in row LL; otherwise from the files on disk | stopped or failed | next RA when the records show the row's facts |
  | `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` | 7, also the first stop (a command timeout, an error in the proof wait other than a termination, or a failed stop command whose snapshot read shows neither the API running with no job and the gate's main process nor the API failed with no job) and a stop in START or the stop before the case 2 put-back, and any throw after a start command, while readiness of that start is not proved, that is not followed by a proved stop (ruling R19 item 4(b), scoped by amendment R19a; after readiness is proved the check codes `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` and the case 5 and case 10 codes stand) | the row RA found, or the row its file action left | not proved stopped; after the run limit `apiAtEnd` may be `UNKNOWN` (LIMIT) | next RA (the gate classifies the API) |
  | `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` | 8; also a non-budget trigger at the start step of the market branch with the effect flag false (G2: no command; ruling R26 item 3) | any row | stopped or failed; as the gate found it when `startSent` is false and no `stopProved` is recorded | next RA; after a no-command end Root reviews the receipt and a fresh RA gate runs under its own GO, no unwind (ruling R26 item 4) |
  | `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` | 6 | no recognised row | stopped | excluded; Root rules by a packet revision |
  | `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` | 9, at any step, also at the gate (before any effect) and at the immediate checks after the case 5 restart | any | any; ready on `ba1a433` after a difference at the immediate checks | next RA only with Root's recorded review in its manifest, which sets its production baseline; with the API ready on `ba1a433`, Root may accept the receipt after its review |
  | termination before the first effect (`PRE_TERMINATED`; no end code) | the RA gate (no command, no effect; a `NOTE TERMINATED` line; the result says terminated) | the row RA found, from the files on disk | as read | Root review; the next RA follows after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from that attempt's start; never counts as an end with the API ready (ruling R15 item 2) |
  | termination after the first effect (`EFF_TERMINATED`; no end code) | any later phase (no command: no stop, no put-back, no start; a `NOTE TERMINATED` line; the result says terminated) | from the files on disk | as read; never claimed stopped | next RA after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds; never counts as an end with the API ready, also after readiness |
- **Bounds.** API gap bound 380 seconds; host bound 1200 seconds (section 11.1); from the stop command RA needs at least 478 seconds remaining, measured against the in-run limit of 1140 seconds (the host bound 1200 minus 60), otherwise it stops before any effect (ruling R6 item 12; ruling R7 item 3, P3-3). While unwinding, RA uses the unwind limit of section 11, as A2 does (ruling R22 item 9). The 100 seconds of a stop after a failed unwind start are host time, not API gap, and the gap bound stays 380.
- **Data.** None. QR-2 audit rows written by panel use stay, and `ba1a433` reads them.
- **Record directory.** One record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory, and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool. The worst-case RA receipt body (64 inventory entries, every progress line and the maximum prior records) fits the receipt frame limit, which a test asserts (ruling R9 item 9).

## 9. Rollback, the RX rule and the full-rollback order

| Step | In-run rollback | Later rollback | GO | Data |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A0 | none | none | none | none |
| A1 | none (inert; a partial stage is left inert and reported) | none | inside GO-A1 | none |
| A2 before readiness | Unwind (section 6.6) | RA; A2 again only under a packet revision or Root ruling that defines the next A2 job ID (ruling R5 item 3); after `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, RA only when the files form a recognised row and production equals the baseline, otherwise Root rules | inside GO-A2 | none |
| A2 after readiness | none | RA; A2 again only under a packet revision or Root ruling that defines the next A2 job ID (ruling R5 item 3) | GO-RA | QR-2 audit rows stay |
| A2M | none (read only) | none | inside GO-A2 | none |
| AR | none (read only) | none | GO-AR | none |
| RA | Unwind (section 8) | A2 again only under a packet revision or Root ruling that defines the next A2 job ID (ruling R4 item 11); the next RA attempt under the rollback-family rule of section 8 and a new GO-RA, as the RA outcome paragraph below states (ruling R4 item 1; ruling R5 items 2 and 3) | inside GO-RA | none |
| R5a, R4, R4H, RX while the re-pin layer is live | Not requested or admitted | GO-RA first, then the chain below | as below | as those packets |

**Why GO-RA comes first.** R5a, R4 and R4H refuse at their gates, before any effect, on a drop-in list that differs from their expected list, and they require the market units inactive. RX classifies only the P4 and P5a layers. With an extra live layer, RX would stop every running B2 writer (the market stream, the collector timer and service, the API and the trading worker), archive the P4 and P5a layers and reload, then fail its loaded-configuration gate and end `STOPPED_BEFORE_SWAP` with nothing restarted. So, while the re-pin layer is live, Root requests and admits no GO-RX (ruling R1 item 7). This is a Root rule, not a mechanical refusal. Root records it in the A2 receipt review, the handoff memory and every later GO-RX request ("RA receipt first").

**RA is admissible in every market-unit class** (ruling R2, P1-1). GO-RC or GO-RM before GO-RA is admissible: RA classifies the market units and the trading worker, records their class, and gives them no command in every class (section 8).

**RX tripwire** (ruling R2, P3-8). While the re-pin layer is live, Root writes a consumed marker line for the next RX job ID (P4 RQ-17 form), so a mistaken RX dispatch stops at its consume step before any effect. Root issues a fresh RX job ID only after an accepted RA receipt, or a Root-reviewed A2 receipt that reopens the chain (below; ruling R5 item 3). This does not replace the later RX gate hardening.

**Full-rollback order after A2.** GO-RA first; GO-RC or GO-RM may come before it. Then the existing chain:
- to turn the panel off or move the pair back: GO-RM (R5a, R4 and R4H need all three market units inactive), then GO-R5a, then GO-R4 or GO-R4H;
- or a full restore: GO-RX, with GO-RC or GO-RM first only when a market unit is failed.

**RA outcomes, exclusion and the accepted RA receipt** (ruling R5 items 2 and 3; ruling R6 items 2, 4 and 5; the words of section 8). RA outcomes: `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` and `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` end with the API ready on `ba1a433`; `RA_UNWOUND`, `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`, `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, `RA_API_NOT_READY`, `RA_API_STOPPED`, `RA_BUDGET_STOP`, `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` and `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` end in a recognised layer state without the API proved ready on `ba1a433` by RA's immediate checks; `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` are excluded. After an RA attempt that did not end with the API ready on `ba1a433`, also after a termination, which never counts as an end with the API ready and is followed by the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds, Root may request the next RA attempt under a new GO-RA that lists the prior attempts; the tool admits it only when the files on disk form a recognised layer state, the prior records show the facts that state needs, and production equals the baseline. Excluded: after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (an archived layer could not be put back) Root rules by a packet revision before any next RA; after `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (a production-identity difference read at any RA step, at the gate before any effect, the RD-32 state) no next RA is admitted without Root's recorded review of the difference in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against. An RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433`, other than with `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, is terminal for the RA family, and no next RA follows it; its receipt is the accepted RA receipt, for the RX tripwire and the return of R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC, when its end row is L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n, the post-P5a configuration is loaded, production is unchanged and the API is ready, after Root accepts it, with a Root review first for a market or check stop; a `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` in such an end row may be accepted by Root in the same way after its review of the difference. A Root-reviewed A2 receipt with end row L0-n or LU-n, no pending file, the API ready on `ba1a433`, the post-P5a configuration loaded and production unchanged reopens that chain in the same way, with no RA.

After an accepted RA receipt, or such an A2 receipt, R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC are valid again with their own rules; RX needs a fresh job ID and manifest (tripwire above); the families retired in section 10 stay retired (ruling R3 item 6; S-P3-7).

**Restore source** (ruling R2 O-10). The full-restore source is the P4 pre-switch backup taken at P4 attempt x4 a02 (2026-10-26T04:49:30Z). It is not refreshed by this packet (ruling R1 item 9). The GO-A2 request shows its UTC time and age. A full restore loses every write since it, including Paper rows, panel audit rows and market bars.

**Later item.** Harden the RX gate to refuse on any unknown live drop-in before its first effect (a separate packet).

## 10. Retirement inventory (before GO-A2)

Rule (main packet P4 step 1; ruling D24; P4 RQ-17 form): Root writes one consumed marker line per family namespace for any open contingency job ID, and pins a new inventory record in the A2, A2M and RA manifests and in every AR manifest built after it. The A2, A2M, RA and AR manifests also pin the U-4 record (below). No AR manifest is built before the inventory record exists; when an AR is needed before GO-A2, Root writes the inventory first, which this section allows at any time before GO-A2 (ruling R17 item 1). Reason: these families' gates bind the pre-repin API layout and are not needed after RA.

**Retire** (open contingency IDs): S6 P0a, P0b, P0c, P1, P3, P4-X4, P4-R6 and P4-R24, P5A-ON, P4M m1 and m6, collector diag d1, collector c1 and c6. Families already retired by ruling D24 (S07, R7B, DP1S, R3) stay retired by reference.

**Keep admissible:** R4, R4H and R5a (they refuse before any effect while the re-pin layer is live); RX (under the rule and the tripwire of section 9); RM and RC (the market-unit stop paths); the families of this packet, including AR, which takes over the c6-form reads after the collector c1 and c6 families are retired (ruling R3 item 1; S-P2-5).

**Inventory (ruling R2 O-5).** Root lists the actual open contingency IDs of these families from the local execution records, writes the marker lines, and pins the inventory record before GO-A2.

**U-4 precondition of GO-A2 (blocker; ruling R2 O-4).** Before GO-A2 Root confirms, from the accepted RM and RC snapshots and not the build sources: (a) that their gates compare no pair digest and no configuration digest of the staging API; (b) whether their GO-record check pins the GO wording. Both GO lines name "pinned revision ba1a433" for the pair. Root records both results. If (a) fails, GO-A2 is not requested until a packet revision. If (b) shows a pinned wording, Root records before GO-A2 how a later GO-RM or GO-RC names both revisions.

**U-4 result** (Root, 28 October 2026; record sha256 `d01105a2119b320f26a9e1b0f504be3865ae38ff036747e95b68adc886b92e93`). Basis: a read-only check of the two accepted snapshots by the architecture auditor (Opus 5.5 / high), and Root's own read of its prep scripts. RM: the build snapshot accepted by ruling D42 (tool hash `7b7ec494`; Root's SHA256SUMS `de3351fc`, 66 of 66 OK). RC: the build snapshot accepted by ruling D51 (tool hash `af4efe23`; Root's SHA256SUMS `ce953a6c`, 75 of 75 OK).
- **(a) PASS for both RM and RC.** Neither gate compares a pair digest or a configuration digest of the staging API. RM reads the API only through state rules (host floors, a pending-job refusal and a need-reload refusal), and its effects are stop and reset-failed on the three market units only. RC does the same for the two collector units; it binds the m1 a03 facts but only shape-checks them, and uses only the stream digest and the restart count. No host region of either tool calls `gPair`, `pairFacts`, `SHOW_TARGET` or the API health URL. U-4 (a) does not block GO-A2.
- **(b) Not pinned in the tools; pinned by the packet templates and Root's GO records.** Tool checks: `ownerGoReference` is any string of 1 to 255 characters and is never compared with GO text; `manifest.pin` and the host binding pin must equal the tool constant `ba1a433`; the runners require the follow-up packet `ebc707c8` (RM) or the collector packet `a296989e` (RC), plus the main packet `e8a18a90`, byte-equal under `docs/`. Root's prep scripts check the GO record's `interpretation.pin` prefix against the tool pin `ba1a433`.
- **Rule for a later GO-RM or GO-RC while the re-pin layer is live.** (1) Keep every template clause from "in one SSH session" onward. (2) Name both revisions in the GO-A2 style: the RM or RC tool pinned to revision `ba1a433`, "while staging runs the mixed-revision pair of packet S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1 A2 job [ref]: API pin [pin] (33e5a8c) (... D: yes) on the staging API, and trading worker revision ba1a433 (includes QR-2 O7: yes), neither of which this step stops, restarts or changes; reversing [P4M | c1] job [ref]: ...". (3) In the GO record keep `interpretation.pin` as `ba1a433` and add `apiPin: 33e5a8c` and the A2 job reference; `manifest.pin` stays `ba1a433`. (4) After an accepted RA receipt, the original templates are true again.
- **Retirement-inventory token rule.** Never write an RM or RC family token into a `manifest*.json` or `GO_CONSUMED*.json` file, and never mark RM or RC consumed. The local record directories of this packet (`s6-staging-api-repin-*`) are scanned by the RM and RC admission scans, at about 16100 and 14400 entries today against a cap of 100000.

## 11. Durations and bounds

- **A0:** local; no host time.
- **A1:** under 1 minute of job time (P1 attempt a02 took about 14 seconds for two trees). Host bound 300 seconds, carried by value from the accepted P1 revision 2 limits.
- **A2:** about 6 to 8 minutes including the delayed check, plus the timer-window wait when needed (at most 420 seconds) and the settle wait when needed (at most 30 seconds). Host bound 1200 seconds. API gap expected 4 to 13 seconds, bound 310 seconds (covers a failed switch at readiness followed by an unwind whose stop needs the read-only wait, with the pre-write check and wait and the market re-check inside the gap). No dump time is added.
- **A2M:** under 1 minute. Host bound 240 seconds. No gap.
- **AR (after a drift, or once right before the GO-A2 request):** under 1 minute, plus the settle wait when needed (at most 30 seconds). Host bound 240 seconds. No gap.
- **RA:** about 3 to 5 minutes, plus the timer-window wait when the timer is active (at most 420 seconds) and the settle wait when needed (at most 30 seconds). Host bound 1200 seconds. API gap expected 4 to 13 seconds, bound 380 seconds.
- **Lost-receipt wait:** 1335 seconds for every family of this packet.
- **Unwind limit** (ruling R19a; ruling R22 item 9): the host bound minus 10 seconds (1190 seconds for the 1200-second bound of A2 and RA), or the host deadline when that is earlier. A2 and RA use it while unwinding. This definition changes no section 11.1 number.
- **Tool payload:** the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool has a ceiling of 262144 bytes in each of the four modes (A2, A2M, RA and AR); the build asserts the size with a worst-case manifest, and before each GO request Root runs the payload-size check with the real manifest (ruling R9 item 11a; ruling R10 item 3(i)).
- **Heartbeat interval:** a manifest input from 50 to 5000 ms (ruling R9 item 11b; section 8).
- **Owner browser steps after A2:** a few minutes, at the owner's convenience, while the port forwarding is up, and before the GO-A2 `notAfter` so that A2M can run under it.
- No gap for the trading worker, the market stream or the collector.

## 12. Residual risks and unknowns

The risks of main packet section 11, follow-up packet section 9 and collector packet section 6 apply. Added or changed by this packet:
- **First manager reload with a running stream and collector timer.** No reload has yet run on this host while the stream or the calendar timer was active. A reload may recompute the timer's next elapse. Persistence is off in the c1 gate record and the gate refuses any other value, so the reload cannot start a catch-up run. Mitigations: the timer-window rule, the pre-write re-check, the empty job list at the reload, and the re-checks after the reload, after the start and at the delayed check. A market change after the reload ends the job after readiness for an owner decision (section 6.4).
- **Process identity drift.** A normal stream restart, or a staging API or trading-worker restart, since the process reference (c6 or an AR record for A1; the A1 record or an AR record for A2) stops A1 or A2 before any effect and spends that GO (section 6.3). Root's path: GO-AR, then a new GO for the next attempt under a contingency job ID; the GO wording does not change. Cost: one extra read-only GO and the new GO of the next attempt. The stream restarts on a venue tick or step change, so this path is likely; the reference chain through A1 shortens the window to the time between A1 and A2. Revision 4 (ruling R4 items 7 and 10): the A2, A2M and RA tool is accepted before GO-A1 is requested, so that window holds no build or review; Root may request one AR without a drift right before the GO-A2 request, and GO-AR and GO-A2 may come in one owner message; the GO-A2 request states the observed stream restart rate. A difference of the staging API, the trading worker or the stream at the A1 end reading leads to GO-AR and, after Root's acceptance, to the staged tree with the AR record as the A2 reference, with no new A1. A transitioning collector service, timer or research worker at the A1 gate is a pre-effect stop, not a drift.
- **Mixed-revision pair.** The API runs the pin and the trading worker runs `ba1a433`. The worker's loaded bytes do not change and the neutrality record proves its closure equal, but the scan reads literal specifiers only; a path built at run time would escape it. Every later GO, receipt, binding update and handoff names both revisions; GO lines that name only "pinned revision ba1a433" for the pair need the U-4 rule of section 10 (recorded on 28 October 2026).
- **RM and RC availability while the re-pin layer is live** (U-4 record, 28 October 2026). RM and RC stay admissible, but each of these residuals makes them refuse before any effect; none is a safety risk: a pending staging-API job (RM and RC exempt only their own units); a staging API that needs a reload (after `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, a lost receipt, or the RA need-reload rows); a production-identity change after the shared reload (a reviewed re-baseline is needed); a layer line with a link, a hook or an `Environment=` entry (the A2 layer must stay the exact four lines of section 7); and any edit of the follow-up, collector or main packet file under `docs/`, which gives `PIN_MISMATCH` (put both-revisions notes only in this packet, in rulings or in the Roadmap).
- **Restore source age.** The P4 pre-switch backup ages regardless of this packet, and two market writers now write continuously. A full restore loses more with each day. A refresh is a separate online-backup decision.
- **Layer-name room.** Unproven until A1 returns the boolean. A false boolean blocks A2; a change of the order rule needs a ruling and a revision.
- **Admission-off layer content** (ruling R16 item 1). No accepted record holds the content digest of the admission-off layer or the per-file drop-in digests. A1 reads them first and exports them privately (section 5.2); until Root accepts the A1 record, the A2 and RA manifests cannot be filled with them.
- **RA baseline after a production change** (ruling R17 item 1). The only accepted record with per-unit production rows is the delayed check r6 a02, which RA uses while the latest receipt reports production equal. No accepted record holds per-unit rows of a later reading. After a production change or an `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, RA needs a Root ruling and a packet revision that names a source of unit rows (section 6.13).
- **RX rule is procedural.** RX itself does not refuse while the re-pin layer is live; the Root rule and the consumed-marker tripwire prevent it. The RX gate hardening is a later packet.
- **Hooks and links through targets.** The two-way rule reads direct links only.
- **RC-Q36 memory with a computation.** The gate and delayed-check readings precede the owner's computing requests; A2M reads the memory after them and whether the QR-2 worker thread exists. The worker bounds hold meanwhile: one computation per API process, a worker thread with 256 MB old generation and a 10-second deadline.
- **D pin reach.** With D, Month beyond the coverage budgets, Year and All Time financial reports compute on the shared host for the first time (re-map case 4), and so does a range within 30 days with a data-time stop on a degradable coverage limit (section 2.2); the owner's All Time request gives the first staging timing. The financial read limits and the QR-2 worker bounds still bound them, and the A2 gate checks Root's case 4 memory estimate (section 2.4).
- **RA reconciling reload.** In a need-reload row RA loads drop-in files that it has checked on disk against the row; a unit-file change outside the drop-in files is not seen before the reload, and the loaded-configuration gate after the reload is the check (case 4: nothing started). Revision 4 (ruling R4 item 3): a need-reload row is admitted only when the A2 or a prior RA progress record shows a rename or archive of the live re-pin layer without a completed reload. A staging API that needs a reload for any other reason (for example a unit-file change outside this packet, which shows as row L0-r, LP-r or LQ-r) refuses before any effect, and Root decides, by a ruling or a packet revision, how that state is reconciled.
- **Rollback-family rule.** A repeated RA after an RA attempt with effects is admissible only under that rule; each attempt needs its own GO-RA, and each can leave the API stopped. Revision 5 (ruling R5 items 2 and 3) excludes by end state: every RA outcome without the API ready on `ba1a433` that ends in a recognised layer state, with the prior records showing the needed facts and production equal to the baseline, has an admissible next RA, and an RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433` is terminal. Revision 6 (ruling R6 item 5; A-P3-3, S-P3-5) names every state with no automatic next step and its path: a terminal RA receipt that Root does not accept (a Root ruling or a packet revision); `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (case 6; a packet revision); `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (the RD-32 state; a next RA only with Root's recorded review in its manifest, which sets its production baseline, or, with the API ready on `ba1a433`, Root's acceptance of the receipt after its review; ruling R6 item 4); a staging API stuck transitioning past the settle wait in a state other than automatic restart, or the activating state with a start sub-state (a Root ruling); a repeated case 4 in an -n row with no progress (a Root ruling); and a lost A2 receipt with an unreadable or missing progress record (a packet revision). A terminated RA attempt never counts as API ready; the next RA follows after the 1335-second wait. The staging API may stay stopped meanwhile. After `A2_UNWIND_FAILED`, RA is admissible only when the files form a recognised row and production equals the baseline; otherwise Root rules (ruling R5 item 3; S-P3-6(c)).
- **Market start after RM or RC before A2** (ruling R4 item 11; A-P3-8, S-P3-7(b)). The start families are retired before GO-A2, and RM and RC only stop. A market start after GO-RC or GO-RM, before A2, therefore needs its own packet or Root ruling, and A2 waits for it.
- **Restart count after a start** (ruling R13 item 2, which amends ruling R12 item 14). Root has no host fact on the reset rule, and the host service manager version is unknown, so the tool must be right under a start that resets the count and under a start that keeps it. The last recorded staging API restart count is 0 (collector start receipt c1-a01, 28 October 2026), which lowers the risk for the A2 main start but does not remove it for RA or the A2 unwind. Residual: in the reset form, a start followed by exactly n0 automatic restarts before the first running snapshot is accepted; the readiness window and its checks still apply.
- **A2 terminated at the pending write** (ruling R13 item 15; tester reading 4). An A2 terminated at the pending-write `ATTEMPTED` line leaves the API stopped in row L0-n, and the next RA refuses `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED`. This fails closed. Recovery needs a Root ruling, a packet revision and its own GO.
- **Budget stop after the RA start, past the deadline** (ruling R13 item 15; tester reading 5). It sends no stop. Reaching it needs one adapter call to overrun its finite limit by about 60 seconds or more.
- **Walk cap and IMMEDIATE cap, unmeasured** (ruling R19 section 3; ruling R21 item 7). The 4-second tree walk cap and the 50-second IMMEDIATE cap are not measured on the host. A slow walk refuses before an effect at a gate and sends no start before a start. Three places fail closed on a false timeout of the walk: the RA put-back start, the RA case 5 restart and the A2 unwind start send no start and leave the API stopped, with why TREE, though the tree may be right (ruling R24 item 6; ruling R25 item 5, audit-r9 P3-7; the run ends `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED` or `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`, and the next step is the one of the outcome tables). The A2 stop-grace path (149) and the pre-write abort path (99) also end in the walked unwind start and include the walk (section 11.1). A slow IMMEDIATE pass ends in a terminal code and sends no command.
- **RA stop-command asymmetry** (ruling R25 item 2). In RA case 5, when the stop command failed and the snapshot read shows the API stopped with no job, the run ends `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` with `stopProved` false; the A2 counterpart (`A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED`) records `stopProved` true. RA fails closed and follows the list of ruling R14 item 3. A failed API read with no job in the same case ends `RA_UNWOUND` with `stopProved` true.
- **Tool comments predate G1** (ruling R26 item 7; audit-r7 P3-2; second-r7 P3-4). Some generated comments in the A2RA core still say that a failed state counts as stopped, without the job condition. The packet quotes G1, not the comments, and the behaviour follows G1. The comments are corrected in the next code round, if one comes. This is a known cosmetic item.
- **Served-URL rule tightenings** (ruling R24 item 3, reading 6). The rule is the P5a regex plus three tightenings: at most 1024 bytes, no `..` segment and no leading `//`. Reading 6 is accepted on condition that Root's dry admission passes against the real P5a served list (ruling R24 item 7); a served list that a tightening refuses blocks the admission before any GO.
- **API state after the run limit** (ruling R19 item 4(b); ruling R21 item 7). After the in-run limit or, while unwinding, the unwind limit (section 11) is passed, the one read-only class read for `apiAtEnd` can fail to run before the host deadline; `apiAtEnd` is then `UNKNOWN` with `apiAtEndWhy` `LIMIT`, in A2 and in RA. The rule that a throw after a start command that is not followed by a proved stop ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` or `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` applies only while readiness of that start is not proved; after readiness is proved the check codes stand (amendment R19a; ruling R22 item 2; ruling R23 items 3, 4 and 7).
- **A torn NOTE line** (ruling R13 item 15; second opinion P3-8). A `NOTE` line that is itself torn fails closed.
- **In-flight scan of A2M and AR** (ruling R12 items 15 and 17). The scan fails closed on a process it cannot read, so an unreadable process table refuses, though host health is never a refusal. It is also recorded as an ADP residual (ruling R12 item 15; ruling R14 item 15).
- **Late stderr after a lost probe race** (ADP residual; ruling R12 item 15; ruling R14 item 15). Recorded as a residual; this packet adds no rule for it.
- **Escape form of envMap** (ADP residual; ruling R12 item 15; ruling R14 item 15). Recorded as a residual; this packet adds no rule for it.
- **Functional proof.** The owner's browser check is the only functional proof on staging (ruling D17). The owner's port forwarding drops often; Root never reopens it.
- **Shared reload and production identity.** Each reload is host-wide and shared with the production units. The D30 normalisation held across the P5a reload; any other reload-visible production change stops before the API start (unwind) or after readiness.
- **Webhook gap.** Staging alerts and webhooks are not served during each API gap (bounds 310 seconds for A2 and 380 seconds for RA).
- **Inert trees accumulate** in the stage directory (no delete); A1 re-checks free disk.
- **Reboot.** The staging API does not start at boot; after a reboot the re-pin layer applies at the next manual start.

## 13. Owner checklist before the effect GOs

Main packet section 12 applies; items 1, 3 and 7 are hard gates that need affirmative confirmation in GO-A2 and GO-RA. GO-A1 and GO-AR confirm items 3 and 7. Items 2, 4, 6 and 8 may be confirmed or accepted as named risks as there. Item 5 (Paper rules) stands from GO-P4.

1. **Staging alerts** (hard gate): staging TradingView alerts and webhooks may be missed during each API gap, or the owner pauses them.
3. **Who can stop staging** (hard gate): the owner names who stops staging units if needed and is reachable during each job and the browser steps.
7. **Production untouched** (hard gate): no production unit, database or configuration is in scope, and the owner knows of no planned production work during the jobs.

Additional items for this packet (letters local to this packet):

- **A. Mixed-revision pair** (hard gate, A2): the owner accepts that the staging API runs the pin while the trading worker stays on `ba1a433`.
- **B. Rollback order and the RX rule** (hard gate, A2): GO-RA comes before any R5a, R4, R4H or RX; GO-RC or GO-RM may come before GO-RA, and RA gives the market units and the trading worker no command in every class; then GO-RM before R5a, R4 or R4H.
- **C. Restore source** (A2): the owner knows the full-restore source and its age, and that a full restore loses every write since it.
- **D. Browser steps** (hard gate, A2): the owner opens the per-Bot report for Week (the Bot with fills), Month and All Time after Root reports, checks that Week shows bar coverage complete and that Month and All Time return the report, with bar coverage "not checked" where the range is over a coverage budget, in English and in Thai, and that the admin health shows `liveEnabled` false (moved from the A2 immediate checks; ruling R12 item 17; Root records the reported value in the A2 acceptance record, ruling R14 item 14), with Root reading request timings and status codes view only; A2M follows.
- **E. First reload with running market units** (named risk allowed, A2 and RA): the stream and collector timer keep running through the reload, which is host-wide and shared with the production units; a market change after the reload stops the job after readiness, or with the API stopped when it does not become ready, for the owner's decision; a market change at a later A2 check leaves the API running and stops the job for the owner's decision.

Every line whose job uses the migration connection selector or reads environment sources says so in one plain clause (ruling D4). No such GO is recorded by this preparation.

the read since A2 is recorded only; counts recorded, never a stop), and the research worker as stopped; the market stream, the fallback collector service, the fallback collector timer and the trading worker are each classified, with the environment properties left out of every unit property read other than the host-side environment-source read of the staging API's own environment property (digest only), as running with any process, inactive or failed, with the class, process continuity and restart count recorded and never compared with the c6, A1, AR or A2 values (a unit in automatic restart gets a read-only settle wait of up to 30 seconds; an unknown state, a state still transitioning after that wait, or a pending job refuses); the trading worker's heartbeat rows and freshness are recorded, and the gate refuses only on an unreadable queue (also a row without a string role), a Live-mode row, a Live signal above zero, an empty set of rows of the trading worker role while the trading worker runs, more than one fresh row whose tick is at or after the trading worker's main-process start minus one second, or any other fresh row that is not a previous process's row (with a running trading worker, a row with a tick before its main-process start; with no running trading worker, a row whose tick does not advance across two reads at least twice the heartbeat interval plus 250 ms apart; such a row is recorded, not refused), never on a stale heartbeat in any class (a named relaxation of the R5a stale-heartbeat refusal, because the tool gives the trading worker no command); all of this under the two-way hooks-and-links rule of A2, and, only when the timer is active, the timer window (a read-only wait of at most 420 seconds, otherwise a stop before any effect); a collector run in progress is waited out, read only, within the same 420-second bound, otherwise a stop before any effect; the environment sources are read only inside the host-side process, and the tool returns booleans, counts and digests only and is not a binding read. This GO is admissible after GO-RC or GO-RM, and the tool gives the market units and the trading worker no command in every class. The staging API may be running, restarting (also in automatic restart, in the activating state with a start sub-state, or with its own pending start, stop or restart job, after the settle wait, for example a crash loop), stopped or failed (another state still transitioning after that wait refuses): the tool stops it if it is running or restarting and proves it stopped (if that stop is not proved within 100 seconds or the API becomes failed, the tool makes no further control call and reports; an error in the proof wait counts as not proved and sends no further command, a command timeout is a stop not proved, and any other failure of the stop command gets one snapshot read, so that the tool ends unwound with nothing started only when the snapshot shows the API running with no job and the gate's main process, or failed with no job), re-checks that no collector run and no job is pending (a read-only wait of up to 15 seconds for a run to end), and acts by the layer state it finds on disk, which must be one of the packet's recognised states, with the drop-in files on disk equal to that state (the live drop-in list and the pending and archive forms of the re-pin layer's name; every other file of the drop-in directory equal in count and digest to the A2 gate inventory, from the reviewed A2 receipt or, when that receipt was lost, from the gate line of the A2 host progress record; with no such line A2 had no effect, and the tool refuses; an archive of the layer's name that no listed A2 or prior RA record explains refuses): with the re-pin layer live, it archives the layer by no-clobber rename; with only a pending re-pin file and no listed record of its rename (a rename counts as shown only when labelled done, or labelled attempted with the file renamed on disk), it records that file's digest in its progress record and then archives the file by no-clobber rename, deleting nothing; with the layer or a pending file archived by the A2 unwind as the A2 receipt or progress record shows, with the layer archived by an earlier RA attempt, with a pending file archived by an earlier RA attempt whose digest matches the line recorded before its rename, or never written (only when no listed A2 or RA record shows a write, rename or archive line of the re-pin layer, labelled attempted or done), it changes no file; it runs one manager reload (host-wide, shared with the production units) if it archived the live re-pin layer or the staging API needs a reload, then checks that the loaded configuration shows the post-P5a drop-in list, the ba1a433 working directory and start command and the QR-2 flag effective 1 from the P5a flag layer, and that the production identity is unchanged, re-checks the market units against its own gate reading, and starts the API[; if the API is failed, clear it with reset-failed right before this start only, never before an unwind start (this optional clause sets the manifest boolean resetFailedGranted to true; without it a failed API refuses before any effect)]. Before every rename, archive, put-back and reload it writes an attempted line with the expected file form and digest into its progress record, and a done line after it; its receipt carries the pre-rename digest line of a pending file. If a step fails before the API is ready, the tool unwinds as section 8 of the packet states: when it archived the layer itself and the archive, the manager reload, the loaded-configuration check or the production-identity check fails, it puts the A2 layer back by rename, runs one more reload (so at most two manager reloads in this job, both host-wide and shared with the production units and the running market units), checks the A2 configuration and the staged tree (a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start) and starts the API on it only if the API was running when the job began; when the API start or readiness fails after it archived the layer itself and the API was running when the job began, it stops the API, puts the layer back, reloads, checks the staged tree as in the case above (a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start) and starts the API on the A2 configuration (if the API becomes failed during that stop, the put-back and its reload still run, nothing is stopped or started, and the failed API is recorded); if it stops before changing the layer, it starts the API again on the configuration it found (after a check of the tree that start uses; a mismatch or an unreadable tree sends no start), only if the API was running when the job began, and, when that is the post-P5a configuration with no live or pending re-pin file and no pending reload, runs the immediate checks and ends `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` or `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, never `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY`. In these named cases it leaves the API stopped and Root reports: when the put-back reload, the A2 configuration check after it, or the start after the put-back or the restart on the configuration it found fails; in every other case of a failed start or readiness (with the re-pin layer archived or never written); when the re-pin layer was already archived or never written and the reconciling reload (also a time-budget stop or a failed progress write at it), the loaded-configuration check or the production-identity check fails, or its archive of a pending file is on disk and that step then fails; when it cannot put an archived layer back; when a stop is not proved or the API becomes failed; and on a later time-budget stop or failed progress write other than at that reconciling reload (after its own archive of the live layer it first puts the layer back and reloads, with no start). After its start command and before readiness is proved, a step-budget or in-run-limit stop first gets one proved stop, the put-back in that row runs only after a proved stop, no start follows, and a stop not proved ends the job as a stop not proved (ruling R12 item 6; ruling R13 item 9). No end code claims that the API stopped without a proved stop: while readiness of that start is not proved (the readiness poll and any check before readiness of that start is proved), a throw after any start command that is not followed by a proved stop ends the job as a stop not proved; after readiness is proved the existing check codes stand. After the in-run limit or, while unwinding, the unwind limit, one read-only class read records the API state, or unknown (limit). If the production identity differs at any step after the gate, the tool starts nothing from that point (after its own archive of the live layer it still puts the layer back and reloads). If the market re-check after the file action (and the reload, if one ran) finds a market unit changed from its own gate reading, the tool does not unwind and runs no extra reload: it gives the market units no command, starts the API on ba1a433, runs the immediate checks and stops, and I decide on GO-RC or GO-RM; if the API start or readiness then fails, it stops the API if it runs and proves the stop, starts nothing (it sends no stop to a failed API) and leaves the API stopped on the post-P5a configuration, and I decide. If an immediate check fails after the API is ready, the tool gives no command, runs no unwind and no reload, leaves the API running on ba1a433 and stops for review by Root. The same holds for a throw, a step-budget stop or an in-run-limit stop at an immediate check, and for a market change seen with a failed immediate check (the end stays the check stop, with the market change recorded); after the API is ready, or after a market change, the tool sends no command at all other than the start, readiness poll and stop of the market branch above (in RA also the reset-failed of the optional clause). A graceful termination never sends a command in any phase (no stop, no put-back, no start); before the first effect (the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload) it ends PRE_TERMINATED with no effect, and after the first effect it ends EFF_TERMINATED, with the end row from the disk and the API state as read; the RA family treats EFF_TERMINATED as a termination with no end code and never as the API ready, and the result says terminated. RA outcomes: `RA_API_READY`, `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` and `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` end with the API ready on `ba1a433`; `RA_UNWOUND`, `RA_UNWIND_RELOAD_FAILED`, `RA_UNWIND_START_FAILED`, `RA_API_NOT_READY`, `RA_API_STOPPED`, `RA_BUDGET_STOP`, `RA_STOP_NOT_PROVED` and `RA_STOP_MARKET_API_NOT_READY` end in a recognised layer state without the API proved ready on `ba1a433` by RA's immediate checks; `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` and `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` are excluded. After an RA attempt that did not end with the API ready on `ba1a433`, also after a termination, which never counts as an end with the API ready and is followed by the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds, Root may request the next RA attempt under a new GO-RA that lists the prior attempts; the tool admits it only when the files on disk form a recognised layer state, the prior records show the facts that state needs, and production equals the baseline. Excluded: after `RA_UNWIND_FAILED` (an archived layer could not be put back) Root rules by a packet revision before any next RA; after `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` (a production-identity difference read at any RA step, at the gate before any effect, the RD-32 state) no next RA is admitted without Root's recorded review of the difference in its manifest, which also sets the production baseline that RA compares against. An RA that ends with the API ready on `ba1a433`, other than with `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE`, is terminal for the RA family, and no next RA follows it; its receipt is the accepted RA receipt, for the RX tripwire and the return of R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC, when its end row is L0-n, LU-n, LR-n or LQ-n, the post-P5a configuration is loaded, production is unchanged and the API is ready, after Root accepts it, with a Root review first for a market or check stop; a `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` receipt with the API ready on `ba1a433` in such an end row may be accepted by Root in the same way after its review of the difference. A Root-reviewed A2 receipt with end row L0-n or LU-n, no pending file, the API ready on `ba1a433`, the post-P5a configuration loaded and production unchanged reopens that chain in the same way, with no RA. I know that a running API can be left stopped, and that Root then reports and I decide. Immediate checks against the ba1a433 served manifest (health 200 in Paper mode, or a 503 with Paper-only mode only when the trading worker shows the same not-running class (inactive or failed) at the gate read and at the immediate read); no other fresh heartbeat row (under the tick-time rule above) and no Live-mode row in every class; the trading worker's own heartbeat (exactly one own row) is checked only when it was fresh at the gate and the trading worker is still the same process, and recorded otherwise. The market stream and the fallback collector service and timer get no command. QR-2 audit rows from panel use stay. The tool writes one record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory, and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool. I know that staging alerts and webhooks are not served during the API gap of up to 380 seconds, expected about 4 to 13 seconds, and that the stream and collector timer may keep running through the reload. I confirm checklist items 1, 3 and 7, and I accept items [E and list] as named risks. No retry. notAfter [UTC].

GO-AR (only after a process-identity drift at an A1 or A2 gate or at the A1 end reading, or once right before GO-A2):

> GO S6-STAGING-API-REPIN-1 AR attempt [a01 | the next attempt, listing the earlier attempts] for Staging QL-P2 Prototype only, a read-only reference reading [after the process-identity stop of [A1 | A2] job [job reference] | after the difference at the end reading of A1 job [job reference] | without a drift, as the reference of A2, right before or together with GO-A2], for API pin [pin] (includes the QR-2 422 range refusal and panel hint: yes; includes the QR-2 bar-coverage degrade D: yes); the staging API and the trading worker stay on revision ba1a433 (includes QR-2 O7: yes), and this step does not stop, restart or change any unit: in one SSH session with the reviewed tool, read only, record the host health, that no other S6 job is in flight and no systemd job is pending, the four production units against the production baseline [r6 a02 reference, digest b5789332, as re-read at c6], and the class of the staging API, trading worker, research worker, market stream, fallback collector service and fallback collector timer, with the main-process digest and restart count of the staging API, the trading worker and the market stream, the configuration and drop-in list digests of the staging API, the trading worker and the three market units, the timer's trigger settings, last trigger and next elapse, and the collector service's result, with the environment properties left out of every unit property read; a unit in automatic restart gets a read-only settle wait of up to 30 seconds, and the tool refuses only on a failed read, another S6 job in flight (the in-flight scan counts a module-input process of another Node binary as a payload and fails closed on a process it cannot read), a pending job or a unit still transitioning after that wait (host health is recorded and never refused). It returns states, counts, digests and booleans only. No start, stop, restart, reset-failed, daemon reload, drop-in, environment read, database connection or delete; no file is read beyond unit properties and process start times; every unit gets no command. Root uses the record as the process reference of [the next A1 attempt | the next A2 attempt | A2 with the tree staged by A1 job [job reference] | A2 under the GO-A2 sent with this GO] only after Root accepts it, and I know that the job that uses it runs only under its own GO. The tool writes one record directory with a progress record and a result file in the dedicated protected backup directory, and the shell's temporary here-document file that carries the tool. I confirm checklist items 3 and 7. No retry. notAfter [UTC].

- Fix rounds re-verify only the changed files with one rerun of the suite. Mutation runs and test-running reviewers are serialized. No reviewer grants acceptance; Root alone accepts each tool before admission and each result afterwards.

## 16. Explicit exclusions

This packet excludes:
- any change to a QR-2 budget value (option A waits for a later pin);
- any D code beyond the reviewed D commit that the pin carries;
- any change to the trading worker: no tree, layer, stop, start or restart;
- any start, stop, restart or reset-failed of the market stream, the fallback collector service, the fallback collector timer or the research worker;
- any daemon reload other than the one reload of A2 and of RA and, in an unwind, at most one more; any control command or reload by A1, A2M or AR;
- a database dump, a counter baseline, a session wait, a migration or any database write by the tools;
- any binding read or binding update; any unit-file, boot-setting, environment-file or linger change;
- any change to the P1, P4, P5a, P4M, collector or diagnosis tools, or to the main, follow-up or collector packets;
- an online backup refresh, a restore, or any delete of a tree, layer, pending file, backup or database (RA archives a pending file by rename);
- any binding read on the host (the trading-worker environment and stream configuration references are copied into the private manifests at build time);
- Pine Bridge activation, research admission, Preflight, profile enrollment, set B, P5b;
- Live trading, a guard reset, Best Inputs apply, the optimizer, P13j or N1 steps;
- any production change;
- any log or journal content beyond counts of fixed event kinds and refusal codes;
- any retry under the same GO.

Spot and Paper only; BINANCE:BTCUSDT Spot 1m. Private acceptance NOT RUN; READY false; P13j and N1 held.

- [P4 x4 a02 result](S6_STAGING_PROTOTYPE_DEPLOY_P4_X4_A02_RESULT_2026-10-26.md) (pre-switch backup of 2026-10-26T04:49:30Z; the trading worker was already stopped at the gate, so no accepted S6 job has stopped a running trading worker; its receipt holds the trading-worker environment digest reference that the P5a a01 gate compared).

Each job leaves one ignored local receipt bound to its manifest, marker, nonce and tool hashes. Root writes one tracked result record per job under the privacy rules (no address, host path, unit name, user name, role name, environment value or secret). An independent reviewer verifies receipt linkage and verdict before Root accepts the record. After any stop, Root reports to the owner and starts nothing else.

**Before any tool pins this packet (Root):**
1. The final packet audit r10 of draft 11 by the packet auditor, with every end code, receipt field and sum checked against the fix round 6 core (snapshot r6), the A1 snapshot r9 and the lane snapshot r5, the postFixChecks of audit r9, and D30 and D31 (ruling R26 items 5, 6 and 8; each item applied; the fixed sentences S0 to S4 still agree word for word in section 8, section 9, the owner summary and GO-RA; no other text changed); then, after A2RA fix round 3 and its follow-up are accepted (the follow-up is fix rounds 5 and 6 (rulings R24 and R25), accepted by ruling R26; accepted seals: A2RA SHA256SUMS c1ed1a24 at snapshot r6 and ADP lane b4168240 at snapshot r5), A1 fix round 2 is accepted (ruling R20; accepted seal: A1 914e643d at snapshot r9), the latest text re-check has passed and Root has taken the final changed-lines look, Root installs revision 7 under `docs/`, takes `PACKET_SHA` from the LF Git blob and sets it in the A1, A2RA and ADP tools (ruling R7 item 1; ruling R10 item 4). The ADP lane is reviewed inside the A2RA fix rounds, so no separate ADP review is an install condition, and an accepted ADP lane must exist at install (ruling R17 item 2). Section 11.1 takes the follow-up coder's sums (fix round 4) in draft 9 and the two walked A2 paths 149 and 99 (fix round 6, G5) in draft 10, before Root installs revision 7 (ruling R21 item 8; ruling R24 item 3; ruling R25 item 7); the packet auditor checks them against the fix round 6 core.
2. Root re-hashes the LF Git blobs of the main, follow-up and collector packets and records them, with this packet's `PACKET_SHA`, in the tool pins (R-2).
3. Revision 6 author readings (a) to (g), confirmed by ruling R7 item 2 and kept here as history:
   - (a) The A2 end codes `A2_PASSED`, `A2_STOP_AT_CHECK`, `A2_UNWOUND`, `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` and `A2_UNWIND_PROGRESS_FAILED` are names chosen by the author; ruling R6 items 3 and 9 name the ends, not the codes. `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` also covers a failed unwind reload, loaded-configuration check or unwind start, which revision 5 did not name and which the P5a unwind reports as its unwind failure; in this packet `A2_UNWIND_FAILED` stays the RD-32 form only (section 6.6). Ruling R7 item 2 records this as a named adaptation away from the P5a code names.
   - (b) `A2_UNWIND_PROGRESS_FAILED` starts nothing, because a progress record that cannot be written is not a state in which to start; the next step is RA in row LP-n.
   - (c) Under the one production rule (ruling R6 item 4), the case 1 put-back and its reload still run in row LL after RA's own archive (the R5a unwind by value), and only the start is withheld; the ruling forbids a start, not the put-back.
   - (d) Case 5 in rows LP-n, LU-r and LR-r keeps `RA_UNWOUND`, because ruling R6 item 2 names only rows L0-n, LU-n, LR-n and LQ-n: with a pending file or a reload still pending, the configuration found is not shown to be the post-P5a configuration with no re-pin file.
   - (e) An API that becomes failed at the first stop, before any file action, is case 5 under the code-precedence rule (`RA_UNWOUND`, the row RA found, nothing started).
   - (f) "Stuck transitioning past the settle wait" (ruling R6 item 5) is read as a staging API in a transitioning state other than automatic restart or start (for example still stopping), because ruling R6 item 11 exempts automatic restart and start.
   - (g) Section 13 item E keeps "a market change after the reload": it describes the reload risk, and ruling R6 item 6 names only GO-RA and the owner summary.

Revision 7 author readings (ruled by Root ruling R10: (a) by item 2, (b) by item 3(a), (c) and (d) by item 3(g), (e) by item 3(i); kept as history with the ruled wording):
   - (a) The guard of ruling R9 item 2 and the market branch. The branch of section 6.4 starts the API, polls readiness and, after a failed start or readiness, stops it; ruling R9 item 2 says that in MARKET the tool sends no control command at all. Ruling R10 item 2 confirms reading (a) in this form: the branch's one start, its readiness poll and, after a failed start, one proved stop (in RA also the reset-failed under ruling R9 item 10g), granted when the change is seen; nothing else in MARKET; nothing at all in READY (ruling R9a; ruling R12 item 3). Section 6.6 states the allowance, and the same holds for RA case 8.
   - (b) "Budget stop" in ruling R9 items 2 and 10i is read as the step-budget stop and the in-run-limit stop of section 11.1; the packet says "step-budget or in-run-limit stop" in every place and the tests inject both (ruling R10 item 3(a)).
   - (c) After a market change, a failed immediate check ends `A2_STOP_MARKET_AT_CHECK` in A2 (section 6.4; a throw or a budget stop at a check ends the same, ruling R9 item 2) and `RA_STOP_AT_CHECK` with `marketChanged` true in RA (ruling R9 item 10f), as the two rulings state; the packet keeps the two codes as ruled (ruling R10 item 3(j)).
   - (d) The archive suffix forms are accepted by ruling R9 item 11d; this packet names no file form (ruling R10 item 3(g)).
   - (e) The payload ceiling of 262144 bytes (ruling R9 item 11a) is read as the size of the tool payload that the shell's temporary here-document file carries, per mode; the build asserts it with a worst-case manifest, and Root runs the payload-size check with the real manifest before each GO request (ruling R10 item 3(i)).

Draft 3 open items D9 to D12 (ruled by Root ruling R15; each is one line, kept as history with its ruling):
   - (D9) Ruling R14 item 4 names `PRE_TERMINATED` but not its next step. Ruled (R15 item 2): sentence S1 governs every termination, `PRE_TERMINATED` included: it never counts as an end with the API ready, the next RA follows after the lost-receipt wait of 1335 seconds from that attempt's start, and the RA family treats it as a termination with no end code (the RA row says this, and "open" is removed). In A2 it is handled like a gate refusal: no effect happened, Root reviews, and a next A2 attempt uses a contingency job ID only when GO-A2 names one, otherwise it needs a new GO (section 6.6 table).
   - (D10) Ruling R14 item 4 says "before the first effect" and "after the first effect". Ruled (R15 item 3): the first effect is the first host-changing step of the mode: a control command, a rename or archive, a pending-file write or a manager reload. The packet uses this definition wherever it splits `PRE_TERMINATED` from `EFF_TERMINATED` (sections 6.6 and 8, GO-A2, GO-RA and section 15). Build: next round (R15 item 8).
   - (D11) For the `A2_UNWOUND` end after a failed first stop command, the API is running on `ba1a433` as found and its readiness is not re-proved. Ruled (R15 item 4): this end never reopens the chain under S4, because readiness is not re-proved. The next step is Root review; a next A2 needs a Root ruling or a packet revision (section 9); RA in row L0-n stays available. The `A2_UNWOUND` row says so for this cause.
   - (D12) Ruling R14 item 19 sets the install conditions. Ruled (R15 item 5): items 24 and 30 of the revision 7 list, and sections 15, 17 and 18, take them in the words of ruling R14 item 19: fix round 3 and its follow-up accepted; the latest text re-check passed; Root's final changed-lines look. The draft 3 re-check has run, so the next re-check is the draft 4 re-check.

Draft 7 open items D17 to D22 (raised by the draft 7 writer; ruled by Root ruling R22; each is one line, kept as history with its ruling):
   - (D17) Ruling R19 item 2.5 and ruling R21 item 5(c) name `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` and say the packet defines both, but do not define them. Section 6.6 reads `stopProved` as the proof of an unwind or phase START stop and `marketStopProved` as the proof of the market branch's one stop. Section 6.4 and the A2 table row for `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` still say the market-branch end with the stop not proved carries `stopProved` false (ruling R13 item 6). Root says whether those two places read `marketStopProved` false. Ruled (R22 item 3): `stopProved` is whether the run's last API stop command was proved (true, false, or null when no stop was sent); `marketStopProved` is the same fact for the stop sent in the A2 market branch and null outside it; in the market branch both are equal; applied in sections 6.4, 6.6, 6.10 and the A2 row.

The draft replaces it at the RA gate, in the tests and also at the A2 reload re-check (section 6.4) and the RA market re-check (case 8), which are after the first effect, where a PRE_ code reads as pre-effect. Root says whether those two places keep the replacement or another code. Ruled (R22 item 4): `PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT` replaces `STREAM_UNREADABLE` only at the RA gate, before any effect; the A2 reload re-check and the RA market re-check return to the draft 6 wording `STREAM_UNREADABLE`; the final pass checks each code against the built core.

When no layer was written and no reload was attempted, nothing is archived; the draft states no end row for that case beyond "the row of an archived and reloaded layer". Root says which row and whether a reload runs. Ruled (R22 item 5): the end row is L0-n; nothing is renamed, no reload runs, and no stop or start is sent; the run ends `A2_UNWIND_API_STOPPED` with the failed API recorded; the next step is Root review, and RA in row L0-n, with the reset-failed grant, can recover.

The draft states neither; section 11.1 is unchanged. Root says whether the count guard belongs in the text. Ruled (R22 item 6): the restart-count rule adds "a count that is not an integer is not ready"; sections 6.6 and 8 say each new walk is counted in its own path sum (section 11.1, final pass).
   - (D21) The section 17 Next build list names the build items of ruling R17 item 4 only. Ruling R19 item 4 adds more build items (tree checks before two starts, the budget stop after a start command, `startSent` and `startRefused`, the failed-API unwind, the 503 class rule, the real-path rule, the served-URL rule). Sections 6.6, 8 and 15 state them; Root says whether Next lists them. Ruled (R22 item 7): Next names the follow-up build list as ruling R19 item 4 (a) to (n), with amendment R19a.

Root says whether any of them is rewritten. Draft 9 open items D26 to D29 (raised by the draft 9 writer; ruled by Root ruling R25; each is one line, kept as history with its ruling):
   - (D26) Ruling R23 item 2 makes `stopProved` null when the run sent no API stop command, and true only when no start followed the last proved stop. Ruling R24 item 4, F5 says the A2 failed-API unwind road sets `stopProved` true with `apiFailed` true, though a start command was sent after the first proved stop and that road sends no further stop. Section 6.6 and the A2 table row state both rules, with F5 as the rule for that road. Root says whether the reading is right; the packet auditor checks the road against the built core (ruling R23 item 2). Ruled (R25 item 2): the built rule is accepted and replaces the wording of R23 item 2: `stopProved` is absent when no stop was sent, true when a stop read after the last stop command showed the API stopped or failed and no start was attempted after that read, and false otherwise; `apiAtEnd` is never proof; not recorded on pass ends; `marketStopProved` only in the A2 market branch; the RA stop-command asymmetry is kept (item 92).
   - (D27) The fix round 4 report names a receipt field `startRefusedFor`, while rulings R22, R23 and R24 (item 4, F4) and draft 9 of this packet named it `startRefusedAt`. Draft 9 kept `startRefusedAt`, and Root was to say whether the packet follows the code. Ruled (R25 item 5): the built field is `startRefusedFor`; the packet uses `startRefusedFor` in every place (this pass); the code is not renamed. [History; superseded by ruling R26 item 2: ruling R25 item 5 read snapshot r4. Fix round 5 renamed the field to `startRefusedAt`, and the fix round 6 core writes `startRefusedAt` at all 4 sites and `startRefusedFor` nowhere. The packet uses `startRefusedAt` in every current place since draft 11 (item 97); history notes may say that fix round 4 named it `startRefusedFor`.]
   - (D28) Ruling R24 item 4, F2 names "the PRE code of the trigger" for an A2 budget stop, clock refusal or throw with no effect yet, but the packet names no such code (only `PRE_TERMINATED`, `PRE_UNIT_SNAPSHOT`, `PRE_DROPIN_SHAPE`, `PRE_INVENTORY_TOO_LARGE`, `PRE_RESET_FAILED_NOT_GRANTED`, `PRE_HEARTBEAT_STALE` and `PRE_ROW_UNRECOGNISED`). Section 6.6 and the first A2 table row say "the PRE code of that trigger". Root says whether the packet lists the built codes. Ruled (R25 item 5): with the effect flag false the A2 route ends `PRE_TIME_BUDGET` or `PRE_TERMINATED`, the only reachable triggers; the packet names both (item 94).
   - (D29) The status lines of draft 9 say "the follow-up is fix round 5 (ruling R24), accepted by ruling R25" in the title, items 24 and 30, section 15, section 17 Next and section 18 item 1, as ruling R24 item 6 words them. Ruling R25 does not exist yet; Root confirms the lines or edits them before install. The effect-flag paragraph of section 6.6 (reading 1) is written for A2 and RA together, because the runner is shared; Root confirms. Ruled (R25 items 5 and 6; audit-r9 P3-8): the status lines read "fix rounds 5 and 6 (rulings R24 and R25), accepted by ruling R26" in items 24 and 30, section 15, section 17 Next (twice) and section 18 item 1; the title holds only the contract sentence; the shared effect-flag paragraph stands, with its PRE_ and EFF_ claim scoped to the snapshot, budget and termination codes (item 94).

Draft 10 open items D30 and D31 (raised by the draft 10 writer; ruled by Root ruling R26 items 5 and 6, confirmed by the final packet audit r10 against the fix round 6 core (ruling R27 item 3); each is one line, kept with its ruling):
   - (D30) Ruling R25 item 7, G1 requires an empty job queue before a failed API counts as stopped, and says the stop poll continues and the end is not proved. The text states exactly that in sections 6.4, 6.6 and 8 and names no further command for a failed API with a queued start job at the A2 unwind or at the RA put-back, because the ruling names none. Root says whether the text should state what the unwind sends in that state, or the packet auditor reads it from the fix round 6 core. Ruled (R26 item 5; confirmed by audit r10, ruling R27 item 3): a failed API with a queued job is not stopped; the stop poll continues within its bound, sends no further command, and the end is not proved; under G1 `apiAtEnd` can read `RUNNING` after a failed unit with a queued job; the packet auditor confirms the end codes from the fix round 6 core and records them (item 99; sections 6.6, 8 and 18).
   - (D31) The text says that `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` carry the same value when the A2 market branch sent its stop (that stop is the run's last stop and no start follows it), and that with no branch stop `stopProved` follows its own rule, which is true after a failed class read with an empty job queue when the run sent an earlier stop. Ruling R25 item 2 does not state either sentence; both follow from the F5 table of the fix round 5 report. Root confirms them, or the packet auditor checks them against the fix round 6 core. Ruled (R26 item 6; confirmed by audit r10, ruling R27 item 3): both sentences stand, and the packet auditor confirms both against the fix round 6 core: when the market branch sent its stop, `stopProved` and `marketStopProved` carry the same value; with no branch stop, a failed class read with an empty job queue after an earlier stop gives `stopProved` true. If the core differs, the text follows the core when the core is the safer reading, and the auditor reports it.

Revision 5 author readings (history; confirmed by ruling R6 item 1):
   - (a) The end-code names of section 8 (`RA_API_READY` follows the R5a `API_READY`; `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` names the RD-32 state because ruling R5 item 2 defines it as any RA end with a production-identity difference, not only an unwind end).
   - (b) The A2 receipt that reopens the chain also needs the API ready on `ba1a433`, the post-P5a configuration loaded and production unchanged (the fix of the second opinion, which ruling R5 adopts), beside end row L0-n or LU-n and no pending file.
   - (c) After `RA_PRODUCTION_DIFFERENCE` the next RA manifest records Root's review of the difference, and the tool checks that record beside the production identity at its gate.
   - (d) The A2 gate inventory line is written after the record directory is created (section 6.4 step 1) and before the API stop; "before its first effect" in ruling R5 item 1 is read as before the first effect on a unit.
   - (e) The tool refuses a next RA when a prior attempt's record shows an outcome with the API ready on `ba1a433` ("no next RA from that state").

Revision 4 author readings and additions (history; closed by ruling R5 as stated above, and item (e) by ruling R6 item 1):
   - (a) The A2 gate records the count and the SHA-256 of every other file of the staging API drop-in directory (section 6.2), because ruling R4 item 4 compares those files with "the A2 gate record"; this is an environment-source read under ruling D4 and is named in GO-A2.
   - (b) Under the lost-receipt clause the compared file set uses the P5a a01 record (ruling R4 item 4). Root confirms from the accepted P5a a01 receipt that it holds the count and the digests of the other drop-in directory files; if it does not, the clause needs another source (for example the A2 host progress record of the gate).
   - (c) Rows L0-r, LP-r and LQ-r always refuse, because no job of this packet deletes a file, so no progress record can show the need-reload fact beside their files (section 8). Rows LR and LQ are told apart by the prior attempt's record of its own row, because a complete pending file has the layer digest.
   - (d) The 1335-second wait before a next RA attempt after a lost RA receipt counts from that attempt's start (section 8, GO-RA).
   - (e) AR acceptance compares the staging API's c6-form configuration digest with "the post-P5a record" (kept from revision 3). The collector start c1 export also holds `pairConfigDigests` and `pairDropInDigests` for the staging API. Root confirms that the P5a a01 digest has the c6 form, or rules that the staging API's reference is the c1 value, as for the trading worker.
   - (f) An RA attempt that ends `RA_STOP_MARKET_AFTER_READY` has the API ready on `ba1a433`. The widened rule would admit a next RA there, though none is needed. Root states whether such a receipt counts as the accepted RA receipt for the RX tripwire and for the return of R5a, R4, R4H, RM and RC.
